# Cicada — active backlog

**Code-backed priority review: 2026-10-06 (`dev` `3efba432`).** Every original G address is retained. Completed scopes moved to [DONE.md](DONE.md); aliases and settled choices remain below. There was no G174 row in the source backlog; that gap is preserved. G187 adds Windows feasibility; G188 adds ingestion modularity. G189 adds the Home revamp (painted banner off for now); G190 asks what exports carry beyond chats (2026-10-06). Provider/source/benchmark/release follow-ups were checked at `dev` `ba7a4957`.

[TODO.md](TODO.md) is the execution order and binding rulings. This file holds reasoning and remaining scope. [BACKLOG_CONTEXT.md](BACKLOG_CONTEXT.md) holds older cross-cutting context and original owner prompts; [working-method.md](working-method.md) defines the implementation bar. Dated progress inside a row is history: its **Remaining scope / priority review** and the TODO execution view take precedence over stale “not merged” or “next” text.

Priority means sequence, not a mandate to finish every row before a small trial. **P0:** actual write/commit/revision correctness and the reported regression. **P1:** useful-memory trial, automatic project continuity across sessions/agents, source refresh, video through Sleep, selected iMessage capture, replacement whole-system benchmarks, lifecycle/provenance and targeted quality work. **Today, 2026-10-06:** working GitHub releases and release/main/website synchronization (G182), with applicable integrity and integration checks. **P2:** CLI, headless packaging, provider/source modularity, reusable start prompts and portable procedures. **P3:** design/mascot and remaining app/Reader improvements. **P4:** replication and sharing after the primary-writer topology. Research follows its trigger. G148 is planned P1 work; full harness completion and new embeddings do not gate a small hands-on trial.

Every row now has an **Implementation review** and a current status; older progress is retained as history. [The per-ID implementation ledger](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md) distinguishes DONE, PARTIAL, UNBUILT, VERIFY, UNVERIFIED, DECISION, CONDITIONAL and REDIRECT, with code evidence and acceptance bounds. G1/G23/G50 were restored from DONE because their original scopes have unfinished slices. A principle or declined choice is not an automatic build task. The paid-run and binding ruling boundaries are unchanged.

The Cognition comparison is saved in [2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md), with implementation work on G180, G186, G55/G112, G76, G16, G132/G181/G92 and G148. Cicada keeps its capture, temporal validity, human verdict and provenance rails while borrowing a simpler portable format. The owner’s follow-up on unfinished source refresh and video consolidation is saved in [source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md](../research/2026-10-06-source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md) and applied to G61/G22/G162/G166.

## Current decisions

- Markdown and git remain authoritative; sqlite-vec/FTS are derived and disposable. No storage migration is scheduled.
- Keep the closed entity types, promotion gate and structured claims. No broad peer ontology rewrite is scheduled.
- Both grounded Ask and direct file traversal are implemented.
- Named banks are implemented. **Owner 2026-10-06 amendment:** selective node/project sharing and multiple contributors are requested design under G16; they are no longer indefinitely parked. A general human/agent/robot peer network remains research-only.
- One primary git writer per bank remains binding for server mode, sync and sharing. A private push-only backup is separate from replication or collaboration.
- The first memory trial is user-started; scheduled runs retain TODO ruling 4. No consolidation, upload or publication was performed by this documentation review.

## North star (moved here from G79, 2026-09-01)

**Cicada is worth having only if it learns from lived experience AND curation, and gives it
back at the right moment** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "the best version of cicada is one that
smartly gives and learns from all my experience as a human + the things that i've curated and
might be relevant to me"). Not a feature — the sentence every other row is scored against,
recorded here so it can arbitrate scope. **Read literally it names two intake streams and one
output discipline.** *Experience* = what actually happened to the user: conversations (MCP
capture, imports), decisions and their reversals, work done in repos, places, people, the
passage of time itself (the decay layer is the only part of Cicada that models experience as
something that *fades*). *Curation* = what the user deliberately chose to keep: saves with
reasons (G71), bookmarks, papers, design references (G70), prompts (G73), skills (G72) —
signal the user paid attention to select, which is categorically different from conversational
exhaust and should probably carry different trust and different decay (already true
structurally: media/bookmarks are `evergreen`, G66). *Smartly gives* = the output discipline,
and the part Cicada is furthest from: **relevance at the right moment, unprompted, without
noise.** Today Cicada answers when asked (`/ask`, `cicada_recall`) and interrupts on a schedule
(inbox); it does not yet notice "you are about to do X and three months ago you curated the
exact thing that helps." **The measurable claim hiding in the sentence** (worth stating as a
thesis-grade goal): the system should surface a *curated* item inside an *experiential* context
the user never explicitly linked — i.e. cross-stream retrieval, not within-stream search. That
is a testable capability (does a Pinterest save from March ever legitimately reach a September
conversation about a project?) and today the honest answer is "only if the user searches for
it." **Use as a filter:** a row that only adds intake (more capture channels) advances half the
sentence; a row that only adds surfaces advances none of it; rows that connect curated
artifacts into live context (G70's design identity, G60's timed questions, G75's handshake
priming, G53's state dictionary, G52's Ask) advance the whole of it — score accordingly.
**Tension to keep honest:** "gives smartly" and "never intrusive" (UX principle 4) pull against
each other; the resolution so far has been pull-not-push, and if that stays fixed, the ceiling
on "smartly gives" is set by whatever the agent loads at conversation start — which makes
**G75** (handshake) and **G53** (state dictionary) load-bearing for the north star, not
niceties. → the scoring lens over the whole table; most directly served by **G53**, **G75**,
**G52**, **G70**, **G66**, **G71**; the reason **G74** is the top blocker (nothing learns while
Sleep is dead).

---

**Current application of that lens:** the plan engine exists; the immediate test is whether the record improves ordinary conversations (G10), followed by cross-stream relevance (G93), skills (G112) and design memory (G70/G151). The historical engine-blocker sentence above is not the current queue.

[Release and integration acceptance](../specs/2026-10-06-release-and-integration-acceptance.md). **Earlier delivery priority/manual-only sketches below are superseded by this owner request; other safety/release rails remain binding.** **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Bundled backend, signed update verification, installer and release workflow exist; no published release was listed in the dated Oct-6 check. First artifact, fresh download/update acceptance, signing/notarization and website link remain. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g182). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Release bundling, versioning, signed-manifest update plumbing and CI are merged. A read-only GitHub release listing on 2026-10-06 returned no releases. Finish clean downloaded-artifact install, first-run/capture/recall, update verification and website link; publish only through the established manual main-promotion/release process. Record actual Apple Silicon/Intel support and Gatekeeper/signing behavior from the artifact rather than promising it. Developer ID/notarization and the desired drag-to-install distribution stay explicit open scope. This backlog edit does not publish/tag/promote anything. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Today** `main` is promoted by hand (a merge of `dev`), with no tag, no version and no build (the repo has no git tags and no GitHub releases, checked 2026-10-05); this Mac updates from `dev` through `scripts/dev/auto-update.sh`; a new person installs from source (`install.sh`, `install.md`). The website's macOS install should always hand out what `main` is. **Shape — a promotion to `main` is a release:** a version tag (`v0.x.y`) → CI builds the release app → signed with a Developer ID and notarized (needs the Apple Developer account) → a `.dmg` on a GitHub Release with notes from the merged PRs → the website's download button points at the latest release's asset, so a release needs no website edit → installed apps update themselves (a Sparkle appcast, or a check against the latest release) → the backend and its Python runtime ship inside the app (today's packaging blocker: no embedded Python) or the first run sets them up. **Two channels:** `dev` stays the developer channel (the auto-updater); releases from `main` are what people get. **Each release:** suites green on `main`, release notes, bank migration notes, the version in the app's `Info.plist` and the API, a smoke test of the `.dmg` on a clean macOS user. **To decide:** the version scheme; Sparkle or our own updater; whether the website lives in this repo; where signing runs (CI secrets vs this Mac); a Homebrew cask for the app beside G181's formula. Relates **G76**, **G88**, **G92**, **G181**. **Owner 2026-10-05: no App Store — people download a `.dmg` from the website and drag it into Applications like any other app.** That is exactly what a Developer ID is for: it is Apple's signing for apps distributed *outside* the App Store, and it is the only thing that removes Gatekeeper's block. Without it a browser-downloaded app (quarantined) opens on macOS 15/26 only through System Settings → Privacy & Security → Open Anyway (Control-click → Open no longer bypasses it since macOS 15), or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` in Terminal; a signature that doesn't match its bundle reads as "damaged". The Apple Developer Program is 99 USD/yr and an individual can enrol (the legal name shows as developer; only the Account Holder creates the Developer ID Application certificate). A Homebrew cask is no way around it: current Homebrew quarantines every cask download and has dropped `--no-quarantine`. The `curl … \| bash` install on the website works only because curl never sets the quarantine attribute — it stays as the from-source path. **Research 2026-10-05 (codebase + Apple + CI + Python packaging, then two adversarial fact-checks) — why the app can't simply be zipped today:** (1) `install_app.sh:105-108` signs ad hoc or with a self-signed local certificate using `--deep`, `--timestamp=none` and no `--options runtime` — notarization rejects all three. (2) The `.app` holds no backend: `bundle.sh` copies the Swift binary, icon and resource bundle and stamps the build machine's checkout path as `CicadaRepoRoot` (`bundle.sh:126`); `BackendProcess.installRoot()` (`BackendProcess.swift:164-176`) falls back to `~/cicada`; the launchd plist, MCP registration and Claude Code/Codex hooks all run `<checkout>/api/.venv/bin/python`, whose interpreter is a symlink into uv's cache. `resolveMemoryPath()` derives the bank from `installRoot()/memory`, so a bundled app would point the bank inside the `.app` — the runtime seam must cut memory-root resolution away from the install root (split-brain class). (3) Size: `sentence-transformers` is a base dependency in `api/pyproject.toml` (it also sits in the `local` extra), so torch comes with every install and the venv measures **906 MB**; the default local embedder is a gated Hugging Face model needing a token. Search already degrades to words when the embedder can't import; the index-build path (`vector_index.rebuild_pending_index` via `entity_resolver.py:385`, `link_recon.py:394`) needs the same, with a reason the person can see. `install.sh:279` still hints `uv sync --extra local` although torch is already in the base install. (4) The backend runs bare `git` for every bank commit (`git_service.py`, `bank_registry.py`), which on a Mac without the Command Line Tools pops Apple's install dialog. (5) No `.github/`, no tags, no releases; the version is hardcoded (0.2 in `bundle.sh:89-90`, 0.1.0 in pyproject and `main.py`). (The 2026-10-05 code audit found the same split at `3d28cd0c` — `bundle.sh:89` vs `api/pyproject.toml:3` — and left it to this row; #182's one `VERSION` file closed it.) (6) `api/.env` has three readers (BackendProcess's overlay, `install-backend-agent.sh`'s memory-path read, `BackendAgentService.swift:119`) and pydantic loads it only when the working directory is `api/`, so today's launchd backend already ignores it apart from the memory path — a latent bug of its own. **Proposed shape (owner asked to record it, not to plan or build yet):** a `.dmg` built in CI on each promotion to `main` that carries its own CPython 3.12 (python-build-standalone, arm64 first) and the locked core dependencies, no venv or shebangs, about 110 MB *estimated* without torch; Mach-O code placed where Apple expects code (`Contents/Frameworks` or `Contents/Helpers`, not `Contents/Resources`); every `.so`/`.dylib` re-signed inside-out with the Developer ID and hardened runtime (library validation only loads same-Team-ID code), never `--deep`; the app stapled as well as the DMG so an offline first launch works; published as a GitHub Release so the website button is a plain link to `https://github.com/rorosaga/cicada/releases/latest/download/Cicada.dmg` (free hosting; a hotfix published after a newer release must use `--latest=false` or it becomes "latest"). The backend registered with `SMAppService.agent` from a plist inside the bundle, re-registered when the app's path or version changes (registration is not reliably kept across updates/moves), never while running from the DMG or translocated. Hooks and MCP point at stable links under `~/.cicada/bin` that the app re-points on launch, so capture survives app moves and updates (G105). Torch and the on-device model become an optional in-app download (bundled uv into Application Support). Sparkle 2 or a latest-release check for updates; a Homebrew cask only after signing. **Gaps the fact-checks found, to settle before building:** CLAUDE.md names `install-backend-agent.sh` the one source of the `com.cicada.backend` plist — a bundled plist changes that rail, and an existing legacy plist with the same label must be booted out or the label changed (migration); under `SMAppService` the app becomes the backend's responsible process, so permissions granted to the app may extend to the backend, against the rail that the launchd backend has no Full Disk Access; deleting the app leaves hooks/MCP pointing at dangling links, so capture would fail silently — needs an uninstall/repair story and a hook that fails loudly; a dev checkout and a released app on one Mac must not fight over port 8000 or re-point hooks away from the checkout; a bundled backend must detect and migrate an older bank on first launch after an update; an update check is a network call the app starts on its own and needs a named gate. Unconfirmed from official sources: the exact Gatekeeper dialog wording and whether Open Anyway asks for an admin password, and the minimum hardened-runtime entitlements for embedded Python (start from `cs.allow-unsigned-executable-memory`; `cs.disable-library-validation` only if a runtime-installed extra needs it). **To decide (added):** pay for the Developer ID or ship an unsigned `.dmg` with Open Anyway instructions (free but every first launch hits the block); embedded runtime vs a signed thin app that installs the backend with uv on first launch (smaller, needs the network, Python code not notarized); torch in the download (~250 MB+, estimate) or as an in-app download; bundle a signed `git` or have onboarding ask once for the Command Line Tools; the default embedder for a fresh install (ungated model, words-only, or the gated one with a token); arm64 only or an Intel build too (no x86_64 torch wheels). **Built 2026-10-05, phase 1 — one version:** a `VERSION` file at the repo root (`0.3.0`) is the only place the version is written; `bundle.sh` stamps it as `CFBundleShortVersionString`, `api/version.py` reads it for FastAPI's `app.version` (so `/healthz`) and the MCP server's `serverInfo`, and `api/pyproject.toml` carries the same string (`test_version.py` holds them together). `CFBundleVersion` is the build number: the commit count, or `CICADA_BUILD_NUMBER` when a build passes one, so it only grows along `main`. Settings → General shows "Version x.y.z (build)" and says so when the background service reports a different version. **Built 2026-10-05, phase 2 — the self-contained app:** `bundle.sh --release --with-backend` carries CPython 3.12, the torch-free release dependency set, Cicada's code, a portable git and the int8 bge-small ONNX model (347 MB unzipped, 138 MB zipped), signed ad hoc inside out; agents, hooks and the launchd plist run `~/.cicada/bin` launchers the app rewrites on launch; `CICADA_PORT` everywhere; `scripts/release/smoke-test.sh` proves a build in a temp folder. Plan and rulings: `docs/plans/2026-10-05-g182-releases.md`. **Built 2026-10-05, phase 4 — the release workflow:** `make release VERSION=x.y.z` bumps, merges dev into main, tags and pushes atomically from a temporary worktree; the tag runs `.github/workflows/release.yml` on macos-26, which builds, smoke-tests, zips, signs the zip with Ed25519 and publishes a GitHub Release with `latest.json`; a `ci/release-dry-run` push does all but publish. **Built 2026-10-05, phase 5 — install and update:** testers run `curl -fsSL …/main/scripts/install-release.sh \| bash`; a release app checks GitHub's latest release on launch and every 6 hours (Settings → General → *Install updates automatically*, on by default), verifies sha256 + Ed25519 before unzipping, and installs on quit or *Restart to update* through a detached helper that keeps the old copy on any failure. **Built 2026-10-06, phase 3 — search models:** each bank is built with its own model (the person's choice → what it records → the default), so a default change never re-embeds a bank; Settings → Memory → Search model offers the built-in model and EmbeddingGemma as an optional download (runtime into `~/.cicada/extras` with the bundled pip, weights with the person's own Hugging Face token, used once). **Built 2026-10-06, phase 6 — docs:** `docs/RELEASING.md` (cut a release, what testers run, the signing key, adding a Developer ID and notarization), the tester line in `install.md` and the README. **Owner decisions taken 2026-10-05:** ship ad hoc-signed (no Developer ID yet), a zip on a GitHub Release (no `.dmg` yet), our own updater (CryptoKit Ed25519, no Sparkle), arm64 only, the small ONNX model bundled with EmbeddingGemma optional, git bundled. **2026-10-06 — the bundled search model is multilingual** (owner: "switch to a multilingual one"): `intfloat/multilingual-e5-small` (int8 ONNX, 113 MB + 16 MB tokenizer, MIT, ungated, 384 dims, `query: ` / `passage: ` prompts, mean pooling) replaces the English-only `bge-small-en-v1.5`, which missed every Spanish query against English notes in a spot check where e5 found all six. Banks keep the model they were built with. **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · release infrastructure built; first publication and install acceptance open. | PARTIAL · Today **Status 2026-10-07:** releases are live. Ruling 19 (a release is a dev→main merge) is implemented (#202: CI on `main` tags `VERSION`, owns its draft by id, publishes with the tag spelled out and verified, only `main`'s tip may publish, per-commit concurrency; `release-check.yml`; `make release`/`make release-pr`). **v0.3.0** published (PR #203) and **v0.3.1** (PR #207; installer UTF-8 hotfix #205: `$REPO…` broke `curl | bash` on every Mac — now linted). Install acceptance PASS on v0.3.1 (real curl line under UTF-8 into a temp dir; `smoke-test.sh` all checks). Stable asset `Cicada-macos-arm64.zip` resolves. Open: website button PR (cicada-website #18), real update acceptance 0.3.0→0.3.1 (needs an isolated home: a release app rewrites `~/.cicada/bin`), the integration matrix after the trial's consolidation, Developer ID/notarization, owner GitHub settings (required check + PR-only `main`). |

## P0 — record integrity and reproducible regressions

| ID | Goal | Reasoning and remaining implementation | Current status |
|---|---|---|---|
| G183 | <a id="g183"></a>**Write-safety gaps the two October audits left open — page-lock coverage, a commit that misses a file, a lock held across a scan, and a bank switch that runs ahead of the server** (found 2026-10-05 by the audits' own reviews; the owner asked 2026-10-06 that every disclosed gap live in the backlog) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Audit fixes landed, but page-lock coverage, follow-up owned-path manifest, scan lock duration, manual dedup commit and optimistic bank activation remain. Implement from current reproductions, preserve true writer attribution. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g183). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Each is disclosed in an audit status doc and none is fixed. **(a) Page-lock coverage.** #181 (audit P1-2) added `page_lock.page_lock(bank)`, a cross-process `flock` held across a page's read → reconcile → write and its commit (lock order page → git → episode; `docs/architecture/storage.md`). Claim writes, retracts and the follow-up resolver take it; **Sleep's own page writes and the inbox's other resolvers do not**, so a Sleep batch and an agent's `cicada_write_claim`, or two resolutions, can still race on one page — the exact lost update P1-2 reproduced (both report `written`, one survives). Fix: route every entity-page writer through the lock (Sleep per page, not per batch, so capture never waits a batch out). **(b) A follow-up that merges into an open conflict item** writes that item but leaves it out of the files it commits, so the item rides along in the next writer's commit under the wrong author — the class #189 (P1-3) fixed for resolutions; this case predates it. Fix: list the merged item in the manifest. **(c) The MCP save's dedup scan holds `episode_lock`** (audit 2026-10-02, K01): `cicada_save_episode` hashes every episode while holding the lock that capture, the stager and Sleep's retirement share, so a save on a large bank makes the Stop hook and the stager wait for the whole scan. Fix: scan unlocked, then take the lock and re-check only the candidates (or keep a hash index). **(d) `ActivateBank` runs ahead of the server**: the optimistic apply sets `store.bank` and hydrates the new bank's cache before `POST /banks/activate` lands (`Sync/Mutations.swift`, `ActivateBank.optimistic`), so for about one round trip a refresh or a write leaves for the server's still-active old bank while the app files it under the new one — the split-brain class (`cicada-mcp-split-brain`; A03 closed it for entity reads only). Fix: send the switch first and hydrate on success, or tag every request with the bank it meant and drop answers for another. **(e)** `POST /maintenance/dedup-sweep` leaves its merges uncommitted — tracked on **G21**. **Rails:** one git writer per bank; a write commits alone, under its true author; capture never waits on Sleep longer than one episode (`capture-and-sleep.md`). Evidence: `docs/goals/audit-2026-10-02/STATUS.md`, `docs/goals/audit-2026-10-05/STATUS.md`. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲 APPLY. | PARTIAL · P0 **Status 2026-10-07:** (a)/(e)/G21 shipped #204 — dedup sweep refuses in the window and commits only its pre-computed footprint (refuses dirty/unmerged/symlinked pages, exact rollback); decay/repos/inbox-resolve guarded. (b) not reproducible on any current path and (c) fixed (save scan outside `episode_lock`, lock hold −85%) — #201. (d) shipped #209: one serialized bank switch, `X-Cicada-Bank` + `409 bank_mismatch`, and every request pinned to one bank (ContextVar); scheduler jobs unpinned. Open: the write-admission lock closing the Sleep-transition race (branch `fix/g183-window-admission`, in review); **new (trial):** imported episodes are not committed by the import, so Sleep's first batch commit swept all 1,520 under the model's author. |
| G177 | <a id="g177"></a>**Narrow the "Sleep is running" refusals to the write window — a drain holds them for hours** (found 2026-09-29 building G163's drain) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Backend is_writing guards and paused-run behavior exist. ProjectWriteGate still keys off running, disabling app edits between drain batches; wire the writing contract through the Store. In-window claim attribution remains open, coordinated with G183. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g177). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** A person-started drain keeps `SleepState.status == "running"` for the whole run — many minutes to hours on a first import — and roughly fourteen guards key off that one word: the 409s on projects, entities, backlog, banks export/delete, local sources, memory, maintenance and the remote connector, MCP's `BACKLOG_SLEEPING` refusal, and `mcp_tools._backend_sleep_running`, which makes a stdio agent's claim stand **uncommitted** while Sleep runs so it rides Sleep's next commit (`test_agent_write_commits.py`). Under a drain that means (a) the app's writes are refused for hours, and (b) an agent's claim written between batches is swept by the next batch's `git add -A` under the Sleep author — the G85-class smear, at drain scale. Awake capture is not gated, so its episodes wait safely for the next run. **Design constraint for a fix:** the refusals only need to cover the window in which Stage 5 is writing to the commit, plus the tail's own commits; between batches the tree is clean and a writer can commit safely under its own author. That wants one predicate (`sleep_cycle.is_writing()`?) that every guard and the MCP probe share — never a per-route patch — and the SSE/`GET /sleep/status` contract unchanged. Bank switching is already refused by name for a drain (`SleepState.drain_run`). → follows **G163**, **G85**, **G135**. Triage: APPLY, S/M. **Built 2026-09-29 (branch `feat/consolidate-reads-everything`):** `sleep_cycle.is_writing()` — a plain or scheduled cycle holds the bank for its whole run; a drain only from a batch's Stage 2 (the pages Stage 5 rewrites are read there) through its commit, plus the run's start and tail. It backs the page-write 409 guards (projects, entities, backlog, local sources, memory, maintenance, paper details, the remote connector), `GET /sleep/status`'s `writing` and `mcp_tools._backend_sleep_running` (an older backend that sends no `writing` reads as `status`), so a stdio claim written between batches commits alone under its harness (`test_sleep_drain.py`). Bank switch, export and delete stay refused for the whole run. A claim written inside a window still rides that batch's commit — the minutes-long, pre-drain exposure. *Sleep page v5 (2026-09-30):* a paused run is `idle` and holds nothing (`is_writing()` false; `test_sleep_paused.py`); a scheduled drain now holds the bank-switch guard for its whole run like any other, disclosed (TODO ruling 16). **Previous execution label (historical):** ◐ built (backend + app toast); in-window claims still ride the batch commit; the app's own Projects/Backlog controls still disable on `running` for the whole run until `/status` carries `writing`. | PARTIAL · P0 **Status 2026-10-07:** app half shipped #209 — `sleep.writing` on `/status` and SSE, `ProjectWriteGate` keys off it, refusals carry `code: sleep_writing`. Open: admission lock (with G183); in-window claim attribution (DECIDE). |
| G104 | <a id="g104"></a>**Resumed conversations — incremental consolidation, efficiency and revision correctness** | **Owner efficiency follow-up / current-code check (2026-10-06, dev ba7a4957):** Every changed captured thread is rewritten as the same episode, requeued and extracted from its full captured body; unchanged bodies skip. There is no continuation-only extraction checkpoint. Repeated consolidation of a growing thread repeats prefix input; quantify tokens/calls/latency rather than asserting a measured bill. [Incremental-consolidation research brief](../research/2026-10-06-incremental-consolidation.md): compare append-only new turns plus bounded prior context, stable turn/chunk reuse and targeted revision-aware reprocessing with full fallback against today's baseline. Keep one conversation/promotion unit, dedup observed support, exact canonical evidence offsets/hash and human conflict authority. Checkpoints advance only after successful owned-path commits; test capture races, retries, edits/deletions/compaction, anaphora and late corrections. Head-stable capture caps can drop later turns and leave the body hash unchanged; investigate retention/visibility with G110/G118, not merely cheaper extraction. G163 owns accepted-reading retry reuse, G171 indexes, G110 pre-Sleep continuity. **Priority split:** P0 actual revision/integrity correctness; P1 RESEARCH efficiency and retention. The optimization does not gate the first small G10 trial. **Previous title (historical):** **A resumed conversation is consolidated twice, as if it were two** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "if i was working on a claude code conversation yesterday > slept > then continued in that conversation, is important that the system knows it is continuing"). **Earlier implementation review (before the efficiency follow-up):** One episode per session and changed-body requeue are implemented. Verify/reconcile removed or changed facts derived from previous revisions so grown sessions do not retain obsolete claims as current; new-file dedup is insufficient. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g104). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Verified: the mechanics exist, nothing uses them.** `session_id` is stamped at capture (G48) and `sleep_cycle._collect_session_ids` gathers them into `Cicada-Session:` trailers — but episode *selection* is purely `processed: false` (`sleep_cycle.py`), so Sleep never asks whether a conversation is still open. The failure sequence: work Monday → Sleep consolidates Monday's episodes and marks them processed → continue the **same** session Tuesday → Sleep consolidates the second half **with no awareness it already drew conclusions from the first**. Two concrete harms: (i) the **promotion threshold** is "referenced in 2+ separate conversations", and one split conversation can satisfy it, promoting an entity that was mentioned twice in a single sitting; (ii) a belief formed from partial context is never revisited when the rest of the conversation arrives — the extraction that saw half the story stands as if it saw all of it. **The tension is that `CLAUDE.md` already anticipated this** ("Conversations don't have clean endings. Batch sees patterns across full day") — but that reasoning assumed a conversation is *over* by nightfall, and G48's resumable sessions break the assumption. **REFINED 2026-09-01 (Rodrigo: "should see that either the first chunk produced something or it didnt, if it did re-check and add or remove or keep the same… or just reconsolidate everything?"). The incremental version is probably right, because most of the reconciliation ALREADY EXISTS:** `claim_reconciler` stamps `valid_to`/`superseded_by` when a new claim replaces an old one (`claim_reconciler.py:138-139`), and Wave-1's predicate work decides whether a second value contradicts or accumulates. So claims extracted from the second half already flow through add/supersede/reinforce **without re-reading the first half**. That narrows the gap to three things: (1) the **promotion threshold miscounts** — "2+ separate conversations" is satisfied by one split conversation; count distinct `session_id`s, not episodes (cheap); (2) nothing marks a belief as **formed from partial context**, so a claim from an open conversation is indistinguishable from one from a finished conversation — a provisional flag would let the later extraction supersede rather than merely contradict; (3) **summaries do not reconcile, only claims do** — the entity body is synthesized prose (`conflict_resolver._synthesize_entity_update:642`), so a summary written from half a conversation is NOT repaired by adding claims and must be regenerated. **Working split: claims incremental, summaries regenerated**, with full reconsolidation reserved for the case where the second half contradicts the first — which claim supersession already detects, so it can trigger itself. Cost argues the same way: with the CLI engine Stage 2 is sequential and dominates, so "re-extract everything nightly" is the wrong default. **Candidate solutions, cheapest first:** (a) **defer** — skip episodes whose session is still resumable and idle less than N hours, letting the conversation settle before consolidating (cheap, no new machinery, delays value by up to a day); (b) **treat a session as the promotion unit** — count distinct `session_id`s rather than episodes when evaluating the 2+-conversations threshold (very cheap, fixes harm (i) only); (c) **reconsolidation — the likeliest right answer**: when a conversation grows after having been consolidated, re-run extraction over the *whole* session and supersede the earlier claims rather than appending to them. The claim layer already supports this exactly — `valid_to` + `superseded_by` is precisely "this belief was replaced by a better-informed one" — so reconsolidation is less new machinery than it sounds, and it generalises beyond resumed sessions to *any* case where better evidence arrives later. Cost: extraction is re-run over already-processed episodes, so it needs a bound (re-consolidate a session at most once more, or only while it stays resumable). → depends on **G48** (session identity, already shipped); touches the promotion model and Stage 1/2; relates **G101** (the raw layer that would make a re-run cheap) and **G74** (the engine that must exist for any re-extraction to be possible). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P0 integrity; P1 RESEARCH incremental processing |

Repair its owned-path commit before using it; automatic scheduled sweeping is a separate later slice. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g21). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** The existing manual endpoint leaves merged paths uncommitted (G183e). Repair manifest/commit attribution before using its mutating mode. Wiring a periodic paid whole-bank sweep is a separate later step and is not a prerequisite to the first trial. Use generic fixtures; real values redacted. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** The Stage-2 resolver already does confidence-based LLM dedup, but **incrementally** — it only compares NEWLY-extracted entities against the existing graph. Two entities that both already exist and aren't re-mentioned (e.g. `bob-example` from run 1 vs `bob-example-long` from run 3) never get re-compared, so residual dups accumulate. Add a periodic full-graph pass: embedding-gate existing-entity pairs (same type, high cosine) → LLM same/different/unsure judge with BOTH pages' context → auto-merge high-confidence (combine pages/claims/relationships, repoint edges + wikilinks, delete loser), nudge the uncertain. Runs every Sleep (or every N) so the graph self-heals instead of needing manual merges. Embedding-gating keeps it cheap (only judge plausible pairs). → user-proposed 2026-06-18; the proper fix for "duplicates I have to merge by hand". Needs a real entity-merge primitive (the current inbox merge path absorbs a *mention*, it doesn't consolidate two rich entities). **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY · commit-safety first; automatic sweep later. | PARTIAL · P0 |

(b) **Claude-Code-driven agentic extraction** (Rodrigo's idea) — a Claude Code *workflow/skill* reads an episode batch and writes to the graph via the MCP Bookworm tools, giving a big reasoning model full agentic latitude to write rich pages + choose intermediate concepts (and dogfoods the agent-facing write path; pairs with G9 `origin: claude-code`). Tension: (b) is richer but less deterministic/idempotent and may skip the structured resolution/decay logic. **Likely best = hybrid:** agent does the rich extraction/summarization + concept abstraction; the deterministic pipeline still owns dedup, contradiction (bi-temporal close), decay, and indexing — i.e. the agent writes *claims* through MCP tools that enforce the structure. → gated by D2 final architecture; feeds the M5 migration build plan. **Re-filed 2026-09-01 (hygiene pass):** the D2 architecture gate this row cites is resolved in practice — the claim-layer foundation (M5a-f) and decay classes (G66) have since shipped, so "gated by D2 final architecture" no longer blocks it. For example it'd be nice for me to open a new claude session rn and have it continue the role and work you are doing. could be in cursor, codex or whatever else." That example is slice 1b's acceptance test. **Owner rulings (TODO ruling 22):** D1 — capture keeps up to ~16k of the person's first message in a session (inside the session budget, same scrub), Sleep cost measured before shipping; D2 — a bounded, read-only, fixed-command git identity observer may run in the hook (harness side) only, the backend still parsing only supplied values. Slice 1b plan in revision after a cross-vendor critique. **Incremental / capture-limit linkage (owner 2026-10-06):** G104 investigates continuation-only consolidation and capture caps that can drop later progress. Pre-Sleep continuity cannot rely on a fully reconsolidated graph or claim fresh/full context when capture was capped. Expose actual checkpoint/capture freshness and missing turns; avoid a second authoritative context store. [G104 research](../research/2026-10-06-incremental-consolidation.md). **Start-prompts linkage (owner 2026-10-06):** G73 saves reusable objective briefs that can initiate work; link the chosen version and inputs to the resulting project/session. This row then continues that work automatically using current state, including before Sleep. Keep reusable templates and per-session working context separately addressable; no duplicated authoritative passport. No manual handoff prompt/recap, no special save invocation and no Sleep prerequisite; not tied to quota exhaustion. Capture, session IDs, bounded handshake/state and recall are foundations, not full working-context continuity. Define revision-aware project/task context with decisions, constraints, rejected approaches, files/change references, verified tests, blockers and next action; recover unconsolidated captured progress through lazy cited reads. Deliver automatically at supported session start/reconnect, verify destination workspace freshness, preserve distinct session lineage and prevent echo capture/double consolidation. Cursor integration is G50/G76; Grok delivery is G135, scoped to supported clients. Ambiguous tasks ask a focused selection question. Missing source/workspace/tool context is explicit, never a false promise of a byte-identical thread. Native transcript forking remains a separate optional research slice. [Required behavior and acceptance](../specs/2026-10-06-project-continuity.md). **Previous title (historical):** **Forking and handing off a live conversation — literal transcript transfer vs. memory handoff** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "an ability to include in cicada (forking ongoing conversations and such...)"). **Earlier implementation review (before the owner’s portability clarification):** Memory recall is portable; literal live-transcript fork/handoff is not implemented. Decide whether scope is a memory packet or transcript transfer, with separate consent and attribution. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g110). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The trigger:** Superset ships a button that transfers a whole Claude Code thread to "Codex, OpenCode, Pi, or any other agent", plus a separate "Fork session…". The stated pain is billing, not memory — hit a model's usage limit mid-session and be forced to downgrade or restart the work elsewhere. **Second prior-art data point, NOT yet researched — Cursor ships "Import from Claude Code"** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, screenshot; flagged explicitly as "research how it works later"). Three things are visible in the dialog and each one matters to this row. **(i) It is a bulk *sync*, not a live handoff** — the button says `Sync`, it enumerates what it found (`Chats (2)`), and the copy is "Bring over your chats, and send a follow-up to fork it", i.e. **import-then-continue**: the thread is replayed into a new Cursor thread and forking happens by *sending the next message*, not by branching a live session. That is a materially different mechanism from Superset's per-thread transfer button and from `--fork-session`, and it is the cheapest of the three to implement — which is itself evidence about where the real difficulty is. **(ii) It necessarily reads the Claude Code transcript store** (`~/.claude/projects/*.jsonl`), so it is a live existence proof that the read is practical — and a direct instance of the rail this row is bounded by: reading is fine, *landing content in a bank* is not. **(iii) The part neither Superset nor this row had considered: it syncs `Plugins & skills (12)` by default, alongside the chats.** A conversation is not self-contained — it presupposes the tools, skills and plugins that were available when it happened, and a thread replayed into an agent lacking them produces confident nonsense about capabilities it does not have. For Cicada this opens a **third packet dimension beyond conversation content and consolidated memory: capability context** — which is, notably, the one dimension Cicada is worst placed to supply and the harnesses are best placed to, since Cicada does not model the receiving agent's toolset at all. **Research questions for later, none answered here:** does Cursor read the transcript files directly or drive a Claude Code CLI/export path; does "send a follow-up to fork it" preserve any lineage id or start a genuinely fresh thread; what is the plugins/skills sync actually copying (files, a manifest, a marketplace reference); and does any of it survive when the source session is still live. Settle these before assuming Cicada should build any of the three modes — a mechanism two vendors have independently chosen is a stronger signal than either one alone. **Cicada is unusually well placed for the memory half, because G48 already shipped the hard part:** a conversation has an identity (`resolve_session_identity`, `mcp/server.py:62-89`), it is stamped onto every episode (`_session_frontmatter`, `:96-108`), it is credited transitively to entities through `source_episodes` (`session_stats._group`), it is written into git as `Cicada-Session:` trailers (`git_service.py:35-62`, capped 50), it is listed by `/conversations/recent` and reopened by `POST /conversations/{id}/resume` (`conversations.py:164-207`). Cicada already knows what a conversation *is* and what it *produced*. What it has never had is any relation **between** conversations. **The design fork, and it must be picked before anything is built. (a) Literal transfer** needs one reader per harness and there is no shared format: Claude Code transcripts (`~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<uuid>.jsonl`) are a parent/child-UUID DAG of Anthropic-Messages turns (`content[].type == "text"`), Codex rollouts (`~/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl`) are a flat sequential log of OpenAI-Responses turns (`content[].type == "input_text"`) carrying `sandbox_policy`/`permission_profile`/git metadata with no Claude analogue — and the codebase's own precedent (`conversations.py:213` `detect_source()` dispatching to one `parse_*` per platform) already answers this: **one translator per harness, not one interchange format**. Both vendors also already ship the primitive (`claude --resume` / `--fork-session`; `codex resume [SESSION_ID\|thread_name]` / `codex fork [SESSION_ID]`, both defaulting to a cwd-filtered picker), so a literal transfer is Cicada re-implementing two CLIs' internals cross-vendor. **State the rail precisely, because the loose version is false:** it is *not* "no code opens a session log" — `harness_stats.codex_rate_limits` (`api/services/harness_stats.py:63-88`, live behind `GET /consumption/harness`, `routers/consumption.py:112`) already `read_text()`s and `json.loads()`es whole Codex rollout files today. The rail is **no transcript content enters a bank**, and G76 measured why: 741 MB `~/.claude/projects` and 73 MB `~/.codex/sessions` against a 2.4 MB curated bank. A translator that lands turns in `memory/` crosses it; one that pipes them CLI-to-CLI does not. **(b) Memory handoff** — mint the child its own identity and seed it with a small packet assembled from what the parent already got consolidated: the entity ids touched (`session_stats.find_conversation`, read **uncapped** — the summary projection truncates at `MAX_CONVERSATION_ENTITIES=12`, `session_stats.py:40`), the claims it wrote (`parse_claims`, `claims.py:175`, filtered to claims whose `source_episodes` intersect this conversation's episodes or whose `all_session_ids()` contains it), and the open questions on those entities — the last an **approximation only**, since inbox items key on `entity_id` and carry no session id anywhere (`inbox_service.py:81,333-334,636-638`; `InboxItem`, `schemas.py:795-819`). **(c) Continue from compact — Rodrigo's third framing, and it reorders the other two** (2026-09-01: "we could have the option to continue from compact (handoff kind of), on another agent (could be another harness), or continue with the same stream (which is what fork is probably)"). **First, a vocabulary correction that swaps which mode is hard:** in Claude Code's own terms `--fork-session` branches to a **new** id, while plain `--resume` continues the **same** id — so "continue with the same stream" is *not* fork, it is resume, and `POST /conversations/{id}/resume` **already does it today**; true forking is a one-flag change. **Second, the compaction boundary is a real and better-shaped seam than a transcript read, but the hook Cicada has written down is the wrong one.** `PreCompact` fires *before* the summary exists — verified structurally against a live transcript: the `compact_boundary` event carries only metadata (`preTokens`/`postTokens`/`trigger`, measured 905,931 → 26,812 tokens, ~30x) and the ~12.6-18.2k-char prose summary is the *next* line, a synthetic `user` turn. The hook that carries content is **`PostCompact`**, which is real and **appears nowhere in Cicada's docs or backlog**. **Third, and this is the trap:** G49 does list a "PreCompact flush" and G76 specs that hook precisely — as a **content-free pointer** (`{session_id, transcript_path, cwd, reason}`, "a pointer, NO content"). So carrying a compaction summary is **not** a second consumer of an already-designed capture point; it deliberately cuts against what G76 chose, and needs its own ruling rather than being smuggled in as an extension. No hooks are installed in the repo today at all (no `hooks` key in `.claude/settings.json`). **Why the framing is still the strongest of the three:** a compaction summary is already lossy, already prose, already stripped of harness structure — i.e. already the packet shape (b) has to construct — and it exists at exactly the moment the user hits the limit the tweet complains about, which is the one moment (b)'s packet is emptiest. **The tempting parallel, checked and only half-true:** a harness compacting because its window filled is structurally the same operation as Sleep — episodic turns compressed into semantic summary under capacity pressure — and Cicada's provenance/claims apparatus is a genuine edge over bare summary prose. But it does **not** cash out as a shortcut: Sleep is nightly batch over *closed* episodes while a hook has seconds mid-task, and compaction content is mid-task working state — the exact granularity G76 already excluded as "volume without judgment". The parallel justifies caring, not reusing the pipeline. **Fourth objection, specific to this half:** the hook surface is a moving target — the live hooks doc lists **33 event names**, most (`Setup`, `PostToolBatch`, `WorktreeCreate`, `PreModelSwitch`, …) absent from Cicada's own inventory — so anything built tightly against it dates fast. **What it does to provenance.** No conversation-to-conversation relation exists: zero hits for `parent_session`/`forked_from`/`thread_id` across every `*.py` and `*.swift`; `ConversationSummary` (`schemas.py:263-296`) and its Swift twin carry a flat `conversation_id`, a closed `kind ∈ {mcp, import}`, and the entities touched — nothing pointing at another conversation. An optional `parent_session_id` threads through exactly the four places `session_id` already flows (episode frontmatter, the 3-field frozen `SessionIdentity` at `mcp/server.py:55-59`, `_group`/`project_conversation`'s "first non-empty across the group" collection, one optional field on `ConversationSummary` + its Swift twin); trailers need no change (already a dedup'd flat list) but **degrade semantically** — a cycle spanning a parent and its forks reads as N unrelated conversations. **Memory stays shared, never branched:** the only branch-memory primitive is `POST /banks/{name}/duplicate`, the wrong grain (a deliberate life-partition, not a per-session artifact), and a working tree per fork would force all 20 routers to become branch-aware and break filesystem-as-single-source-of-truth. **One thing to NOT claim:** sibling forks that contradict each other are not silently archived — `claim_reconciler.trust_decision` (`claim_reconciler.py:170-236`) is trust-gated, returns `SUPERSEDE` for two `agent_extracted` claims only `if newer` and otherwise `CONFLICT_NUDGE` (`:210-212`), and since `valid_from` is always a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` two same-day forks tie and **already raise an inbox card**. The narrow real gap is that the card's option description (`_claim_option`, `:248-259` — age plus source episode) cannot say "this was a deliberate branch point". **Strongest objection, and it is serious: the packet is emptiest exactly when it is wanted.** Sleep is batch by ruling ("Batch over real-time consolidation"), and `_get_unprocessed_episodes` / `POST /sleep/trigger` run over the whole bank's `processed: false` backlog — there is no scoped-to-one-conversation sleep — so a session forked twenty minutes in has episodes and near-zero consolidated claims, and populating its packet would mean a synchronous bank-wide Sleep (slow, real spend, touches unrelated entities). The feature's value therefore skews to forking **old, already-slept** conversations, not the live "I hit my limit, hand off now" case the tweet is actually about. Second objection: a brand-new session already inherits everything Sleep consolidated, for free, via `cicada_recall` ("use this at the start of conversations", `mcp/server.py:137`) — lineage metadata improves provenance *presentation*, it unblocks no capability. **NOT DECIDED — do not build a half on assumption.** (i) literal mid-session branch vs. linear handoff (Superset ships both as separate buttons; only the handoff half is satisfiable inside the bank rail); (ii) whether a same-harness fork should get a NEW session id at all, or keep the parent's — keeping it sidesteps **G104** entirely for the common case, while a fork otherwise makes G104 strictly worse (three fragments of one shared prefix, and the "2+ separate conversations" promotion threshold reading siblings as independent corroboration); (iii) who calls the packet — a new MCP tool the parent invokes pre-handoff, or the user pasting; (iv) whether cross-harness lineage is admissible at all, since no signal crosses harnesses (a Codex rollout has no Claude UUID concept), so `parent_session_id` would be a **declared** fact self-reported by a third-party tool — an unauthenticated provenance channel Cicada has never accepted; (v) **UNVERIFIED** whether `claude --fork-session` exposes a parent-session env var to the child's MCP process, and under what name — only a structurally analogous pair was observed live (`CLAUDE_CODE_BRIDGE_SESSION_ID` beside `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION=1`, for subagent spawns), and `resolve_session_identity` reads neither; settle it with an actual `--fork-session` env dump. Note that a *minting* path is not the gap — tier 2 is documented as an "explicit override for any MCP client" with `CICADA_SESSION_HARNESS` naming the harness (`mcp/server.py:42-43`) and tier 3 mints a `ses_` id for any caller (`:82-86`); what is missing is a Codex-side auto-detect branch. **Triage: RESEARCH, not committed work — and the cheap half is already filed.** G53's state dictionary plus G75's handshake hand any connecting agent a curated cursor with no transcript read at all; a fork packet is largely that payload plus a `from_conversation_id`, so build those first and see whether the fork want survives. → extends **G48** (conversation identity, the only reason this is addressable at all), reuses **G53**/**G75** rather than duplicating them; bounded by **G76**'s no-transcript-content-in-a-bank rail and its 741 MB / 73 MB / 2.4 MB measurement, and **contradicting G76's content-free-pointer choice** for the compact half, which needs its own ruling; extends **G49** (whose plugin design names a PreCompact flush but not `PostCompact`, the hook this actually needs); blocked on a **G104** ruling (a fork multiplies its double-consolidation harm); relates **G105** (the per-harness transcript stores and the absence of an interchange format) and **G106** (the two-way conversation↔entity loop a packet would render). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P1 **Status 2026-10-07:** slice 1a shipped #210 — same-folder rollover before Sleep (SessionStart startup/clear, fail-closed source, whole-context budget, `cicada_continue` pager; registry + disposable index in a guarded home outside every bank; no git in the hook path). Owner gates pending: A (PreCompact/SessionEnd flush vs ruling 7), B (capture-cap head+tail retention), C (G149 strip only harness-marked spans). Acceptance 5 recorded as a strict behavioural xfail (T10). Live acceptance deferred until the trial's bank is no longer active. |

[Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g61). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Owner follow-up (2026-10-06), current remaining scope:** [source/video follow-up](../research/2026-10-06-source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md) traces the actual code. Refresh entries, optional source-node links, fact grouping and source edit/add UI are implemented; these are distinct from wikilinks and evidence citations. The pending-question agent check is shadow-only, not general refresh for an ordinary current-fact query. Add an easy profile/social source flow with a purpose picker mapped to real predicates (`works-at`, `works-on`, `website`, general reference), access/trust and fact-check freshness; do not conflate confirmed site identity with fresh facts. Reuse an existing canonical source node and offer explicit save/create-and-link when useful; mandatory source-node creation is a design question, with lightweight refs retained in the recommendation. Expose the ranked cheat sheet to relevant recall/detail/CLI, define on-demand checks independent of inbox items and their finding-record path, then bounded Sleep pre-checks/agent handoffs. A permitted running agent can read the relevant source before answering; adding a source alone does not grant access. Current S3 never settles/reorders/changes a belief; old automatic-settlement sketches below are superseded by that rail. See the note for the approved-vs-proposed distinction and acceptance criteria. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** A contributor (model *or* user) can attach **sources** to a fact: a URL, a file, or a plain-English instruction ("my LinkedIn profile has my current job", "the `about` page of the company site lists the team"). Stored as a `sources:` frontmatter list on the entity (`{ref, kind: url\|path\|note, predicate?, added_by, added_at}`) and optionally on individual claims — distinct from `source_episodes` (provenance of *where a belief came from*) and from the `## Links` body section (loose bookmarks): a source is a **cheat-sheet for refreshing** a specific fact. Uses: (a) Stage 3 conflict scoring consults sources before asking — if a `works_at` conflict has a LinkedIn source, the model can propose "your profile says X" as the leading option, or resolve silently when trust rules allow; (b) the app's entity page shows sources with an "add source" row (URL or free text) and a "check now" action (`POST /entities/{id}/sources/check` → fetch → LLM compare → claim proposal via the inbox, never a silent overwrite); (c) `cicada_write_claim` accepts `sources=[…]` so a model can record "user told me to check <url> for this" mid-conversation. Fetching is keyless and rate-limited; private pages simply stay `note`-kind reminders the user acts on. → extends G60 (conflict inputs), M3/A2 (contributors), G9 (origins), G22/G30 (media & links). **Shipped 2026-08-30 (minimal slice):** `sources:` frontmatter via `api/services/fact_sources.py`, `GET/POST/DELETE /entities/{id}/sources`, `cicada_write_claim(sources=)`, the conflict-card `hint`, and the app's entity Sources section. Fetching/"check now" remains a follow-up. **Update 2026-09-23 — agent-first clarification (Rodrigo: "remember in our cicada system inbox, some things can be clarified by checking a link to a website or an app using browser harness or computer use or something similar, so make sure to accomodate entities to have sources that can be used for clarification before scaling it to the user itself").** Reopened: this is (a) and (b) above, unbuilt. **The design is an escalation ladder:** before an inbox question reaches the person it climbs (1) Cicada's own public fetch — `link_enrichment.default_fetch` verbatim, zero-LLM, user-triggered "Check now" (`POST /inbox/{id}/check`, not the `/entities/{id}/sources/check` sketched in (b)) ungated like `sync_now`, the Sleep-tail pre-check behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH`, no fourth gate; (2) an agent the person runs (browser harness, computer use, an app, a file), in flow or in a routine the person set up (`cicada_check_nudges(checkable=true)`), reporting through a new `cicada_record_check` (remote scope `record`) — the G140 watch-record shape: one `source-check` episode, ≤ 3 × 240-char quotes, `processed: true` from birth, a new seventh evidence kind `source` with a required `source [<host or app>]:` marker (never `page`, which `kind_for` reserves for an entity document); (3) the person, with the check on the card. **Which items are checkable** is one pure read-time function over kind (claim-path single-valued conflicts; divergence, clarification and merge inform-only; decay/normalization/removal never) and a new predicate `locus: world\|artifact\|person` (G121's marking at the predicate level; `person` is never checked, `artifact` recommends only, unknown needs a person-added predicate-matched source). **Sources grow additively:** `access` (public/signed_in/local, inferred), `kind: app\|repo`, `add_source` keyed on `(ref, predicate)`, the hint derived at read and voiced by `added_by` (today it says "You said" for agent and cicada sources), `cicada_add_source`, "Where could this be checked?" and "Only I know" on the card. **Resolution:** a finding settles only when two witnesses agree — a local agent's (or, 💸 later, Cicada's own LLM) semantic `supports` plus Cicada's own rail re-read of the same site within 24 h that locates the quote and finds no other option's label anywhere on the page. It never closes a human claim, the owner's own page recommends only, a remote report never settles, and the winner is capped at 0.8 below a person's 0.9. A settle is authored `Cicada-Author: cicada`, no engine, rule in the manifest (the G116(a) question with a second caller), and moves to `inbox/settled/`, reviewable, with "Ask me instead" as a real revert that writes `_check_overruled.yaml` so the pair never auto-settles again. **Ships in shadow first:** a settle-grade finding takes DR-42's first highlight and the person's pick grades it (`check_verdict`); the owner flips "Let a source settle a question" to auto with the numbers in view — never automatically. **The ToS rail binds Cicada's bytes; an agent in the person's own session is the person reading** (the G140 precedent); Cicada never spawns or drives one, never relays a credential, never searches for a person, and (pending owner ruling) gives no check line for a host it refuses to fetch itself. **Also fixes, in S0:** Stage 5.57's `default_summarize` fetch is ungated and looser than the rail (`sleep_cycle.py:1348-1352`); the duplicate `source_episode` key in `_conflict_nudge`; organic resolution not checking `is_human`. An agent's `proposes` report writes no claim (a check-time claim would supersede the machine options through the reconciler); it adds an option the person can pick. Slices S0–S8 (S0–S2 backend-only, startable now; S2 is a read-only coverage census that gates the rest), 16 rulings R-AC1…R-AC16, and owner decisions D-AC1…D-AC5 (settle default, LinkedIn-class hosts, the seventh evidence kind, G116(a), the contract change) are in `docs/specs/2026-09-23-g61-agent-first-clarification-design.md`. → uses **G118**, **G140**, **G113**, **G135**, **G115** (its Phase 2 "Research this" judge becomes a checker writing the same record); gives **G121** its world/personal marking and its re-verification mechanism; decides with **G116(a)**. APPLY, M×9 (S8 💸). **Ruled 2026-09-23 (owner: "i'd go with your suggestions"):** D-AC1 shadow first, flipped only by the owner; D-AC2 a `Check first:` line for refused hosts, only in the person's own open session, never signing in, inform-only forever; D-AC3 the `source` evidence kind; D-AC4 `cicada` authors a check-executed write; D-AC5 contract step 2 lets an agent check before asking. **S0 shipped 2026-09-23 (`feat/g61-sources-checkable`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-23-g61-s0-s2.md`):** Stage 5.57's page read goes through `link_enrichment.default_fetch` behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` (it had no gate and downloaded whole bodies on a 5 s timeout); `add_source` is keyed on `(ref, predicate)`; a conflict's `hint` is derived at read (`fact_sources.served_hint`) in the voice of whoever added the source, and the Sleep writers stop storing it; `_conflict_nudge` cites the newest episode (a behaviour change for new items); organic resolution requires `claim_reconciler.is_human` (a narrowing: a `user_stated` claim from a non-human origin no longer closes a question by itself); the demo bank's works-at conflict carries a person-added `example.com` source. **S1 shipped 2026-09-23:** the source record gains `access` (stated, else inferred at read), `kind: app\|repo`, `accepted` and `only_me` (also on `POST /entities/{id}/sources`, where the person's repeat applies them); the vocabulary gains `locus: world\|artifact\|person` (seed + bank map, most conservative wins); `cicada_write_claim(sources=)` takes `{ref, access}`; new `cicada_add_source` (remote `record`, never a path or repo remotely); Stage 5.56 attaches a URL found verbatim in a new claim's cited span on a world/artifact predicate (zero LLM). **S2 shipped 2026-09-23:** `InboxItem.check` `{state, reason, locus, targets, rungs, settle_eligible}` derived at read by `source_check` (pure, engine-free, no fetch, nothing stored; no ETag or app change — the app's decoder ignores it); `GET /inbox/check-census` and `scripts/check-census.sh <bank>` give ids-free counts — the coverage measurement spec §15 takes at S2 and again two weeks after S5. Found on the way: `paper_metadata` already writes a `cicada` source (a DOI link), contrary to the spec's §4.3 note; it is never settle-grade. DS-3a (2026-09-24): the entity card's Look it up at shows S1's fields in words — the fact, the stated access (a path or repo reads as a file on this Mac, an app as an app), who added it, accepted / only-me — and no check line; `GET /entities/{id}/sources` serves only the STATED access, so an unstated url shows none until it also serves `effective_access`. **2026-09-29 (reading-the-web spec):** the Reader ladder (**G164**) is this row's rung 1 and the agent reading queue (**G166**, `cicada_reading_queue` / `cicada_record_read`) its rung 2; S4's `default_fetch` reuse becomes the ladder, so G61 S4 and G164's S1 share one implementation. The optional classifier navigator (**G168**) is where 'find the page that holds this fact' from a source's home page would use a small model. **2026-09-30 — Rodrigo:** "source should be something that was supposed to be built already, as also to give agents a place to look for information to update memory before surfacing it to the user … where does rodrigo work, my linkedin profile might be a source for update … Sources are of extreme importance, and from them even the images can be derived." Two things follow. (1) **Sources onto pages is the next step, as the census already said** (2026-09-23: 22 of 35 open items need a source first, 0 checkable): a company, tool or project page needs its own website as a source, and the person's own pages their profile pages, before any checker has something to check — so the next slice puts sources on pages (Stage 1 proposing an official site for a world-locus page, verified on Cicada's rail before it is kept; an agent adding one with `cicada_add_source`; "What would settle this?" on the card), then S3's agent check through G166's queue. (2) **Pictures come from sources** (G146's brand rung): `logo_service.domain_for` should read a page's website source first and never fall back to guessing a domain from the name — on the live bank the name guess gave a small company a stranger's mark and an AI provider a fireworks show's (real names redacted). A page with no website source gets its monogram until it has one. **2026-09-30 — S3-a shipped (sources as a living set; PR1 of the plan, branch `feat/sources-set`).** Rodrigo: "sources … could be multiple, not just one. An agent can store/change/delete sources depending on whether they are relevant for information update. Sources themselves can be linked to their own memory node which might have more info about it." Built: a page holds many sources per fact (caps `MAX_SOURCES` 30, `MAX_PER_PREDICATE` 8, `MAX_TOMBSTONES` 20 — D7); `fact_sources.rank` is the one picker (the person's, then a taken or verified one, Cicada's, an agent's) and `source_trusted`; `cicada_change_source` (`update \| remove`, `record` scope, `reason` required for a removal) and `entity`/`origin` on `cicada_add_source`; `fact_sources.owns_source` (an agent changes only what it added; a connection only its own `origin: remote:<id>`; the person's, a taken one, Cicada's and a Sleep model's never); `POST /entities/{id}/sources/change` keyed `(ref, predicate)`; a removal leaves a `sources_removed:` tombstone every non-person writer respects (today `attach_cited_urls`, `write_claim(sources=)`, `cicada_add_source` and Cicada's own DOI/skill-page adds; Stage 1's proposal and the website backfill will consult it when PR2 lands; an agent's own removal does not block another agent, the person's and Cicada's do — D6; the index DELETE tombstones too); `entity:` on a source (validated, never creates a page, a stale id reads as none, exact-match backfill `source_links` on the Sleep tail and `POST /maintenance/link-sources` — D8 — a read-time graph edge like `has repo`, the card's "Open page ›"); `entity_merge` now carries `sources`, `sources_removed` and repoints `entity:` (a page merge used to drop a page's sources); the card groups by fact with a per-row menu. Contract 12 / remote 9. **Deviations:** the unidentified `agent` label owns no entry (Stage 1's proposals carry it too), so a harness with no label cannot change its own; `cicada_add_source` used to write uncommitted while Sleep runs; it is refused then too (fixed in the review). **Still to build:** PR2 (a site verified on Cicada's rail, `domain_for` reading only a trusted website source, the logo purge, Stage 1's `website` field) and PR3 (the agent check on G166's queue). **2026-09-30 — S3-b shipped (PR2 of the plan: websites verified, pictures from sources; branch `feat/sources-sites`).** Rodrigo: "the image resolver is not working the best for not as known things. My idea is maybe to get it from the icon of the actual website source … Sources are of extreme importance, and from them even the images can be derived." Built: Stage 1's optional `website` (company/tool/project; `sanitize_website` keeps an https origin of a public, non-platform host); stored UNVERIFIED by the create branch (`fact_sources.propose_site`, never over a tombstone); an engine-free backfill from a `website` claim or a `## Links` host that is the page's own name; `link_enrichment.fetch_identity` on `default_fetch`'s transport (`_stream_html`); `site_sources.judge`/`verify` (the name plus two words of the page's own summary; D1, revised in review: verified stamped; only a positive namesake (the name on the page, its summary words absent) is removed and remembered; anything unproven — a thin page, a redirect, a bot block — is "not confirmed"; a network failure is tried three nights, then again in a month, never tombstoned); the Sleep-tail step behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` (≤ 25 fetches, one per site, `sleep/site-check`) and `POST /maintenance/verify-sites`. `logo_service.domain_for` now reads ONLY a trusted `website` source (no name guess, no `## Links`, saved-link or claim fallback, no platform host, never a person); `LOGO_RULE` 2 purges an older cache once (`sites/` spared); a page that loses its site drops its mark. The card shows "Official site · confirmed <day>" or "not confirmed yet · Use this site", a type-aware add field, and each row's site mark (`GET /entities/{id}/sources/icon/{site}`). **Still to build:** PR3 (the agent check on G166's queue). **Not yet seen live:** the confirmed / not-confirmed / mismatch split on the real bank (run `POST /maintenance/verify-sites` on a copy first and read the counts). **2026-09-30 — S3 shipped, shadow (PR3 of the plan; branch `feat/sources-check`).** Rodrigo: "give agents a place to look for information to update memory before surfacing it to the user … where does <person> work, my profile page might be a source for update." Built: check entries in G166's reading queue (a pending `checkable` question's own ranked sources; the one consent is the per-site permission, D5; the owner's page only what the person added or took, D2; least recently checked first); `cicada_record_check` (`record` scope) writing one `source-check` episode with `attachment [host]:` page-kind quotes (D4) and a `checks[]` row on the item; `check_agent` ledger kind; contract 13 / remote 10; `GET /inbox` `checks[]`; the card shows what the agent reported with no highlight, no tag, no reorder; Settings' site list shows how many questions a site could answer. `website` sources match page-level questions and a verified one waives the unknown-locus gate. **Nothing settles, holds or reorders** (S4–S8 wait: the fetch rung, settling, the hold window, retirement, the paid witness). **Not yet seen live:** a real agent checking a real profile page; run `scripts/check-census.sh` on a copy of the bank before and after to see how many questions became checkable (counts only). **Audit 2026-10-02 A05/A06 (`fix/audit-app-consistency`):** the card's source writes ran as independent tasks with whole-list snapshots, so a failure could roll back another write's success; they now run through a card-local `SourceWriteQueue` in order. An add cleared the field before its request and swallowed failures (`try?`); it is now the `EntitySourceAdd` mutation, keeps the draft and toasts the server's sentence. **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · core sources built; general refresh + source UX priority, P2. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G22 | <a id="g22"></a>**Video & frame/image entities + "watch video" agent skill** | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Watch records create attributed episodes that Sleep can consume. Video/frame interpretation is supplied by the person's agent; raw saves do not watch themselves. Prove watched content survives consolidation and paraphrased recall. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g22). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Owner follow-up (2026-10-06):** [source/video follow-up](../research/2026-10-06-source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md). The old status saying the app watch UI is unbuilt is stale: G162 implements it. `watch_record._write_episode` creates `processed: false` video-watch episodes and `record` writes an attributed `describes` claim with timed excerpts; source-agnostic Sleep can consume the episode. What remains is real acquisition → record → Sleep → project/concept recall acceptance with timestamp provenance and creator/owner distinction. Saving a raw video link is not watching or video consolidation. Full transcripts/new video/frame entity types in the founding sketch are superseded; retain the excerpts/no-download/closed-type rails. Do not report this end-to-end path as proven from queue UI or a synthetic record test alone. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** First-class **`video`** entities (a link/reference to the video) and **`image`/`frame`** entities. **Source of truth = the video's transcript/captions**, stored with the entity; the *agent* watches the video (via a Claude video skill — Rodrigo flagged [`bradautomates/claude-video`](https://github.com/bradautomates/claude-video), not yet set up) and saves a **summary + any discussion thoughts** relevant to the conversation as the entity body. Later a query like *"show me all the robotics videos I've saved for my research"* surfaces them and the agent knows what each entails. → extends **G2** (media taxonomy), builds on `cicada_save_url`; the summary-grounded-in-transcript pattern is the media cousin of **Phase 3 source-grounded rewrite** (`docs/specs/2026-07-03-retrieval-and-bookworm-improvement-design.md`). **The 2026-09-05 in-app video renderer (Track V) is G11's *preview* half and moves this row not at all:** a saved video now plays in the app, but transcripts/captions as the entity body — the half that answers the query above — remain the open work here. **G140 (2026-09-23, `feat/memory-quality`):** the four defects in the documented watch chain are fixed — a note for an already-saved video is kept as its own episode instead of dropped; both save replies name the episode to cite; `external:<name>` is a value the observer schema accepts; and a watch is recorded by `cicada_record_watch(url, summary, excerpts=[{t, quote}], chapters?)` as one episode (`assistant:` summary, `video [m:ss]:` quotes) plus one `describes` claim whose quote spans have the new `media` kind — excerpts only, never a transcript (R5 D3), Cicada never downloads a video. Vimeo/Loom descriptions are kept and chapters parsed from them (`media.chapters`). The row's "transcript stored with the entity" is superseded by cited excerpts. Open: the app's watch UI (Track P/O). **2026-09-29:** the app's watch UI (this row's open item) is designed as G162 — states, a queue outside the bank, a thumbnail picker and a person-triggered watch run ([`2026-09-29-video-understanding-design.md`](../specs/2026-09-29-video-understanding-design.md)); 'transcript stored with the entity' stays superseded by cited excerpts. **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · recording and Sleep intake built; useful-video acceptance open, P2. | PARTIAL · P1 |
