So using skills like claude-video might allow us to do this but maybe we should have a queue and ask the user to run a separate consolidation with selection (showing thumbnails), of what videos to consolidate. By default we can always get whatever info from metadata or also have a transcript only. But then when looking at the videos consolidated show wether it was seen by an agent or just transcript consolidate or both or neither. And in the app i can enable to actually watch the video which would put that 'watch' in queue, same with 'transcript in queue'.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Queue/leases, watch-run UI, derived state and attributed recorder exist. Prove recording → Sleep → useful grounded recall; fix observed unknown-host, returning-to-run and seek seams. Optional duration/model rungs are not prerequisites. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g162). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Owner follow-up (2026-10-06):** [source/video follow-up](../research/2026-10-06-source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md). Prioritize the usable watch workflow and G22 end-to-end acceptance before peripheral capture channels. States/queue/handoff and unprocessed watch records are implemented. Verify the reviewed skill path/re-pin (V4b), allowed acquisition, current login/reading-method integration with G166, unknown-host queueing, return-to-run UX and timestamp navigation against current code. Queue credit, agent-reported basis and Sleep processing are different states. V5 optional lengths and V6 direct keyed understanding stay DECIDE and do not block the existing agent path. Include one allowed watched-video trial in G10; do not require optional direct API processing or new embeddings before it. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The gap, from the code (spec §1, read at `dev` @ `2155940`).** `cicada_record_watch` (G140) writes one episode and one `describes` claim per watch, but nothing says *how* a video was read (a transcript or the frames), the app has no surface for the record (G22's open item, Track P/O), and nothing lets the person choose which of the saved videos deserve an agent's time. Watching is the expensive step, so it must be deliberate; a title and description cost nothing. **The design ([`2026-09-29-video-understanding-design.md`](../specs/2026-09-29-video-understanding-design.md), 20 review critiques checked against the code, 19 held and are fixed; §12 is the record).** (1) *Two steps named apart:* Sleep consolidates what an agent recorded; a **watch run** is a separate, person-started act in the Feed's Videos view (a thumbnail picker and a run card, then *Copy for an agent*). Cicada never downloads, watches or transcribes; the person's own agent does the work under G140's rail. (2) *Modes:* metadata always (free); transcript the cheap per-video default; watch always a deliberate click. Sizes show as Light, Medium or Heavy plus minutes, never a price (the standing no-price ruling); there is no batch cap (owner 2026-09-29/30): a hand-off takes every selected video, an agent's claim leases 10 a call and its prompt loops, and the queue file's only ceiling is a 2,000-row safety limit. (3) *Five honest states*, a set-union over the records: `none`, `transcript`, `watched`, `watched_and_transcript` and `recorded` ("method not given" — every legacy or basis-less record, never labelled 'Watched'); the owner named four, the fifth is the honest answer for every record made before this change. (4) *Identity:* the queue and the state key on the url-index `videoKey`, never the media entity id, because same-titled videos share an entity id (see G172). (5) *The queue is outside the bank* (no commit noise, no dirty-tree smear), with a satisfaction table (a `transcript` record closes a transcript request, `frames` or `both` close either, a basis-less record closes only the leaseholder's entry) and a `VideoRefresh` sync component. (6) *MCP:* `cicada_video_queue` (read scope) and `cicada_video_claim` (record scope) — two tools because one tool cannot carry two scopes; `record_watch` itself is unchanged apart from a `basis`. Copy says 'picked up by an agent', not 'being watched', and derives 'Read by Sleep' from `processed_by`. (7) *Rails:* the 'Use my browser' method is removed for YouTube; a new grep test with a named allowlist guards the no-download rule; optional V5 API data stays outside the bank with a 30-day life. **Slices:** V1 states and basis; V2 queue and the two tools; V3 picker, run card, hand-off and demo (the queue lives outside the bank, so the demo shows the run card by an app fixture plus the person's own click, and adds two saved videos that move the pinned media counts); V4a neutral skill wording, V4b after a re-pin; V5 an optional YouTube key for lengths (DECIDE); V6 an in-app Gemini watch on the person's own key (DECIDE, gated). Acceptance tests A1–A9, B1–B11, C1–C11, D1–D4, E1–E5, F1–F5. **Also filed here, as sub-items not new rows:** a Cicada-launched agent for watching (needs a non-`--safe-mode` spawn; RESEARCH, not recommended) and a logged-in-browser read for non-YouTube videos (decided with **G166**). **Open questions for the owner (spec §8):** Q1 may Cicada itself ask Gemini to watch a link (recommend not yet); Q2 the queue outside the bank (recommend yes); Q3 auto-queue transcripts on save (recommend no, one click plus an off-by-default setting); Q4 the run lives in the Feed, not the Sleep page (recommend the Feed); Q5 an optional YouTube key for lengths (recommend yes, off by default); Q6 how loud to be about the local downloader (recommend keep it listed, and lead with the path where the person's own agent opens the video link itself, after a re-pin — the copy names no provider); Q7 keep the word 'watch run'; Q8 a logged-in-browser read for non-YouTube videos (recommend deciding it with G166); Q9 keep the fifth state. → extends **G22**, **G140**, Track V; reads against **G138** (the skill catalog), **G125** R10 (Sleep keeps one trigger), **G166**. Triage: V1–V4 APPLY, V5 and V6 DECIDE. **2026-09-30 — the boards were approved and the backend built (branch `feat/video-watch`, spec §13; TODO ruling 17).** Rodrigo: "I also like the watch video designs, apply them." Amended by the owner's words: **no batch cap** (a hand-off takes every selected video, a claim leases 10 a call and the prompt loops; the queue file's safety ceiling is 2,000 rows, not 200), **provider-neutral copy** (nothing names a provider or model as the one doing the job; the engine `gemini_url` became `video_link`), and **no site list for video** (any saved video can be queued; a login wall is handed back with a `needs_login` code and surfaced, and whether an agent may use the person's browser is the reading plan's single permission — the hand-off carries it as an instruction only while it is on). **Built (backend):** the states and their derivation (`video_state.py`, one shared kind rule with the Feed, `video_kind.json`), the record's `basis`/`engine`/`duration` at all five argument sites, the queue outside the bank (`video_queue.py`, `videoQueue` sync stamp that makes a lease lapse visible, `nextChangeAt`), `cicada_video_queue` and `cicada_video_claim` at every MCP site (not refused while Sleep runs; a lapse is judged only when Sleep does not hold the pages), the hand-off prompt and `/videos/*` routes, the provenance `watch` block and fidelity, the demo's three states, the `video_queue` ledger kind, the contract clause (CONTRACT_VERSION 10, remote 7). **Built (app, same branch):** the Feed's video rows and the Videos strip, the picker and the watch run card (the boards' four states, *Copy for an agent*, progress on `batch.done`), `VideoBlock` in the Feed's detail and the entity card's media block, the Reader's watch header and per-turn fidelity, the Sleep page's one Details row (`VideosWaitingRow`), `VideoStateCache` (revalidates at `nextChangeAt`), all copy provider-neutral under a test. The librarian's `/watch` line (V4a) is rewritten neutral. **Not built:** V4b/V5/V6; the room sentence's "An agent has picked up n videos." rung (left to the Sleep v5 branch, which owns `RoomSentence`; the input is `VideoSummary.claimed`); the needs-login row's *Allow your agent to use your browser* button and its sentence (both wait for the reading branch's permission API; `VideoActions.for(_:permission:)` takes `nil` until then, and `nil` shows no sentence — final review 2026-09-30); and "How your agent watches" (a seam: `video_prompt.method_clause`, filled by the reading branch's `agent_methods`). **Open:** a video on a host `video_urls` does not know is a link and cannot be queued (recommend a follow-up "Have an agent watch this" on any saved non-paper link); the boards do not show how to return to an unfinished run (ask the designer). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · queue/UI/basis built; watching path and end-to-end acceptance open, P2. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G166 | <a id="g166"></a>**Reading the web, part 3 — reading with an agent, and the person's own signed-in sessions** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29: "if using something like browser harness in the background or browser use from chatgpt or claude directly is really good because that way stuff like linkedin, twitter or whatever can be seen from the logged in sessions of the user themselves." [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g166). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Owner follow-up (2026-10-06):** [source/video follow-up](../research/2026-10-06-source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md). This is the access/method layer used by G61 source checks and G162 video handoffs. A general source cheat sheet does not itself authorize a read; preserve site/method permissions, master switch, source removal/revocation, caller scopes and login/blocked outcomes. Cicada does not spawn a signed-in browser; the person’s running agent uses its permitted tools. Verify current source/video handoff integration before repeating old “branch pending” work. Reader Links/public-reader extensions and Route B remain their named open scopes. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Answer to the second question: consolidation itself never gets a browser** (spec §8.1) — Sleep reads text it was given; a browser is a different, person-visible act. **Measured (spec §8.2):** in `claude -p`, `--chrome` overrides the locks the spawn sets, so a spawned engine with a browser is a different risk class from today's. Whether it finishes without an interactive prompt is unverified until S6. **What is on this Mac today (§8.3):** detected by bundle id, never by opening a profile folder; there is no per-browser 'Connected' state and no 'in your browser' copy. **The two ways an agent reads (§8.4):** *Route A*, the recommended one — the person's own agent (with its own browser skill or extension) reads through `cicada_reading_queue` and records with `cicada_record_read`, contract item 9 and its own primer cache key; *Route B*, a spawned browse call class (`BROWSE_FLAGS`), only if three spikes say yes (S6, then S7). **Safeguards:** pages Cicada's reader could not read reach an agent only via a per-link *Ask an agent* or a per-site permission the person turns on (one entry per site per call; nothing is on until they do), and Cicada promises nothing about what the agent does in its browser; an ask's URL is visible to any connection holding `read` (TODO ruling 14 departs from the spec here — the person's own "Ask an agent" is the consent to hand that one URL to an agent; `sources` still gates every verbatim word of the person's conversations). A read episode uses the `attachment [<host>]:` grammar so its spans are `page`, is `processed: true`, and its excerpts are labelled unverified ('as <agent> read it'). Page content is untrusted input, and what Cicada can and cannot check is said once (§8.5, §8.6). **Reworked 2026-09-30 (owner: "limiting the amount of sites makes no sense to me, because we will never know which sites this will happen"; TODO ruling 14 amended):** there is no pre-picked site list. A site is *surfaced* when Cicada's own reader could not read one of its pages (`reading_walls`: a sign-in, a consent wall, a refusal or a host the backend never requests, from stamps the fetchers write at save time, in the in-cycle pass and in the backfill, only while the page holds no words), listed on Settings → Reading the web ("Sites that need your browser") with measured counts and a per-site switch (`reading.agent_sites`, `GET /reading/sites`, `PUT /reading/settings` `sites`); the queue is the person's asks plus the wall pages of allowed sites, derived at read (`reading_queue`), so a switch writes one line and a new page joins with no write. Per-page *Ask an agent* stays and needs no site permission; an agent's `needs_login` pauses a site's derived entries until the row expires or the person turns the site on again. Favicons for the surfaced sites come from the icon service only (the walled site is never contacted). **How your agent reads** (`agent_methods`; G178 generalises it): the person chooses "Let my agent choose", the agent's own tools, or a catalog skill (`browser-harness`, `macos-harness`, pinned and hash-verified, copy-only `agent-prompt` install) — an instruction in the hand-off prompt, the stdio queue reply and one primer line, never to a remote connection — and a chosen skill gets a `type: skill` page tagged `agent-skill` in the memory graph. All reading copy is provider-neutral (owner, 2026-09-30: providers are "literally just providers"; `test_provider_neutral_copy.py`). Terms of the walled platforms forbid automated access even when signed in — the person's account is the one at risk (§5.1, research, not legal advice). **Modes by the owner's word (§8.7):** Fast reader (default) · With an agent · With my browser. **Open questions (spec §14):** D-RW5 ship 'With my browser' at all (recommend yes, last — S4's Route A needs no Cicada code that touches a session — behind an acknowledgement, per-link asks and a per-site permission offered only for a site Cicada's reader could not read); the video spec's Q8 (a logged-in read for non-YouTube videos) is decided here. → extends **G61** (rung 2), **G102**, **G140**, **G105**; the skills half is **G138**; the video seam is **G162**. Built: `cicada_reading_queue` / `cicada_record_read` (stdio and remote: `read` / `record` scope), the ask store outside the bank, `reading.json` settings with the versioned first-use acknowledgement, `page_read.py` (episode with `page` quote spans, a `describes` claim closed on re-read, stamp, thin description), contract item 9 (CONTRACT_VERSION 9, remote 6) and the hook's waiting-links sentence, the `reading` sync component, `GET /sources` `read` block, `read_agent` ledger kind, R-RW4/5/8/9 as TODO ruling 14. **Deviations from the spec, all recorded in ruling 14 or here:** outcomes other than `read` never touch the bank; an ask's URL is visible to `read` scope; public rows (`needs_js`, `robots`) wait for S1; "Ask an agent" in the Reader's Links section waits for S2 (the REST already takes any URL). **The chip label (review 2026-09-29, closed):** a quote Cicada never had is never shown as a bare "From the page". `/episodes/{id}/text` and each provenance conversation row carry the episode's `source` (`page-read`; both ETags gained `src1`), and the claim chip, its hover, the Reader's turn and "Noted from this conversation" say "From the page, as <agent> read it" (`EvidenceLabel.speaker(source:)`). **Ambient "needs login" (review):** the Feed row carries "Needs sign-in" in its second line, and a toast announces a link that just hit a wall (never on the first look after launch or a bank switch); the agent's own `note` shows in the Read section as "<agent> noted: …". The last-read day on Settings → Reading the web is a small file (`reading-last.json`), not a ledger scan. **Not built:** the Reader's Links section, Route B, S1. **Review 2026-09-29:** `cicada_record_read` refuses every outcome for a link with no live ask row (before, a saved link with no ask took any; since 2026-09-30, a wall page of a site the person allowed is recordable too), and a `read` is refused while Sleep runs on stdio, with a reply that says to keep the summary and record it when Cicada has finished (holding a `read` in the ask store to commit after Sleep is open). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · agent reading built; source/video handoffs and Reader extensions open. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G98 | <a id="g98"></a>**Roughly half the inbox is structurally unanswerable — fix the queue before dressing it up** (found 2026-09-01 while diagnosing G97; 41-agent sweep, every claim adversarially re-checked) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** #196 implements served-count parity and read-side filtering. Remaining upstream canonical subject/object generation and retirement of dead-subject questions are not fixed by hiding them at read time. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g98). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** During this review the served-inbox count fix merged as #196 (`3efba432`): shared read-side subject/defer filtering and count parity are implemented. Do not implement those again. Remaining scope is upstream retirement of invalid-subject questions, resolver/object canonicalization and generation-time validation, plus any author-scoped resolution gap confirmed against current code. The multi-valued predicate card rule and G171 decay dedup/cap are already implemented; close this row only when upstream cleanup is covered. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The measured headline: 22 of 49 pending items (45%) have a subject that is missing from the graph or archived, and nothing in the read path checks.** `load_inbox` filters only deferred items (`inbox_service.py:112-130`), the router filters only by kind (`inbox.py:22-26`), and `refresh_open_questions` reasons only over claims (`inbox_questions.py:127-182`). So **the decay engine archived these entities and the inbox kept asking about them** — including 7 archived conflict subjects at near-zero confidence (π0 at 0.14, π0.5 at 0.09, Octo at 0.09, <person-a> at 0.014, heat_parallel.c at 0.0). *This is very likely what the user saw when a conflict about "pi" looked nonsensical.* These items are 74 days old against `inbox_stale_after_days = 90`, so the existing staleness escalation has not reached them yet either. **Fix:** have `refresh_open_questions` close any item whose subject page is absent or `status: archived` (trigger `inbox/subject_archived`), and have `load_inbox` skip them defensively. **Removes 22 items with no UI change.** **Second: predicate multiplicity accounts for most of the conflicts.** 9 of 27 treat `uses` as single-valued when it is a set — a hybrid solver legitimately uses MPI *and* OpenMP, Hono runs on Deno, a project can be Python *and* C++; Cicada's own item offers `awake-sleep-architecture / neo4j / fastapi / react-three-js / leann-vector-index` as if a tech stack were mutually exclusive. **Fix:** mark predicates functional vs set-valued in `predicates.py` and raise conflicts only for functional ones (`works-at`, `role`, `status`); `uses`, `is-a`, `located-in` accumulate. **Third: 6 more conflicts are entity-resolution artifacts** — the two "conflicting" values are the same concept under two names (`vla` vs `vision-language-action-models`, both `type: concept`, both `active`, near-identical summaries; same for `world-models` vs `world-models-for-robotics`). One merge silently kills six questions — so **run merge before conflict in the Sleep pipeline**, and normalize the OBJECT side of a claim through the entity resolver before comparing. **Fourth, and it is the most embarrassing single item:** `inbox-1446` asks where **the user himself** works (<company-a> vs <company-b> vs <company-c> (real values redacted)) but `rodrigo-sagastegui` has no entity page — the user is split across `rodrigo.md` and `rodrigo-jesus-sagastegui.md`. Resolving it would write to nothing. Cause: `entity_id` is minted as `sanitize_id(entity_name)` with **no existence check** (`clarification_manager.py:94`), even though `id_utils.resolve_entity_file` is already used a few lines later for merge targets. Refuse to write an item whose subject does not resolve. **Context:** all 49 were written in one bulk backfill on 2026-06-18 over an import of 1,197 episodes of which only 3 are `mcp` captures — 787 are bookmarks and 193 apple-notes — so the extractor was largely reasoning over third-party web content rather than the user's life. That is a one-off condition, but it is why the queue reads the way it does. **Two upstream gates remove ~26 of 49 without touching a pixel**, which is why this row outranks G97: 49 well-rendered unanswerable questions is still an inbox nobody opens. → fixes the input side of **G60** (question objects), unblocks **G97**, all deterministic (**G80**); relates **G65(a)** (slug collisions) and **G21** (dedup sweep). **2026-09-03 evidence:** a project's `uses` predicate rendered a 7-option conflict card (architecture concept vs database vs three frameworks) — multi-valued predicates must never open a conflict; see G115 for the card-side rule. **Card-side rule shipped 2026-09-03 (G115 Phase 1):** `predicates.cardinality` (bank map ∪ committed seed, `multi` winning — `install_predicate_map` never refreshes a populated map, so a bank seeded before commit `e9a7c6b` still calls `uses` single-valued; anything in neither list is `unknown` and still asked), `write_claim_nudges` skips `multi` (counted), `load_inbox` serves an existing multi-valued conflict as `informational` (list + `Got it`, no claim touched). The subject-gone gate and the entity-resolution half are unchanged and still open here. **2026-09-29:** the decay half of the pile-up is closed by **G171** (PR #141): one open decay question per entity, at most 10 new per cycle, the overflow counted and raised next cycle. **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · inbox freshness; local changes need validation. | PARTIAL · P1 **Status 2026-10-07 (trial):** the first real batch raised 24 'Confirm a predicate fold' items, all pure slug formatting (~3,500 projected for the drain). Core fix shipped #211 (a label's own slug is not a fold). Per-pair dedup, remembered answers and the clean-up migration are on `fix/g98-no-slug-fold-questions` (review round 1: coverage, locking, migration admission). Also observed: extraction emits sentence-long predicates — prompt guidance to review. |
| G118 | <a id="g118"></a>**Full provenance and traceability of memory — every belief shows the raw span, the contributor's reasoning, and the prompt that caused the write (a better NotebookLM)** (Rodrigo 2026-09-02: "full provenance and traceability of memory, even for the UI where I can see a raw reference with the whole page or the highlighted part that is referenced… highlights of where the contributor got the information or what different parts of conversation (can also reference own reasoning) helped consolidate something in memory… also the user's prompts that made the model act like this, like traces. Like an even better version of how NotebookLM references something from the material you give it" — marked by the owner as central to the vision) | **Section-level provenance (owner 2026-10-07).** "for me provenance is a massive edge … even through the app i'd like to see what are all the conversations and chunks that contributed to certain parts of the memory entity's page." Today only claims carry spans; a page's synthesized prose knows its contributing conversations only page-wide (`source_episodes`, `GET /entities/{id}/provenance`), and claims written before this row's spans have none (no backfill). Next slice to plan: when Sleep writes or rewrites a section, record the evidence spans it drew on per section (spans, not copies), so the app's entity card shows, per section, the conversations and chunks behind it and opens each in the Reader at the passage. The qmd research (2026-10-07) is checked for chunk-retrieval ideas here. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Six evidence kinds, exact offsets/hash and Reader citations exist. Trigger/prompt traces and citable contributor rationales are not implemented; validate video timestamp seeking and history/span navigation separately. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g118). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists today, and where it stops:** a belief carries `source_episodes` (which conversation) and a commit carries `Cicada-Author:` / `Cicada-Session:` (which model, which conversation) — file-level and conversation-level provenance. It does not say *which sentence* (G100, unbuilt), *why the contributor concluded it* (Stage-1 output has no `evidence`/`reasoning` field that survives into the claim), or *what the person asked for* that led an agent to write (an MCP `cicada_write_claim` / `cicada_save_episode` call records the claim, not the turn that triggered it). `GET /entities/{id}/history` answers "when and by whom", never "from what". **The bar:** NotebookLM shows the cited passage inline; Cicada must do that *and* keep it bi-temporal (the span as it was when the belief formed), attributed (model, session, observer), and two-directional (from a span: everything it convinced; from a belief: every span, across conversations, links and notes). **Design — four layers, each additive to the existing files:** (1) **Spans, not copies.** A claim gains `evidence: [{episode, start, end, kind: user\|assistant\|reasoning\|page}]` — byte offsets into the stored episode/page text, never a quoted copy (privacy: the bank already holds the text; the ledger stays ids-only). Stage-1 extraction returns the exact substring it relied on; the resolver validates it exists in the episode and stores offsets (an unverifiable span is recorded as `kind: reasoning` with no offsets, never faked). Absorbs **G100**. (2) **Reasoning as a citable source.** The contributor's own justification (the model's `why` for a merge, a conflict pick, a decay archive, a skill) is stored as a short `rationale` on the claim/nudge with the model id — the model citing itself is a first-class evidence kind, shown distinctly from what the person said. (3) **Trigger traces.** Every agent write records the *turn that caused it*: for MCP writes, the calling tool's `context` (the user's last message excerpt as a span in the episode the MCP seam already captures — never read from `~/.claude` transcripts, G48 rail) and the tool name; for Sleep writes, the cycle id and stage. Surfaced as `trigger: {kind: user_prompt\|agent_decision\|sleep_stage, span?, tool?, cycle?}`. Consumes **G113**'s ledger shape (ids/enums) and **G105** (deterministic capture is what makes 'the turn that caused it' always exist). (4) **The viewer.** Entity page → claim → evidence chips → a raw-source pane that opens the whole episode/page with the cited spans highlighted and the rest dimmed, scrollable, with the other beliefs that cite the same passage listed alongside; from `cicada_ask` answers, every citation is a span, not a page (**G93**'s output half); from an inbox card, the cause span (**G97**/**G115**). Keyboard: ⌘[ back to the belief. **Rails:** engine-free read path (offsets → slices, ≤ a few ms); ETag ship-together (episodes join the entity/inbox ETags where the span text is served); transcripts under `~/.claude` are never read — if the MCP seam did not capture the turn, the trace says so; spans go stale when an episode is rewritten — store the episode's content hash beside the offsets and mark `stale` on mismatch rather than mis-highlight; nothing here is auto-applied. **Ordering:** span capture at extraction (Stage-1 + resolver) first, because every later layer reads it; then the viewer; then triggers; then reasoning. → absorbs **G100**; consumes **G105**, **G113**, **G48**; feeds **G93** (cited answers), **G97**/**G115** (cause on cards), **G106** (conversation ↔ entity browser is the same pane), **G101** (the evidence layer is what makes raw conversations worth keeping), **G112** (a compiled skill's `## Evidence` becomes real citations). APPLY, L (four slices, each shippable). **Slice 1 shipped 2026-09-03 (PR #44, `feat/provenance-spans`):** `Claim.evidence: [{episode, start, end, kind, hash}]` (offsets into the parsed body, ```claims fence excluded for a page; `sha256[:12]` → `stale` on mismatch; kinds `user\|assistant\|page\|reasoning`, speaker by the last `<role>:` marker, no marker = user); one engine-free module `api/services/evidence.py` (exact → whitespace → case-insensitive, never fuzzy; unlocatable = `reasoning`, claim still written); Stage-1 `evidence_quote` verified in `extract` against the chunked body with the chunk as window; `cicada_write_claim`/`write_claim` take `evidence: [{episode, quote}]`; link recon `page` spans on the media entity; Telegram `saved-because` cites its section; `_reinforce` merges spans; `evidence` on `/claims`, `/timeline`, `/transclude`; `GET /episodes/{id}/span`. Legacy claims: no evidence, no backfill, `to_dict` omits the empty key. **Slice 2, server half, built 2026-09-23 (`feat/provenance-viewer`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-23-provenance-backend.md`):** one freshness rule, `evidence.span_status` → `current \| grown \| stale` — design amendment A7: a turn-boundary prefix of an appended episode that still hashes to the stored value is `grown`, exact, and highlights (the Stop hook and G20 both rewrite by appending; pages never grow) — used by `/span` (+`grown`), the inbox cause and every new payload, and a stale span travels without wash offsets; `GET /episodes/{id}/text` (the whole evidence text ≤ 400,000 chars, `turns[]` from the one marker parser `speaker_kind` reads, per-turn `ts` only from a stored sidecar, an asserted `start/end/hash` or derived `focus=<entity>`); `GET /entities/{id}/provenance` (contributors = claim `authored_by` + one trailer-only `git log` of the page, conversations grouped by `session_id`/`source_id`, best quote asserted → derived → stale, coverage over current claims stated honestly); `GET /episodes/{id}/citations` (raw-text prefilter over `entities/`, spans + derived + reasoning rows, capped with `partial`); `/ask` citations carry `claimId` + `evidence`; `ClaimModel`/`EntityHistoryEntry` gain `authorKind`/`authorProvider` (+ `sessionIds`, `recordedAt`). The chat importer keeps each message's time as `turns: [{offset, ts, speaker}]` outside `content_hash`, capped at 500 because frontmatter is parsed on every cold scan (costs measured in the plan); `markdown_parser.parse` reads with libyaml's `CSafeLoader` when present, because the sidecar made the pure-Python scanner ~7x slower on every full episode scan (2,000 imports: `list_all_episodes` 2.88 s → 0.42 s, the Stop hook's first-capture scan back under its 3 s timeout). G100's derived-span class exists on read payloads only — never in `EVIDENCE_KINDS`, never written. None of the payloads is a Store domain (ETags for the client's in-memory cache only; no `VersionVector` mapping). **Slice 2, the app, built 2026-09-23 (`feat/provenance-ui`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-23-provenance-ui.md`):** the app decodes `evidence` (dropped since slice 1 — `Claim.CodingKeys` never named it) and every slice-2 payload tolerantly, and slices every offset as a Unicode scalar through one `ScalarText`. Every claim carries **evidence chips** — "You said", "<agent> replied", "From the page", "Inferred", or "Mentioned here" for a legacy claim's name match — whose hover preview shows the words in the quote face, washed when quoted ("Quoted by the contributor"), bold when derived ("Found by searching the conversation"), plain when stale; a click opens the **Reader**, a trailing inspector beside whatever is open, with the conversation as turns (speaker labels, a time only where one is stored), scrolled to the washed span, stale/grown/derived/inferred/truncated said in words, the capture-honesty line, Resume, a "cited here" navigator and "Noted from this conversation" (the inverse index, each belief jumping to its words or landing on the graph). The entity card gains **"Where this came from"** — the writers with their real marks and two nouns (beliefs, edits), the conversations that fed it with the best sentence of each, and coverage stated as "N of M beliefs here have an exact quote"; G61's "Sources" became "Look it up at"; the claim footer and the History tab draw `ContributorAvatar` with plain trust words; the inbox cause gains its harness mark and "Show in conversation"; Ask answers carry evidence chips. Nothing is written, cached on disk, or made a Store domain. **Open:** P6 (palette rows → the Reader, Track S), find-in-conversation (Track S's search field), an episode id on `ConversationSummary` (so "Open conversation" works outside an entity card), a `span` read-telemetry surface, a speaker name on `/span` for meeting chips, a remote write's app label bucketed as `harness` by `author_identity` (done, F2-back R-B9), seeking a video from a `media` chip, per-turn times for Stop-hook episodes (their `turns` key is a count today), slice 3 trigger traces, slice 4 rationale, `describes` claims on link enrichment. **G140 (2026-09-23):** a sixth kind, `media` (after G134's fifth, `speaker`) — what a video said, a `video [m:ss]:` line in a watch episode (the time is required, so a person's own "Video: …" line stays theirs); `speaker_kind`, `media_time` and `turns()` read one marker grammar (`evidence._marker`, which since the merge with G134 also reads `speaker:<label>:` lines, and an episode's `evidence_kind` override still wins first), and the position is derived at read (`evidence.media_time`, served as `t` on `/episodes/{id}/span` and each turn), never stored. An agent's note on a saved link is written under `assistant:` so it never reads as the person's. **DS-2 (2026-09-24):** the Reader is a column; asserted spans wash, found mentions are semibold, in the Inbox's quote too. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ slice 1 ✅ (PR #44); slice 2 ✅ (server PR #72, app PR #78); slices 3–4 open. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G147 | <a id="g147"></a>**Frequency-aware decay (spacing) + per-type tuning** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24, on the proposed fix: "ok i like the fix you propose so please go ahead and work on that") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Mention-week spacing, per-type tuning and visible effective pace exist. Entity merge omits the loser's kept_on list, losing feedback; preserve it with tests. Reinforcement increments remain G85, not part of this fix. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g147). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The defect (verified 2026-09-24):** decay was a flat per-class weekly rate from the last reference (`conflict_resolver.py:168-196` at `ecb59c7`) — a page mentioned in 50 separate conversations faded exactly as fast as one mentioned twice, while CLAUDE.md claimed decay was "proportional to how frequently it used to be referenced" (it read nothing about frequency) and that archived pages move to `archive/` (they keep their file, `status: archived`). **The rule now:** weekly rate = base (class rate or explicit `decay_rate:`) × f(w) × the bank's per-type pace, `w` = distinct ISO weeks among `source_episodes` dates ∪ the person's *keep* days (`kept_on`; every unparseable id together counts once), f(w) = max(0.25, 1/(1+0.6·ln w)). Weeks of silence to the decay question / to archive from 0.6: active w=1 4/8, w=4 7.3/14.7, w=12 10/20, w=52 13.5/27; durable w=1 10/20 → w=52 33.7/67.4; volatile w=1 1.3/2.7 → w=52 4.5/9.0. α=1.0 was rejected (floor at ~20 weeks: five months and five years look alike), α=0.3 too (a 52-week page asked after 8.7 weeks, barely twice a one-week page). **Keep** records the day in `kept_on` (not `source_episodes`, whose entries every reader treats as episode ids; not git-derived, which would put a subprocess in every pass). **Claims** multiply their existing amount by f(w_claim), weeks from `source_episodes` ∪ cited `ep_*` evidence documents ∪ the subject's keeps — session ids carry no date, `recorded_at` moves on every restatement, so neither counts; no new claim field. **Per-type pace** (agent proposes, person disposes): `GET /memory/decay-suggestions` counts, per type, each page's latest decay answer in the last 180 days of the bank's own git history (never the telemetry ledger) and suggests 0.5 ("fade more slowly") at ≥ 5 answers and ≥ 80 % keep, 1.5 at ≥ 80 % archive; `PUT /memory/decay-tuning` stores `<bank>/_decay_tuning.yaml` (travels with the bank, committed alone as `Cicada-Author: user`, 409 while Sleep runs); both engines multiply by it. Settings → Memory shows "Let people fade more slowly?" with Apply · Not now (per viewer, back after 5 more answers) and each chosen pace with Reset. **Visibility:** `GET /entities/{id}` gains a derived `decay: {class, effectiveRatePerWeek, mentionWeeks}` from the pass's own function (`decay_policy.effective`), and the card's Details "Fades" row reads "Slowly — mentioned across 12 weeks". Unchanged: the one-week cap per cycle, the `decayed_through` watermark, the 0.2/0.4 thresholds, `RECOVERY_CONFIDENCE`, G85's `cicada`-authored decay commit; nothing stored is migrated. **Disclosed, not fixed:** a merge (`entity_merge._LIST_FIELDS`) unions the absorbed page's `source_episodes` but not its `kept_on`. **Not here:** ranking recall by confidence (nothing ranks by it today; a question for G148's benchmark pass to measure before anything changes); reinforcement as an increment (G85 (4)); the Feed's media relevance (media is evergreen). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-09-24-r4-frequency-aware-decay.md` (rulings R-FD1 … R-FD13). → answers **G85** (3), and corrects **G120**'s premise; builds on **G66**, **G113**; respects **G78**. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ built on `feat/r4-decay` (round 4). | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G93 | <a id="g93"></a>**Ask/search is built — the open question is what "smart" adds, and whether any of it is chargeable** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a smart search inside cicada or ask ai inside cicada might be useful and maybe even part of a paid plan (add idea to backlog for later discussion)") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Grounded Ask, lexical/vector/claim fusion and name-triggered hook recall exist. Cross-stream curated relevance and right-moment usefulness remain unproved; drive changes from the hands-on trial. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g93). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Already shipped:** `GET /search` (cross-graph entities + episodes) and `POST /ask` (grounded NL answer with wikilink citations and gap analysis) are both mounted (`api/main.py:154-155`), and the app has a ⌘K Ask panel (G52). So this row is not "build search" — it is **what would make it smart**, and that has a precise answer from **G79**: today Ask retrieves *within* whatever the question names; the north star is **cross-stream retrieval** — surfacing a curated artifact inside an experiential context the user never explicitly linked ("you saved this three months ago and it answers the thing you're deciding now"). That is the capability worth building and the only one that is genuinely hard. Concrete gaps under it: no temporal scoping ("what was I thinking about in March"), no saved/standing queries, no proactive surfacing (Ask is strictly pull — the north star's "smartly gives" half is unbuilt), and Ask is a single-shot answer rather than a conversation over memory. **On the paid-plan idea — the honest complication, worth deciding before designing around it:** if **G74(a)**'s BYO-compute model holds, the user's *own* Claude subscription pays for the inference behind every Ask. Charging for the query while they fund the tokens is a hard sell and invites the obvious "why not just ask Claude directly". The defensible monetization is the thing the user cannot trivially self-host: **hosted E2E sync, a mobile client, continuity across devices, and a managed engine for people with no Claude/Codex subscription at all** — i.e. G92 route (b). Park the pricing question there rather than attaching it to Ask. → extends **G52** (Ask panel), **A5** (gap analysis), **D3** ("answers not pages", already decided); depends on **G79** for what "smart" means and **G92** for whether anything is charged. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ search/ask half already shipped (`GET /search` + `POST /ask` mounted `api/main.py:154-155`; ⌘K Ask panel, G52); the cross-stream-retrieval "smart" half is open — see TODO Wave C #13. G136 (2026-09-23) made ⌘K a find palette with Ask as a mode (⌘⏎); what "smart" adds — cross-stream retrieval — stays this row's.. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G103 | <a id="g103"></a>**Surface the observer model in the UI — whose belief is this, and who was in the room** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a small detail in the UI that makes reference to the model of entity observer relations (like who was in the conversation, who's belief, etc). this goes in hand with the import of conversations") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Claim/evidence observer fields and some labels exist. Finish meaningful option-level observer labels and meeting participants; this affects current trustworthy answers as well as future sharing. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g103). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** The data model already supports this and the UI barely admits it. Every claim carries `observer` — `agent \| rodrigo \| external:<name>` (`claims.py:68`) — the graph aggregates per-node observer sets (`graph_builder.py:115-122`), and the decay engine already weights trust per observer. But the only surface is the **All / Cicada / You / External** segmented control, which **G84(d)** found filters on claim observer, dims rather than filters, and returns true unconditionally for the 56% of nodes carrying no claims — so three of its four segments look identical and it communicates nothing. **What to build:** (i) on an **entity card**, show per-claim attribution inline — "you said this", "Cicada inferred this from a conversation", "heard from <person>" — so a page stops reading as one undifferentiated set of facts; (ii) on a **conversation** (Activity ▸ Conversations, and the import flow), show **who was present** and let a belief be traced to the participant who asserted it; (iii) relabel the observer control to what it means (G84(d) already proposes `Everything · Inferred by Cicada · Stated by you · Heard from others`) and gate segments on non-empty membership. **Why it pairs with conversation import, as the user says:** an imported agent chat has exactly one human observer, so today's model is adequate and invisible; the moment **G95** lands and a *meeting* is imported, a single episode carries several humans, and "whose belief is this" stops being cosmetic and becomes the difference between recording what *you* think and what someone told you. Building the surface now — while the answer is simple — means the multi-party case has somewhere to render rather than forcing a UI scramble later. **Dependency:** identity is only meaningful once speakers resolve to real people (**G81** contacts), otherwise every meeting mints a fresh half-identified `external:` observer. → extends **G84(d)** (the observer control), the claim layer's existing `observer`/`source_trust`; pairs with **G95** (meetings), depends on **G81** (contacts) for names that mean anything; supports **G100** (a citation is stronger when it names who said it). **(iv) First surface (G115 Phase 2):** each inbox option carries `observer` / `authored_by` from its claim, rendered through one `observer_label(observer, owner)` — `you said` / `Cicada inferred (<model>)` / `heard from <name>` — and the owner literal comes from a new `observer_owner` setting (default `"user"`) replacing the hardcoded string at `inbox_service.py:813`; a conflict card then shows the two disagreeing sentences side by side with who said each. **G118 slice 2 (server) makes "who wrote this" legible:** `GET /entities/{id}/provenance` returns per-author claim and commit counts with `kind`/`provider` from one `git_service.author_identity`, and `ClaimModel` carries `authorKind`/`authorProvider`, so the card and the chip render a contributor without re-deriving it; per-claim observer labels and "who was in the room" stay this row's. **G118 slice 2 (app) renders who wrote what:** every claim's footer names its writer with the contributors strip's own face (`ContributorAvatar` — a provider's real mark, "You", or Cicada's bookworm) and a plain trust word ("You told Cicada", "Cicada noticed", "Cicada concluded", "From a source"); every evidence chip says who spoke — "You said", "<agent> replied", and "Someone else said" for a meeting speaker, never "you" (R-N2); the entity card's "Where this came from" lists the writers with belief and edit counts. The inbox option's observer label and a meeting's "who was in the room" stay this row's. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G53 | <a id="g53"></a>**Live state dictionary — the context passport as a runtime object** (Rodrigo agreed 2026-08-29) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** The state dictionary and bounded handshake are implemented. Test passport freshness and attribution in fresh sessions under G10; do not duplicate _state.md into a second authoritative MEMORY.md. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g53). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Cicada has raw episodes and consolidated entities but no small, documented **live** context object. MHS's state dictionary — "each device described and onboarded once, its variables, controls and sensor values recorded in a single dictionary in shared memory, in a documented format", which *fresh* agent instances read to coordinate — is the missing awake-state layer. Build `memory/_state.md` (YAML frontmatter + short body, regenerated deterministically — zero LLM — by structural Sleep and on every relevant write): active bank; connections + engine in use (G50); current/active projects (top-N by recency/confidence) with their repos' live branch state (repo-link layer); open tasks/ideas (G13) and pending inbox count; recent conversations with resume handles (G48); last Sleep + queue depth; the user's standing preferences (skills). Documented schema so *any* harness reads it in one file at session start: the G49 SessionStart primer hook injects it; `cicada_recall` returns it in `cicada-hints`; Codex/other harnesses read it via AGENTS.md pointer. Not a cache of entity pages — a **cursor into them** (ids + one-liners + wikilinks), so it stays small. → extends `_index.md` (cold-start MoC) with a *now* view; relates to G48, G49 (primer hook), G13, G50, context-passport roadmap. **Shipped 2026-09-03 (PR #45, `feat/state-handshake`):** `api/services/state_dictionary.py` writes `<bank>/_state.md` (schema v1: engine, sleep, inbox counts, top-N projects with bounded live repo state, people, conversations sans `resumable`, skill one-liners, the G121 sentence; ≤ 6 KB, `inputs_version`-debounced, write-if-changed); regenerated by the Sleep tail (own `cicada` commit, trigger `sleep/state`, `_finalize` split), lazily by `GET /state`, best-effort after an inbox resolution; `GET /state?refresh=true`. **Final review (2026-09-03):** every regeneration commits itself through one `refresh_and_commit` helper — a read-side write left dirty was reproduced riding in the next `git add -A` writer's `Cicada-Author: user` commit; `sleep.next_at` left the file (it advanced daily under a live schedule and made every idle night commit, against R1) and is added per request by `GET /state` on the local clock. OPEN: G13 open tasks/ideas are not a section yet (no producer); the app's Store does not fetch `/state`. **G140 (2026-09-23): schema v2.** The now-view reads the G66 classes — `standing` (durable/evergreen non-skill pages), `focus` (active/volatile pages referenced in the last 14 days), `preferences` re-ranked by confidence alone with working-agreement tags first — and the owner comes from `owner_identity.resolve_observer` with an `owner_one_liner` (the builder used to read only the env override). `inputs_version` folds in the schema, so an upgraded backend rebuilds once. Trim order: people → focus → standing → conversations → preferences → projects. The timezone is never written here (it is per request, in the handshake). **Previous execution label (historical):** ✅. | VERIFY · P1 |
| G113 | <a id="g113"></a>**Grounded reward — record every human verdict on memory so extraction quality becomes a number** (Silver & Sutton, *Welcome to the Era of Experience*: a reward must be "grounded" in the environment's reply rather than in a designer's prior judgement; Rodrigo 2026-09-01 shared the paper as Cicada's framing) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Human verdict ledger/reporting and decay-tuning suggestions exist. Feedback-conditioned extraction/prompt changes and broader learning slices remain; nothing auto-applies learned settings. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g113). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The finding (verified 2026-09-01):** the user grades the extractor dozens of times — every decay keep/archive, conflict pick/both/neither, clarification answer, merge accept/reject — and NOTHING records the verdict as a signal. `inbox_service.resolve` commits `trigger: inbox/{kind}/resolved` whatever the action (:295-297) — keep and archive produce byte-identical manifest lines; the chosen option survives only as a diffable inbox-file deletion. No telemetry event is written on any resolution (`telemetry.py:21` KINDS has no resolution kind; `inbox_service.py` never imports it; `kind="ask"`/`"import"` are declared and never emitted). `GET /consumption/*` and `GET /contributors` count cost and volume, never agreement (`consumption_stats.py:91-252`; `git_service.py:645-745`). Extractor confidence (`entity_extractor.py:28-47`; relationship default 0.6 :514; "<0.5" gates clarification :106) is never compared with an outcome. `claim_reconciler._reinforce` (:143-162) bumps confidence with no count, so `session_ids` (`claims.py:97-106`) is the only precision proxy. Stage-3's `audit` list — the one machine record of "an agent claim was overruled by the trust table" — is returned by `claim_pipeline` (:159) and read by nobody (`grep audit sleep_cycle.py` → 0). **Four verdicts are actively lost:** (a) the `divergence` ("Keep my statement / Update to X") and `normalization` ("Correct fold / Wrong fold") items Stage 5.56 writes (`inbox_generator.py:333-338`) are NOT in `InboxKind` (`schemas.py:765-769`) — `InboxItem(kind='divergence')` raises ValidationError (verified), `load_inbox` skips them as unparseable (:169-172), resolve would 400 (:286-287) — so the two questions that most directly grade the extractor accumulate on disk unanswerable; (b) merge REJECT has no action — the app sends `dismiss`, which deletes the item (`inbox_service.py:712-713`; `InboxCardView.swift:177`), and `clarification_manager.find_open` (:73-83) re-proposes the same pair next cycle; (c) decay `keep_active` never touches the claim the nudge came from (`claim_id` written at `inbox_generator.py:365`, never read in `_resolve_decay` :331-368) and `remind_later` writes a `snooze_until` nothing reads (:354-355) while still committing "entity updated"; (d) a clarification answer writes body prose only (:667-710) — no `user_stated` claim, so it earns none of the human protection a conflict "neither" earns (:549-574). Also: `dropped` status is read in five places and set by none outside a test fixture; an out-of-band editor correction is swept into the next Sleep commit under `sleep/extraction` with the MODEL's author trailer (`sleep_cycle.py:1462-1479,:1553-1569`) — the `user/manual_edit` trigger in CLAUDE.md is emitted by no production code; `dedup_sweep` verdicts (:82-99) are returned, never persisted, and re-judged (paid) every sweep. **Why this matters beyond bookkeeping:** the paper's argument is that an agent cannot improve past its designer's priors without a reward grounded in consequences. Cicada already HAS the grounded signal — the user's verdicts are the environment's reply — and throws it away, which is why G78 calls measurement "the weakest link" and why G98's 22/49-dead-subjects number came from a manual sweep. **Design — a feedback ledger, $0.** (1) **Carry the verdict in the commit:** trigger `inbox/{kind}/resolved:{action}` (`keep_active`, `archive`, `pick:<key>`, `both`, `neither`, `answer`, `merge`, `reject`, `defer`) — inert to entity-line parsing like every trailer; `_infer_change_type` learns `archived`. (2) **Emit a telemetry event** `kind="resolution"` with refs `{item_id, kind, predicate, entity_id, action, option_key, winner_claim_id, loser_claim_ids, extractor_confidence, extractor_model, item_age_days}` from `inbox_service.resolve` (the MCP `cicada_resolve_inbox` path only POSTs, so one writer covers both); plus `kind="audit"` for Stage-3's `audit` list and `kind="dedup_verdict"` for `dedup_sweep`. (3) **Make the four lost verdicts real:** add `divergence` + `normalization` to `InboxKind` with resolve branches (keep-mine / update / both; correct / wrong-fold → unfold + record the pair); a `reject` action on merge that writes the pair to `_merge_rejected.yaml`, consulted by `find_open` and `dedup_sweep` before proposing; decay keep restores the source claim's confidence; clarification answers write a `user_stated` claim (`origin: clarification`) beside the prose. (4) **Read it back:** `GET /consumption/feedback` — per-model / per-predicate / per-kind agreement rates (keep vs archive, pick-agent vs neither, correct vs wrong fold), a calibration curve (extractor-confidence bucket → user-agreement rate), and a corrections count from `Cicada-Author: user` commits — rendered as a fourth card on Activity. (5) **Close the loop later (💸, DECIDE):** feeding rates back into prompts or thresholds is G78's territory and needs the ledger first; the ledger is the prerequisite, not the optimizer. **Rails:** the ledger lives in `~/.cicada/telemetry/` like every other event — machine-global, never in the bank, ids and enums only, no personal text; a verdict is recorded, never auto-applied to future extraction without the user seeing the rate. → the measurement half of **G78** (gbrain-evals) and a live number for **G98**; extends **G51** (ledger + `/consumption`), **G60** (claim-aware resolve), **G85** (author-attribution correctness is the precondition); consumes **G97** (context on the question side); relates **G103** (observer in the UI), **G100** (span citation as the thing a user confirms or denies), **G72**/**G112** (a compiled skill's `## Evidence` is only trustworthy once verdicts are counted). **(2b, decided in G115, 2026-09-02; slices 1–2 merged as PR #31 at `09a4b66`, `_verdict` at `inbox_service.py:302`, `remind_later → _defer(7)` still Task 5):** the wire carries the verdict before the answer — each option gets `recommended` (the one option `_verdict` scores `agreed`: Sleep's proposal, never freshness, so a stale-escalated `neither` and a no-claim entity-path conflict never carry it) and `verdict`, computed once from R3 so a test asserts wire == ledger for every kind × key; `merge_suggestion` never carries `recommended` (G81); the resolve response echoes `{changed: {closed, kept, created, archived}, verdict, extractor_confidence}` and both surfaces show only `changed`; the shipped `resolution` event gains additive refs `surface (app\|mcp\|telegram)`, `harness`, `session_id`, `recommended_key`, `picked_recommended`, `cause_tier`, `silent_days`, `defer_count`, `via_conversation_match`, `rule_applied`, and a new `ask` kind (ids only) records each item an agent surfaced so asked-vs-answered is a number; `session_id` lives in the ledger, not in a `Cicada-Session:` trailer on the user commit (contract unchanged pending G116). **Shipped 2026-09-05 (PR #59, `feat/feedback-ledger`) — slices 3–7:** trigger `inbox/<kind>/resolved:<label>` + `statusChange` for decay (slices 1–2, already covered above); `divergence` + `normalization` resolvable in API and Swift; merge reject persisted in `_merge_rejected.yaml`; decay `keep_active` and clarification answers reach the claim layer; `GET /consumption/feedback` + a Feedback tile in Sources ▸ Advanced. Still open: slice 5 (rates → prompts, 💸 DECIDE under G78) and the 2b additive refs above (G115 Phase 2/3). **2026-09-23 (G61 phase 2):** two feedback kinds join the ledger — `check` (every source check: checker kind, method, outcome, grade, ids and enums only, a regex-guarded no-URL rule) and `check_verdict` (the person's reply to a check: agreed/overruled in shadow, confirmed/undone after an auto settle, silent expiry graded as neither) — and `resolution` gains additive `checked_key`/`picked_checked`; a `settled:` or `check:` trigger is never counted as the person's verdict, and `_verdict` stays byte-identical. **First consumer (G147, 2026-09-24):** decay answers in the bank's own history (`inbox/decay/resolved:<label>`) become per-type pace *suggestions* in Settings → Memory — applied only when the person says so. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G115 | <a id="g115"></a>**Inbox redesign — one question object, cause on the card, Sleep's proposal marked, a scoped "don't ask again", and an in-conversation ask gate** (decision memo 2026-09-02, four-design study judged on three lenses — UI-port spine 115 / stream-first 114 / conversation-first 109 / reward-first 109; a four-point spread is a mandate for a spine and grafts, not for one design; critic pass applied, base `dev @ 09a4b66`; owner's complaints in G97/G98/G100/G103/G113) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Question cards, cause display, resolutions and defer flows exist. Verify remaining interaction slices and implement only the scoped rule/ask-gate work allowed by G116; no silent automatic belief settlement. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g115). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Problem.** The inbox is a priority-sorted list of cards that hide their cause (G97), never say what Sleep is betting on, grade the person forever one item at a time with no way to write a ruling once, reach an agent only through a body-text substring match that returns everything (`mcp/server.py::_topic_matches`, no G98 subject gate, no kind check, a hand-rolled frontmatter parser at `:738` that disagrees with the API about which files exist), and make decay a three-button special case outside the one component G60 built (`InboxCardView.swift:162-164` short-circuits `actionRow` on `.decay`; `_resolve_decay`'s else-branch unlinks the file). **Evidence (verified).** G98: 45% of items unanswerable; G105: 0 MCP invocations in 12 days, so "agent-initiated" is theoretical; `InboxItem` (`schemas.py:795-819`) carries no `source_episode`, `entity_type` or recommendation; **the ETag trap has two halves** — `api/routers/inbox.py` must widen `etag_for(mp, "inbox", "entities", "episodes")` AND `VersionVector.swift:8-9` maps `entities` → `[.graph,.contributors,.origins]` and `episodes` → `[.status,.origins,.sources,.channels]`, neither refreshing `.inbox`, so `.inbox` must be added to both mappings in the same commit or `SnapshotCache` keeps stale context until the inbox itself moves; `graph_builder._dir_mtime` (`:536-544`) globs `*.md` + dir mtime, so a YAML rule file under `inbox/` would tick nothing; a per-cycle write cap is a silent drop for Stage-2 clarifications (`entity_resolver.pending_actions` on an episode then marked processed) — so **no cap**. **Design (the ruling).** (1) One question object for every kind — `decay`/`divergence`/`normalization` synthesised at read in `_item_from_file` (decay: `Still tracking {name}?`, options `archive`/`keep`, `allow_defer=True`), no file rewrite; clarification `title` becomes the question (`Who is {name}?`). (2) **Item shape, all additive** — `InboxOption += {recommended, verdict, action; Phase 2: observer, authored_by, confidence, episode_id, excerpt, preview}`; `InboxItem += {entity_type, source_episode, source_episode_timestamp, cause: {episode_id, timestamp, conversation_id, harness, origin, conversation_title, excerpt, mention_offsets, tier: item\|claim\|entity, span_kind: derived}, silent_days, extractor_confidence, extractor_model, recommended_key, group_id, defer_count; Phase 2: neighbours, rule_offers}`; `InboxResolveRequest += {surface: app\|mcp\|telegram, harness, session_id (ledger refs only), note, rule}`; response echoes `{changed: {closed, kept, created, archived}, verdict, extractor_confidence}` and both surfaces render only `changed`. `age_days`, `silent_days`, `cause.excerpt`, `mention_offsets`, `recommended`, `verdict` are derived at read, never stored. (3) `cause` resolves by G97's three tiers (item → freshest option claim → subject `source_episodes`), `[ no source recorded ]` served when all fail, never a hidden card; a `bank_index.parse_count` budget test guards the ~100 ms figure. (4) **`recommended` == the one option the shipped `_verdict` (`inbox_service.py:302`, R3) scores `agreed` — Sleep's proposal, never freshness** — computed once so a test asserts wire == ledger for every kind × key; `verdict` is on the wire for agents and tests and never rendered as copy. Corollaries (critic C1/C0): on a stale-escalated conflict `Neither anymore` is `overruled` under R3, so it is NEVER marked — either nothing is marked or Sleep's own claim stays marked and freshness orders the rest; an entity-path conflict with no item `claim_id` grades every pick `neutral` (the Task-2 footnote) and therefore never carries a `(Recommended)` — a large share of live conflicts per G98. **Never on `merge_suggestion`, no initial highlight, never in a bundle (G81)**; `reject` persists to `_merge_rejected.yaml` (R5). (5) `<memory>/_inbox_rules.yaml` at bank root beside `_merge_rejected.yaml`, permission-rule grammar `kind(scope[, scope]): decision`, scope values enums/slugs only (parser rejects whitespace, so a rule string is ledger-safe), precedence `never > defer > action > ask`; Phase 2 ships `never` + `defer:N` + `decay(entity=X): never` → the existing evergreen `PUT`; merge rules are `never` only; a `rules` sync component (`file_mtime`) mapped to `[.inbox]`; rule EXECUTION (`archive\|keep\|accept`) waits on **G116(a)**. A `never` rule is NOT the tool for G98's entity-resolution artifacts (~15 of 27 conflicts) — that half stays G98's Stage-2 fix and must not be `never`-ruled away. (6) `cicada_check_nudges(mode="ask"\|"list", entity_ids, topic, limit=3, kinds, conversation_id, include_context)` gated server-side by a pure `inbox_ask_gate.candidates(...)`: `entity_id ∈ entity_ids` OR a top-5 `search_entities` hit with score ≥ 0.55 (`vector_index.py:295`, `1.0 − distance`) OR a ≥4-char name token — body-only lexical matches dropped; every `load_inbox` gate (G98 subject gate, not deferred, `kind ∈ InboxKind`, no `never` rule); an in-process `_ASKED_THIS_SESSION` set (one MCP process = one conversation, G48 — a skip is never written to the bank); `normalization` hidden from the agent path; hard cap 5. Both MCP readers (`handle_check_nudges`, `_relevant_inbox`) go through `inbox_service.load_inbox`. `render_question` v2 prints `inbox-NNN · <kind> · entity_id=<slug>` (the slug is what the next call's `entity_ids=` needs), the question, a `Cause:` line, `k) label (Recommended) — <age> · <observer_label>`; the existing `a) Label — 6 months ago` prefix is preserved for `test_mcp_inbox_questions.py`. The discipline (one question per turn, after the work, quote the Cause line, never blocking, `skip=true` never re-asks, resolve only with the person's own answer) is G75's primer text — server-side filter, copy-only discipline. (7) Card: kind icon (never a monogram for non-entity kinds — made on the certain facts only; G97's refuted pfp explanation is NOT re-derived here and stays open there), line 1 question, line 2 cause (`From "<title>" · <harness> · <age>`), preview pane with the mention bolded from `mention_offsets`, `(Recommended)` row first, descriptions age-first then provenance (`you said this` / `Cicada inferred` / `Cicada's guess at 0.42`), `Other…`, footer `Not now — ask again in 7 days (l)`; copy rules enforced by a NEW `inbox_questions.validate_question` (Phase 1 code, ~30 lines — it does not exist today) in tests; keyboard via `QuestionSelection` (`1–4` pick, `⏎`, `o`, `l` = defer, `Esc` = collapse with NO write — a new enum case beside today's `pick/openOther/moveUp/moveDown/activate`, `c` conversation, `e` entity, `⌘[` back to the same card). (8) Silence: explicit later = defer + `defer_count` on the file; `defer_count ≥ 3` → 90-day auto-defer by the Stage 5.56 sweep as `cicada` (G85 shape); belief kinds (decay/conflict/divergence/normalization) silence-close under `inbox/silence_closed` (verdict `neutral`, reopen through the producers' merge-on-collision) — **clocked in Sleep cycles actually run (or the decay `decayed_through` watermark), never wall-clock** (TODO ruling 1: an engine outage is not user silence), and `silent_days` copy must never read as "why this decayed"; clarification/merge silence-close is owner question 3 (recommended: close too, 180 Sleep-counted days, traced and reopenable — recommended, not yet ruled). (9) Toast/tool result say what changed (`Archived alpha-project · 1 belief closed`), never the grade. **Rails.** Zero LLM on any read/generate/refresh/render path (G74/G80; a monkeypatched-providers test raises if touched); ledger ids/enums/counts/floats only — additive refs on the shipped `resolution` event (`surface`, `harness`, `session_id`, `recommended_key`, `picked_recommended`, `cause_tier`, `silent_days`, `defer_count`, `via_conversation_match`, `rule_applied`) plus new `ask` (ids only, per surfaced item) and `rule_written` (rule string) kinds in `FEEDBACK_KINDS` (R7) — never a behaviour input (settled #6; the proposal engine stays G113 slice 5, 💸 DECIDE under G78); user commits stay `Cicada-Author: user` and session-less pending **G116(b)**; the `"rodrigo"` observer literal at `inbox_service.py:813` becomes a `settings.observer_owner` (default `"user"`) rendered through one `observer_label`; no owner name in any template; every new field optional so older app builds decode. **Phases.** **1** (1–2 days, engine-free, no dependency — parallel to G113 slices 3–7 in its own worktree; the two touch disjoint functions of `inbox_service.py`): `inbox_context.py` (new: `resolve_cause`, `excerpt` with `mention_offsets`), schema fields, `_item_from_file` cause + decay synthesis + `recommended` via the shipped `_verdict`, `write_claim_nudges` persists `source_episode`, ETag both halves, `render_question` v2, `validate_question` (new), Swift `InboxCause` decode + `QuestionSelection` cases + `.decay` routed through `QuestionView`, `observer_owner`. **Phase 1 DOES touch the resolve path** — it accepts `resolve`+`archive\|keep` on decay as aliases of `archive`/`keep_active`, which must preserve the shipped R1 trigger labels that `test_inbox_resolution_provenance.py` asserts; `remind_later → _defer(7)` (R6) is G113 Task 5 and is pending on `dev` (`inbox_service.py:607` still writes `snooze_until`) — Phase 1 leans on it, does not own it. **2** (after G113 slices 3–4 and G106(i)): ask gate + MCP through `load_inbox` + `ask` event + handshake text (with G75), `never`/`defer:N` rules + `rules` component + Rules sheet, observer capsule + per-option excerpt + merge two-column preview (G103's first surface), `c`/`e`/`⌘[` navigation, response echo + `defer_count` persisted. **3** (after G116 and G75/G49): grouped cards (≤4 per entity, header chips, Now 12 / Later, `⌘⏎` accept-all-recommended excluding merges, `_inbox_counts` via `load_inbox`), Sleep-counted silence-close, the rule executor with the rule string in the manifest line, SessionStart injection replacing the copy-only per-session cap. → delivers **G97** (Phase 1, ahead of its wave position because every later phase stands on it) and the inbox half of **G113**; consumes **G60**, **G98**; first surface for **G103**; first caller of **G106**; evidence for **G108(b)**; carries **G75**'s inbox contract; gated by **G116** for rule execution and the session trailer. **Complaints answered by id:** **G114(3)** — the Telegram `/remind` reminder item is owned here as a `decay`-shaped `defer` with `surface: telegram` (G114 R4 parks it as a `capture_kind: reminder` note until then); **G110(b)** — `cause.conversation_id` on the wire plus `conversation_id?` on `cicada_check_nudges` is its approximation and `via_conversation_match` measures it, so do not reopen it; **G13** ("you had an open idea about X — still relevant?") rides the `decay` kind on a `concept`/`project` subject, no new kind; **G95 / G55 / G72** depend on the inbox staying one generic component — which is the argument for no new kinds (R4); **G100** stays where it is (asserted spans render as `span_kind: asserted` beside today's `derived`). **Owner, 2026-09-03 (evidence for Phase 1's card):** "the card has a chevron arrow at the end; I click on it but it doesn't do anything… the long list of URLs that doesn't end is not great" — two concrete defects the redesign must close first: a chevron that does nothing (either it opens the question or it is not drawn), and merge/clarification items over media that render every URL inline (collapse to a count + the top 3 with 'show all', never an unbounded list). The design study (judge panel memo, 2026-09-02) is already done — Phase 1 should start with these two. **Owner, 2026-09-03 — suggested outcome with a sentence of why (Phase 2, gated):** "some sort of agent that looks at the raw context of where the conflict or duplication is happening; if it passes a certain confidence threshold it politely gives a sentence of what it thinks the outcome should be — 'according to context I think it should be like this' — and the human confirms with a check mark or picks himself. Not every conflict gets a suggestion. Log these suggestions for later training." Design: this is the `(Recommended)` option plus a **rationale**, produced only when a judge over the item's cause spans (G118) is confident ≥ `inbox_suggest_min_confidence` (default 0.8) — below that the card shows options with no suggestion, exactly as today. The rationale is one sentence, written into the inbox item (bank, so it is provenance), attributed to the model, rendered under the recommended option as *Cicada thinks: …* with a one-key accept (⏎ on the recommended row) and the ordinary options beside it. Never auto-applied. **Training log:** the G113 `resolution` event already carries `recommended_key` and `picked_recommended`; add `suggestion_confidence` and `rationale_id` (ids/enums only — the sentence stays in the bank) so agreement-with-suggestion becomes a rate on `/consumption/feedback` and a labelled set for a future model. Runs in Stage 5 (the item is created there) or on demand for existing items, engine = the Sleep engine of the cycle, capped per cycle. **Owner, 2026-09-03 — research-resolvable conflicts (same Phase 2 agent, second tool):** "there are conflicts that can be solved by just looking up the repo — a conflict or clarification solved with research can be the outcome; maybe part of the Sleep cycle." Example seen on a project entity's `uses` predicate: `<architecture-concept>` vs `<database-x>` — the linked repo answers it in one grep (no such dependency). Design: the suggestion judge gets a bounded, read-only tool set drawn from what the entity already declares — `repos:` via `git_service` (file list, dependency manifests, grep; on demand, never cached), `sources:` URLs (G61, fetched under the G102 rules), and the cause spans — and its rationale must name what it checked (*"the repo's `pyproject.toml` has no `<database-x>` dependency; `<architecture-concept>` appears in `CLAUDE.md`"*); a rationale with no checked source is downgraded below the confidence gate. Runs where the item is created (Stage 5) or on demand from the card (*Research this*), capped per cycle, engine-free when only file reads are needed. The same item also shows a **G98** artefact: `uses` is a multi-valued predicate, so five true values were rendered as a conflict — Phase 1 must not ask for a winner on a predicate the schema marks multi-valued (cardinality from `_predicates.yaml`), only on single-valued ones. **Phase 1 shipped 2026-09-03 (PR #48, `feat/inbox-phase1`):** `inbox_context.py` (cause, three tiers, `[ no source recorded ]`), additive wire fields (`entity_type`, `source_episode`, `claim_id`, `cause`, `extractor_confidence`/`_model`, `recommended_key`, `informational`; options `recommended` + `verdict`), decay synthesised at read with `archive` recommended, `recommended_key` == the unique `_verdict`-agreed key (never neither/both/merge/clarification; absent on entity-path conflicts), options served recommended-first, `remind_later → _defer(7)` (G113 R6 landed here), `_owner_observer(settings)`, ledger `recommended_key`/`picked_recommended`, ETag both halves, `render_question` v2 + decay as a question on MCP, `validate_question`, the card (kind icon, question/cause lines, bolded excerpt, `QuestionView` for the two kinds that carry a question object — decay and conflict; clarification/merge keep their rows until Phase 2 — `1–4 ⏎ o l Esc`, real chevron, collapse past four lines, merge two-name preview, honest empty state), and the G98 informational rule. **Rulings made in the plan** (`docs/plans/2026-09-03-g115-inbox-phase1.md`): the informational dismiss grades `overruled` (the overruled belief is "these conflict"); clarification never carries a Recommended; decay's Recommended is `archive`; `cardinality` unions its two sources with `multi` winning rather than letting a stale bank map out-vote the seed; `_owner_observer`'s unset fallback stays the historical literal (not `user`) so an existing bank keeps one observer lineage — the choice moves to G117; the merge preview is two names + suggested type until G103. **Final review (3 fixes, same branch):** decay's `allow_other: false` is now honoured end to end — MCP's `Other / Later` line gates each half on its own flag (it was inviting free text an agent could not have honoured) and `_normalize_decay_request` 400s a `resolve`/`answer` carrying free text (it used to be appended to the entity body while the page stayed `decaying` and the item was deleted, inverting a “still relevant” answer); `recommended_key` synthesises decay's read-time options so `_emit_resolution` records the recommendation the card actually showed instead of `null`; and both MCP reader loops build ONE `InboxContext` instead of one per item (a per-item `episodes/`+`entities/` scan measured 425 ms for 40 items on a 2,000-episode bank, in the conversation path). Phases 2–3 unchanged. **G118 slice 2 (server):** the cause's asserted span now survives a continued conversation (`grown` — `_asserted_span` reads `evidence.span_status`), and "Show in conversation" needs no new field: the Reader opens `GET /episodes/{id}/text?start=&end=` at the cause window's absolute offset plus the mention offset. **G118 slice 2 (app):** the cause line wears its harness's real mark, the excerpt reads in the quote face, and "Show in conversation" opens the Reader on the mention — a cause found by name stays labelled found by name there. **DS-2 (2026-09-24):** the Inbox is D's progressive columns; one tap with a 5 s send-delay Undo, so an undone answer never reaches the ledger. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G141 | <a id="g141"></a>**Project timelines — dated happenings with every participant linked, a "you are here" band, and the knowledge around a project, planned or not** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "in cicada i need a good way to track 'projects' with clusters of knowledge and ongoing things being dated. So for example if i'm working on a robotics project, i may or mayb not have a timeline setup for it. I'd like to see graphically me today and progress throughout the timeline, and also have stuff like 'yesterday, Rodrigo [user], got a pdf guide [url pdf], provided to him by <person-a> [person], that walk him through connecting to the lab cluster [specs of lab cluster in the cluster entity maybe], and is connecting to run the <demo-x> demo'. Stuff like this would be awesome." — real names redacted) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Happenings, milestones, participants and timeline read models exist. Finish named graph/project/date/evidence/interaction seams and verify them; do not rebuild the event writer or claim done from backend-only events. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g141). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** The owner wants four things: projects as first-class things to track, with or without a plan; a graphical timeline per project showing "me today" and progress; dated happenings narrated with every participant linked (the owner, a person, a document with its URL, a tool whose specs live on its own page, a sub-goal) and with a status (done yesterday, ongoing today); and a cluster of the people, tools, documents and sub-projects around the project. None of this exists as one thing. There is no per-project timeline, no milestone (`grep -ri milestone api/` finds only comments), no record with more than two participants, and nothing reads "an ongoing thing nobody mentioned for three weeks". The dated data that does exist is never drawn together: G17 `due` claims (`entity_extractor.py:113-117`), G140 `expected_end` (`claims.py:184`; `ClaimModel` does not even carry it, `schemas.py:710`), supersede chains, G118 spans with turn times (`evidence.py:399,431`) and conversation timestamps. **Four defects the design leans on (verified at dev 6eea9ee).** (1) Sleep drops every claim whose subject has no page, under a false comment ("re-emitted next cycle"; the episode is already marked processed), and it keys the subject by `sanitize_id(raw name)` rather than Stage 2's id, so "the user connects to X" lands on a page that does not exist (`claim_pipeline.py:139-146`, `entity_extractor.py:530-531`). (2) Reconcile folds Monday's and Thursday's same-object claim into Monday's and never moves `valid_from` (`claim_reconciler.py:436-445`). (3) Expiry closes a met `due` and a missed one the same way, so an expired date says nothing about done (`claim_expiry.py:41-133`). (4) Stop-hook episodes store `turns:` as an integer count (`transcript_capture.py:261,284`), so a Claude Code session resumed over three days dates everything to day 1. **The design** (spec `docs/specs/2026-09-23-g141-project-timelines-design.md`, rulings R-PJ1 to R-PJ23; three architect designs judged twice). Happenings and milestones are **claims**: not prose, not a new file type, and never an 11th entity type (G17's "July 8th" lesson). There are two predicates: `happened` (ongoing / done / dropped) and `milestone` (planned / done / missed / dropped; one slot per slug, so a moved target is a visible supersede chain).
