There are four optional fields, each omitted when empty: `status`, `target` (which expiry never reads), `participants` (a closed role set, each linking an exact substring of a plain sentence, with no wikilinks in YAML) and `date_basis` (stated / turn / episode / person / written). A milestone's slug is its `object`, never its `context`, because a non-`general` context becomes a graph facet satellite (`claim_contexts.is_facet`); the person's app writes use `origin: companion_app`, which joins `_HUMAN_ORIGINS` (`claim_reconciler.py:62`) so they are protected. A done happening is **born closed** (`valid_to == valid_from`), so every reader that treats open as "currently believed" stays correct with no change. The few history readers learn one test, `claims.is_event`, which a grep-gate test enforces. Events are not records, so they stay in FTS and in episode citations, where they render as dated happenings rather than "No longer current" (`provenance.py:443`, `search_service.py:444`). Only `progress.py` writes an event predicate. Event cardinality lives in code, because an existing bank's stale `_predicates.yaml` cannot be trusted (`predicates.py:159-189`). Dedup matches span identity first, then date-aware ids, so a G104 re-read never duplicates. Python decides every date through one resolver against the turn's time, and the resolver beats the model. Nothing relative is stored, and neither wire carries today or a viewer zone. **The order is derive, then write, then spend.** A $0 read model assembles moments from what every bank already holds, with the owner's own washed sentence as the narration. $0 writers follow: `cicada_note_progress` over MCP and the person's in-app Log field, milestones and Done / Still going / Stopped. Sleep extraction comes last, 💸 and gated on owner-graded measurements. A quiet ongoing thread or an overdue milestone raises an engine-free `followup` inbox item instead of decaying. Its answers are G113 verdicts that grade the extractor. The app is a Projects page in the owner's Direction D grammar: list, then project, then Reader, with a 96 pt band (a Today marker in `textPrimary`; milestone diamonds hollow, filled, ghosted or slashed; done dots, ongoing capsules, a density strip). An unplanned project draws only what happened and an open end. **Absorbs and cites.** It absorbs **G4** for projects: "how did it end up going?" becomes the follow-up, and "solved by X" is the answer written as a done happening. It absorbs **G13**'s per-project half: the status alphabet, the per-project list, and the inbox surfacing. G13 keeps the app-wide board and undated "we should…" capture. It extends **G17**: a project `due` reads as a milestone until the person touches it, and an expired `due` reads "passed, no word on how it went", never done. It delivers **G140**'s open P10 (open-loops cursor: `_state.md` v3 `now`) and P11 (last time in this project: `cicada_project` and `GET /projects`) for projects; G140's app surface for its bank-wide `cicada_timeline` stays with Tracks P/O. It cites **G53** (`_state.md` v3 `now`/`next`), **G75** (Current lines, R12), **G106**, **G108** (the rail cell needs its ruling), **G113**, **G115** (the cause rail), **G118** (spans; the Stop-hook sidecar amends R-PB4) and **G124** (`ConversationRow`, Resume, sparklines). **Triage: APPLY.** PJ-0 to PJ-6 cost $0. PJ-7 (Stage-1 happening extraction) is 💸, about +15–30% Stage-1 spend on BYOK, $0 on the plans, and ruling 4 is unchanged. It is built only if M1–M3 show derived moments plus agent writes fall short. PJ-8 (a consented per-project re-read) is 💸, user-triggered only. **Size: L** (nine PR-sized slices). The backend slices PJ-0 (page-less fix), PJ-1 (read model) and PJ-4 (Stop-hook turn stamps) can start now, then PJ-2 (`cicada_project` + handshake) and PJ-3 (event claims + writes). PJ-5 (the app) waits for the DS shell and the G108 ruling. PJ-6 follow-ups come after PJ-3. **Open DECIDEs:** the Projects rail cell and its number (G108; default ⌘7 after Sources so no shortcut moves); the band's colour (neutral shapes vs DESIGN_RULES §10's "dots use data hues", R-PJ21); holding an unpromoted subject's claims in the pending store (split out of PJ-0 on purpose). **Shipped 2026-09-23 (PR #88, `feat/g141-capture-side`).** PJ-0: Stage 2 returns its `name_to_id` and Stage 5.56 keys every claim endpoint through it (`entity_resolver.endpoint_id`, the edge rule as one function), maps the closed owner surfaces ("the user", "me", …) onto the `owner: true` page, and counts the claims whose subject still has no page (`claims_page_less`: counts in the log, the claim ids at DEBUG, never a subject; carried into the `sleep_run` row as M3's measure); the false "re-emitted next cycle" comment is gone, and the R-PJ17 hold is `claim_pipeline.hold_page_less`, a seam that holds nothing until PJ-0b (the hold was ruled yes on 2026-09-23). Claims now follow Stage 2's matches, its fuzzy merges included (disclosed; G98's). PJ-4: the Stop hook writes `turns: [{offset, ts, speaker}]` through `episode_staging.stamps_for` (the hook's `role: text` body is the stager's line shape byte for byte, so the spec's "cannot reuse" did not hold), outside the hash, the last key, head-stable at 500; the episode `timestamp` stays the session's start, a pre-PJ-4 episode keeps its count until its session's next rewrite, and a lint keeps the `turns` key to its three modules. **Carry-forward:** the on-disk `memory/banks/demo` holds one non-synthetic capture, so every screenshot and fixture comes from a freshly generated demo bank. From this track on no capture writer can write into a demo bank (`demo_guard`, see G117), so the capture leak cannot recur; the old copy still needs regenerating. **Ruled 2026-09-23 (owner: "i'd go with your suggestions … maybe like a green bar that fills up? something that fits the aesthetic. make nodes in the timeline clickable"):** Projects is the eighth rail item (⌘8, after Sources; Home took ⌘1); the band is a meadow-green fill that fills up to Today with every node clickable; Sleep holds an unpromoted subject's claims with the pending entity (new slice PJ-0b); R-PJ16 accepted. **Built PJ-0b (2026-09-24, `feat/g141-hold-page-less`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-24-g141-hold-page-less.md`, R-HP1…R-HP13).** The pending store moved to `pending_store.py`, now its one writer; `vector_index` delegates. Stage 5.56 holds a page-less subject's Stage-1 claims on the pending line whose slug is that subject (`held_claims`, as `Claim.to_dict()`: spans, not copies; at most 50 per name, head-stable, the rest counted), and releases them first, through Stage 3, in the cycle whose Stage 5 gave the name a page: onto Stage 2's own exact verdict for the name, with the conversation they were heard in credited on the page. A holding line leaves the store only then. Promotion keeps it and a re-park carries it, so a cancel or a failure between Stage 2 and the claim write loses no held claim. Never held (still counted as `claims_page_less`): a subject with no pending line (Stage 1 named the endpoint differently from the entity), the owner surfaces, a claim closed in its own batch, and an event. Counts only: `claims_held`, `claims_released`, `claims_hold_capped` and `claims_waiting` on the `sleep_run` row. **Open:** nothing expires a pending name (an owner question; R7 and D2). **Built PJ-5 (2026-09-24, `feat/g141-projects-page`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-24-g141-projects-page.md`, R-PP1…R-PP27).** Projects is the eighth page (⌘8). The list, at D density, shows Active · Quiet · All, a mini green bar per project, the next milestone or "No plan yet", people from the graph and the compact age. A project opens with the band — a green bar that fills up to "You, today", every mark a button (ring, hover words, ←/→, ⏎ into the Reader) — then Log progress (dated by the server from the words, with Undo), Now, Lately (one sentence per happening, every participant a chip), Plan and Around this project; the Reader or a card is the third column. Writes are `Mutation`s with the server's own 409 sentence. `/projects` stays out of the Store (an in-memory, ETag-revalidated cache). `ProjectState` runs `timeline_state.json` beside the Python, and the app's fixture is the demo's real wire, pinned by a pytest. **Reported, not built:** `GraphNode` dates (a backend change), people on `ProjectRow`, a hash on `TimelineQuote`, Home's "In motion", the Graph card's "Open project ›", event rows elsewhere ("day · status · sentence"), Move, and the PJ-8 menu item. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🟡 PJ-1/2/3/6 built (feat/g141-read-write); PJ-0b built (feat/g141-hold-page-less); PJ-5 built (feat/g141-projects-page); GraphNode dates reported (backend). | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G169 | <a id="g169"></a>**Every bank starts with its owner — a "(you)" page, seeded on creation and filled by chats and consolidation** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29: "the owner page should be created automatically with a (you) parenthesis at the start of a new bank, with a small sentence like the main user or something. It gets populated with beliefs as consolidation and chats happen. Lets not assume users will have a bank to carry knowledge from, tho in the exports, chatgpt and claude might have a 'memory' markdown that might be useful to extract info about the user but a lot might be outdated too, that why cicada is better.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Bank owner seeding and owner-flag rendering are merged. Confirm an empty/new bank and imported consolidation correctly enrich it; stale not-merged text does not mean missing implementation. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g169). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** The seeded owner-page implementation is present in merged dev; the historical PR #140 “open” label below is stale. Validate a clean bank, required owner setup, neutral seeding and no cross-bank carryover in G10. This row stays active for that live acceptance, not for a second implementation. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The gap:** a new bank had no owner page until onboarding's owner PUT, so a first drain read a hundred conversations into a graph with no 'me' to attach the person's own statements to (G117's owner-page question, left open in round 4). **Shipped in PR #140 (branch `fix/first-run-extraction-owner`, opened, not merged):** `create_bank` seeds one `owner: true` person page in its own `cicada` commit — the machine-level name from `owner.json` when one was saved, else a neutral `Owner` placeholder (`owner_placeholder: true`, id `owner`) that opens 'The main person this memory belongs to.'; never the demo bank; the lifespan also seeds the first-boot default bank (which never goes through `create_bank`), only when brand new. **Rulings inside it:** `name` stays the plain name, because Stage 2 matches a mention to a page by `name` and a stored '(you)' would stop the person's own name resolving — the app renders 'Name (you)' from the flag; `PUT /settings/owner` adopts a placeholder (renames it, keeps its id and claims) rather than writing a second owner page; **nothing is carried from another bank** — only the machine-level name and an id, so no user is assumed to have prior knowledge to import. Because the seeded page would make `graph.nodes.isEmpty` false and hide the Welcome and the empty states, the app now uses `hasNoContentBeyondOwner` (FirstRunGate, Graph, Clusters). Existing banks are not migrated; the page is evergreen so a drain's decay never touches it. **The exports' own memory files** (a Claude or ChatGPT 'memory' markdown) are useful leads but often outdated — see **G170**: they arrive as dated, lower-trust *assistant* words that Sleep checks against what the person actually said. **Not verified live:** a clean first run on a fresh bank (Welcome with the owner node present, '(you)' in the graph and card). **Follow-up:** the Sleep v5 first drain shows the page filling ('your page has N beliefs', **G163** §4.8). → resolves the owner-page question in **G117**; feeds **G163**, **G170**. **Previous execution label (historical):** CODE MERGED · live fresh-bank acceptance in G10. | VERIFY · P1 |
| G170 | <a id="g170"></a>**A first import reads honestly — quoted attachments are documents, memory files are background, and an import is not silence** (found 2026-09-29 reviewing the first-run path with a real ~1,200-conversation export in mind; no bank content recorded here) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Import attachment/background/date/silence fixes are merged. Check representative fresh/delta exports under G10 rather than rebuilding parsers from historical failure notes. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g170). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Import attachment/authorship and decay-stamp fixes are implemented in merged dev; historical open-PR status is stale. Verify real export shapes through the permitted import UI, document versus speaker attribution and date preservation in the G10 trial. Do not infer real-data quality from synthetic unit tests. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Two defects, both on the first-drain path, fixed in PR #140 (opened, not merged).** (1) *Extraction:* a quoted `attachment [...]` turn is a document the person shared, not their words, and a `claude_memory` episode is dated, lower-trust background; Stage 1 is now told both, and `parse_anthropic_memories` declares `evidence_kind: assistant` so those spans read as Claude's, never the person's (builds on the `attachment [<file name>]:` grammar, G118). (2) *Decay:* an import is not silence. A page or claim a cycle creates or references from months-old episodes keeps its content date (`last_referenced`, `valid_from`) but gets `decayed_through` = the cycle's date, so silence counts from when Cicada learned it. Without this a multi-cycle drain of a backdated export charged, and could archive, what it had just read. `decayed_through` is a stamp, not a change to the arithmetic, so **TODO ruling 1 is respected** (still at most one week per cycle, measured from `max(last_referenced, decayed_through)`). CLAUDE.md updated in the three places these made it wrong (decay, evidence kind, owner page). **Tests:** `test_decay_backdated_import.py`, `test_evidence_extraction.py`, `test_conversations.py`. **Not verified live:** a real multi-cycle drain of a backdated export. → relates **G147**, **G118**, **G163** (the drain runs both decay engines once at the end), **G169**. **Previous execution label (historical):** CODE MERGED · real export/drain acceptance in G10. | VERIFY · P1 |
| G171 | <a id="g171"></a>**Sleep at scale — decay questions asked once and capped, and both derived indexes update incrementally, off the event loop** (found 2026-09-29 reviewing what a bank with many fading pages does to a cycle) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Decay-question dedup/caps and incremental off-loop indexes are implemented. Validate a realistic drain's capped questions/index updates; remaining write-lock defects are G183, not proof this feature is absent. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g171). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Incremental indexes and capped/deduplicated decay questions are implemented in merged dev; historical PR #141 open status is stale. During G10 check progress, pause/resume and UI responsiveness on the chosen batch. Avoid a new scale benchmark or embedding upgrade before observing the actual bottleneck. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** (1) *Decay questions.* A bank with many fading pages piled up duplicate 'Still tracking…?' items every cycle, and a *keep* answer only reached the first claim of a merged question. A decay inbox item is now keyed by entity: an entity with an open decay item (pending or deferred, raised by the entity path or by any of its fading claims) is refreshed, not duplicated; a cycle opens at most `decay_inbox_cap_per_cycle` (10) new ones, lowest confidence first, through one `DecayBudget` shared by `inbox_generator.generate` and `write_claim_nudges`; nudges the cap turns away are counted in the cycle's `sleep_run` row (`decay_nudges_deferred`) and raised again next cycle — deferred, never dropped silently. Each fading claim joins `claim_ids`, so a *keep* reaches every claim the question covered. An older pile is collapsed once by `inbox_migration.dedup_decay_items` (own `.deduped_decay` marker, oldest kept, claim ids folded in), registered in `bank_migrations`. (2) *Indexes.* Sleep rebuilt both derived indexes in full each cycle, blocking the event loop while embedding the whole bank. The sqlite-vec index keeps a stable `key` and the `hash` of the embedded text per row (`SqliteVecIndexer._sync_kind`): a cycle embeds only new and changed texts, removes deleted ones and refreshes metadata in place without an embed; a missing table, a pre-`hash` schema, another model (recorded per kind) or another width rebuilds that table in full, and an embed that fails leaves the previous index untouched. FTS goes through `search_index.refresh` (the `ensure_fresh` stamp diff), so an idle night re-indexes nothing; a full build only when the file is missing, damaged or of another schema. Sleep runs both through `asyncio.to_thread`. **Shipped in PR #141 (`fix/sleep-inbox-and-indexes`, opened, not merged);** CLAUDE.md updated in the vector index, FTS freshness and decay sections; tests `test_decay_inbox_dedup.py`, `test_decay_inbox_dedup_migration.py`, `test_index_incremental.py`. **Not verified live:** the wall-clock gain on a large bank, that the event loop stays responsive during a real cycle, and the one-time dedup migration against a large real pile. → relates **G98** (the queue must be answerable), **G147**, **G136**, **G163** (a drain calls the indexes once per batch). **Previous execution label (historical):** CODE MERGED · real drain responsiveness in G10. | VERIFY · P1 |
| G172 | <a id="g172"></a>**Saved links: same-titled saves never overwrite each other, and reading new pages is never starved by reuse** (found 2026-09-29 reviewing the link path; also the identity fact G162 depends on) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** URL identity/collision and fair read-budget fixes are merged. Include same-title saves and new-versus-reused links when the trial contains media; conversation-only trial can proceed without this acceptance slice. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g172). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** ID collision/enrichment-floor implementation is in merged dev; historical PR #139 open status is stale. Verify only the remaining live acceptance: same-titled saves do not overwrite and new-page reading never drops below the configured floor. This can follow the first export trial unless that trial includes saved links. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** (1) *Collision.* `media_ingestor._media_entity_id` slugged only the title and `write_media_entity` overwrites, so a second link titled e.g. 'Home' replaced the first's page and orphaned its `url_index` row. The id now takes the URL-hash suffix (already used for truncated slugs) when the plain id names a page written for a *different* URL, `media-<slug>-<sha256(normalized url)[:8]>`, deterministic so a re-save finds it again. Ids are never renamed: a URL already in the index returns `duplicate` before the id is minted, a page for the same URL (index lost) keeps the plain id and is rewritten in place, a legacy page with no `media.url_hash` is judged by its `media.url`. Pages already overwritten before the fix are not recovered. **This is why G162 keys the video queue on the url-index `videoKey`, never the entity id.** (2) *Throughput.* Reuse (a description the page already carries, zero LLM) and fetch-plus-summarize shared one per-night cap with reuse spent first, so a night with at least a cap's worth of reuse candidates read no new page. Both the tail `backfill` and the in-cycle `enrich_media_links` now reserve a floor of the cap for fetch and summary work: `fetch_floor(settings, cap)` = `CICADA_LINK_ENRICH_FETCH_MIN_PER_CYCLE` when set, else half the cap rounded up, clamped to `[0, cap]` (`0` restores reuse-first). The floor only reserves slots for fetch candidates that exist and only when the fetch tier can run; unused slots return to reuse, so the nightly total stays at the cap. In-cycle `_candidates` now honours the 30-day fetch backoff for a page that needs a fetch. Rails untouched: the ToS rail, never-scraped papers and video hosts, the per-cycle cap. **Shipped in PR #139 (`fix/saved-links-collision-and-throughput`, opened, not merged);** tests `test_media_entity_id_collision.py`, `test_link_backfill.py`. **Not verified live:** the floor's effect on a real backlog's drain rate and the collision behaviour on existing banks. → relates **G102**, **G164** (the Reader ladder replaces `default_fetch` under this floor), **G162**. **Previous execution label (historical):** CODE MERGED · saved-link acceptance remains. | VERIFY · P1 |

Record hands-on observations as observations, not benchmark scores. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The problem.** Cicada's claim layer, Sleep dedup, decay and hybrid recall have never been measured on a public dataset, so "better memory" is an assertion. The post's own figures (MemoryBench: Filesystem 54.2%, RAG 58.3%, Supermemory 85.9%) cannot stand in: the harness calls every provider's `search()` unconditionally, so the "tool is not called" failure it argues against is never exercised; its Filesystem baseline is per-session `gpt-4o-mini` extraction plus query-word substring matching, not Claude Code's memory; each provider ships its own answer prompt; its LoCoMo loader grades 444 of 446 adversarial questions against the string `"undefined"` (`String(qa.answer)` on items with only `adversarial_answer`); the published run's dataset, models and commit are not in the repo; and the vendor wrote the harness it wins. **Evidence:** [`../research/2026-09-24-memory-benchmarks-and-implicit-recall.md`](../research/2026-09-24-memory-benchmarks-and-implicit-recall.md) — MemoryBench read at commit `94e2af5` (providers, six checkpointed phases, `gpt-4o` answerer and binary judge, per-type prompts, MemScore), LongMemEval/LoCoMo/ConvoMem methodology and ten known pitfalls (a full-context baseline beating a memory system on LoCoMo is the one that matters most at personal scale). **Verified blocker in the existing harness:** `benchmarks/run_table3.py:223`, `run_table1.py:90` and `run_ablation.py:96` pass `Settings(memory_path=…)`; `memory_path` is a property and `memory_root` accepts only its env aliases (`api/config.py:33-41,261`), so both `memory_path=` and `memory_root=` are silently dropped (tested 2026-09-24: both resolve to the default root; only `CICADA_MEMORY_PATH` takes) — a Sleep run there can land in whatever bank the environment resolves, and `benchmarks/_bootstrap.py:42-43` loads `api/.env` into the environment first. **Two more:** Sleep's "today" is the wall clock (`sleep_cycle.py:1330` passes no `now` to `conflict_resolver.resolve_and_prune`, `:41,145`; `claim_pipeline.py:315`; expiry at `sleep_cycle.py:836`) — decay is capped at 7 days a cycle (`conflict_resolver.py:33`, `claim_reconciler.py:73`), so it does not archive a dated dataset at once, but expiry closes every past-dated `due`/`expected_end` claim on the first cycle and multi-cycle runs decay on 2026's clock, skewing knowledge-update and temporal questions; and a direct `sleep_cycle.run` is user-triggered (`:1012`), so ruling 4 does not stop it spending plan quota. **Design constraints:** a throwaway bank per unit (a LoCoMo conversation; a LongMemEval question) under a `cicada_bench_` temp dir, refused otherwise; a separate `CICADA_HOME` (connections prefs, telemetry, token never the owner's); `CICADA_MEMORY_PATH` by env, telemetry/capture/connector fetch off; **never the owner's bank, never read its `memory/`**; sessions ingested as dated `EpisodeDraft`s (`episode_staging.py:86-92,285-290` — the importers' shared stager; `cicada_save_episode` stamps now, the Stop hook stamps the harness session's start); engine pinned to `byok` + a named model; **both modes** — H (hooks: recall injected every question) and T (tools: the model decides, with the handshake contract, tool-call rate reported, ≥ 2 answer models) — plus a full-context control and no-Sleep vs Sleep (G101 measured); answer model and judge fixed across rows; **reported per category** with context tokens and p50/p95 latency beside accuracy, every model and the dataset hashes named, no single headline and no row set beside 85.9%. **Build first, in order:** (1) fix the `Settings` bug + the path guard; (3) LongMemEval_S + LoCoMo loaders; (4) the engine pin; (5) the tools-mode loop with call logging; (6) MemoryBench's per-type judge prompts, validated on ≥ 50 human-graded answers before any number is trusted (`benchmarks/README.md:57-58`). **First cheap pass:** 30 LongMemEval_S questions (5 per type) no-Sleep in H, T × 2 and full context; then one LoCoMo conversation with and without one Sleep. Optional later: a `cicada` provider inside MemoryBench for a like-for-like row. → measures **G149**; answers **G101**; extends the `benchmarks/` thesis harness and **G78**'s eval question; bounded by ruling 4 and the privacy rule. For example i use <agent-a> a lot which is an ai agent i text on imessage. Can i process the conversations i have with it and consolidate them in memory without me thinking about it and just setting up the sync and selection of conversations i want consolidated?" — the agent's name redacted) | **Owner priority/detection follow-up (2026-10-06):** Selected iMessage sync is P1; the owner explicitly wants this implemented. Build the minimum G188 reader/parser/stager seam alongside it. Add opt-in modular AI-agent identification, with Instinct as the initial detector, separate from transport and inference providers. Use permitted scoped metadata, expose uncertainty and durable user overrides; detection cannot widen selected capture, raise trust or turn agent speech into the owner’s words. Verify selected conversation → continued/revised sync → actual Sleep → grounded answers with correct speaker/source/evidence. Other messenger exports follow later. [Requirements](../specs/2026-10-06-provider-and-ingestion-modularity.md). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** No scoped iMessage conversation reader was found. App must read selected bytes with FDA, backend parse; implement speaker/source/date/attachment/privacy semantics before any capture, then exports for other messengers. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g179). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 requests selected iMessage conversations as consolidation input. The app obtains permission and reads bytes; the backend receives scrubbed staged data, never opens ~/Library. Start with explicit selected threads/date range, stable source keys, speaker attribution, delta updates/deletions and visible consent/revocation. Do not capture the whole message database by default. No personal thread names/examples belong in public docs. Other messaging providers follow later. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Not G29:** G29's bot sees only what is forwarded to it, never an existing conversation. **The sources, as found 2026-10-05 (from documentation and the platforms' known layouts; nothing on the owner's Mac was opened):** (1) **iMessage** — `~/Library/Messages/chat.db`, SQLite (`message`, `handle`, `chat`, `chat_message_join`); recent macOS often leaves `message.text` empty and keeps the body in `attributedBody` (an archived attributed string), so both are decoded; `date` is nanoseconds since 2001; `message.ROWID` is the cursor; with Messages in iCloud the phone's conversations reach the Mac's file. **Reading it needs Full Disk Access for the app — there is no lighter route.** Tapbacks (`associated_message_type`) are skipped; attachments are named, not read, at first; an edit or unsend (`date_edited`, `date_retracted`) updates the staged episode. (2) **WhatsApp** — the Mac app's `ChatStorage.sqlite` in its group container (`group.net.whatsapp.WhatsApp.shared`; `ZWAMESSAGE`, `ZWACHATSESSION`): undocumented and unverified here, behind macOS's app-data permission, and only the history the desktop app synced; check its structure (never content) on the owner's Mac before building. Unofficial WhatsApp clients are out (the ToS rail). (3) **Telegram** — the Mac apps encrypt their local stores, and the API would mean Cicada signing in as the person, which G166's rule forbids; the route is Telegram's own *Export chat history* (JSON) into a watched folder, so a manual step now and then. **Shape (existing rails, no new ones):** the app reads `~/Library` and posts bytes; the backend stages through `episode_staging` keyed by service + conversation + sitting (edits in place, removals tombstoned); **one episode per sitting** — a gap of a few hours closes it (G104's one-episode-per-session reading); **nothing is read until the person ticks a conversation** (Settings lists chats with their last activity, one toggle each — the browsers' read-only-after-turned-on rule); Sleep consolidates on its own schedule. **Provenance:** a texted agent's replies are that agent's turns, never the person's own words (as G135's remote claims and G105's agent replies); group chats carry other people's words, and per-conversation opt-in is the gate; every writer scrubs. **To decide:** Full Disk Access (the owner found it "too much" for bookmarks, 2026-09-23 — here it is optional and scoped to this feature, with the exact-fix sheet); whether attachments are ever read; the sitting gap; what unticking a conversation does to episodes already saved (stop syncing; keep unless removed). **Order:** iMessage (covers the texted agent; a stable local store) → WhatsApp after the structure check → Telegram by export. Relates **G29** (the bot), **G12** (chat-history import), **G104/G105** (sessions, deterministic capture), **G134** (the shared stager), **G81** (names for handles), **G30** (opt-in local reads). **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY · scoped iMessage prototype; permission boundary to decide. | UNBUILT · P1 |

Build governed commands, stable structured output, discovery/skill and error contracts; measure MCP schema savings without breaking no-shell clients. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g180). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner confirms a leaner MCP and CLI as priorities. Consolidate G157 here. Build a headless cicada CLI over the same governed services with machine-readable output, structured errors, bounded paging/discovery and import/consolidate/status/recall/export operations. Measure the existing 30-tool schema and total task context, then shorten descriptions or evaluate scoped profiles/grouping. Keep hosted/no-shell MCP clients, capability scopes, provenance and R12 parity; a generic execute door must not bypass them. AMR export is G186, packaging/platform work G181/G187. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Why.** The MCP server lists 30 tools whose definitions are ~34.6k characters (~8.7k tokens, measured 2026-10-05), paid up front by every client that loads tool schemas eagerly; an agent with a shell (Claude Code, Codex, an agent on a server — G181) can run a command and read `--help` only when it needs to. Capture and recall already left the tool-call path for hooks (G105, G149) because depending on a model choosing to call a tool was unreliable; the CLI is the same move for an agent's deliberate actions. **MCP stays** as the door for clients without a shell — Claude Desktop and claude.ai, ChatGPT, the phone through G135 (the auth case) — and for the `initialize` briefing and the harness's identity. **Shape — one implementation, two thin doors.** The CLI and the MCP handlers call the same `api.services` functions, so no rule gets a second copy (scrub, staging, `episode_lock`, the per-bank git lock, provenance trailers, the revision-checked mark of #176). It lives in this repo (`api/cli.py` and an entry point; a Homebrew tap is a separate one-file repo holding only the formula — G181). Commands mirror the tools: `cicada recall \| ask \| timeline \| project \| save \| claim \| retract \| pending \| mark \| inbox \| sources \| backlog \| handshake \| sleep status \| bank`, each with `--json`; writes carry `Cicada-Author` and the session from the harness's environment (G48). **Launcher:** never a venv console script — G88 found the shebang hardcodes the interpreter and breaks when the repo moves — but a small launcher that resolves the repo's venv, or `uv tool` / brew. **The skill:** `SKILL.md` and `skills/cicada-librarian` teach the CLI first to shell agents (a few lines plus `cicada --help`) and the MCP to the rest; `install.md` (G76) installs both; the handshake names whichever door the client has (R12). **To decide:** whether CLI writes go through the running backend's HTTP API (one writer process) or in-process like the MCP server (works with the backend down, same locks); which MCP tools to merge once shell agents use the CLI. Relates **G76**, **G88**, **G105**, **G135**, **G149**, **G181**. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §1: owner asks for a leaner MCP alongside CLI-first shell agents. Fresh static `TOOLS` baseline: 30 tools, default `json.dumps` 35,109 chars (8,777 tokens by chars/4), compact JSON 33,772 chars — proxy, not client billing. Audit shared description policy into the existing handshake/skill; evaluate related-operation grouping and optional profiles against representative workflows and compatibility. Preserve R12, sources/read/record/answer scope separation, provenance and ownership; no generic dispatcher bypass. Measure total schema/primer/skill/discovery overhead and task/tool-selection outcomes before/after; do not pick a reduced tool count without evidence. G186 is the export consumer.** **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY · first implementation after trial blockers, P1. | UNBUILT · P2 |

| G191 | <a id="g191"></a>**Ask why at the moment of saving — a native one-tap "what interested you?" prompt when the person bookmarks or pins something** (Rodrigo 2026-10-07) | **Owner, verbatim (2026-10-07):** "when i bookmark something like a website in chrome or safari or any other browser, i sometimes have an intention, e.g. i save a technical blog that explains something in interest that some things there are useful to replicate in my own projects. Or i save a website because i love the design of their application and web and also their launch video. So i thought maybe cicada as the macos app can have a slick prompt that appears that looks like part of MacOS and asks for a what interested you in saving this? and you write a small note on it (even better if cicada gives me options to just click, with the option to further describe too. if i click anything else tho the prompt dissappears. Detecting also when i pin somehting in pinterest, twitter or anything else would also be amazing." **Why it matters:** a bookmark records *what* was saved; the person's intent is the *why*, and today it is lost — Sleep must guess relevance later from the page alone. A stated intent is the person's own words at the moment of the action (highest trust, `observer: person`), so it grounds recall relevance (G93: "the blog I saved for its sync technique"), feeds the projects the save was meant for (G141/G150 — "useful to replicate in my own projects"), and is exactly the grounded reward signal the Era-of-Experience framing asks for (G113). It also turns a passive bookmark pile (G86, G3) into intentions an agent can act on, and later into skills (G112). **What exists:** the app already watches browser bookmarks and knows the moment one is added (G129 Chrome/Safari bookmark watch, seen-set; G119 other browsers; G160 tab groups) — the trigger is there; nothing asks. **Design constraints:** (1) native look — a small non-activating floating panel (NSPanel, chrome-layer Liquid Glass only, DR rules; follows DESIGN_RULES direction D), positioned near the menu bar / the saving app, never steals focus or keyboard input from the browser; (2) one tap — a few deterministic choices (e.g. design I like, a technique to replicate, read later, for <project>, inspiration) plus an optional "say more" field; (3) any click elsewhere, a timeout, or Esc dismisses it silently and the bookmark is still captured as today (UX principle 4: non-intrusive, never required); (4) **no LLM at capture time** — the quick-pick options must come from deterministic sources (a fixed set plus the person's active project and backlog names, G141/G150), never a model call; (5) the answer is stored with the bookmark's episode as the person's own words through the one staging path (episode_staging, scrub), so Sleep reads it with the page; (6) rate limit and "don't ask for this site/folder" so a bulk import or a bookmark-bar reorganisation never floods prompts; (7) a Settings switch, on by default only after the owner tries it. **Pins and likes elsewhere (Pinterest, X/Twitter, …):** RESEARCH — the ToS rail forbids reading behind a login and the backend never fetches login-walled pages; candidate routes are an opt-in browser extension the person installs (sees the pin/like click in their own browser, posts bytes like the app does), official exports/APIs the person connects, or the person's own agent reading on request (G166). Detection is not capture until the person allows the site (per-site switch). Relates G129/G119/G160/G86/G3/G93/G113/G141/G150/G166/G112. Triage: **APPLY** for browser bookmarks (P2), **RESEARCH** for third-party pins/likes. | UNBUILT · P2 |

| G193 | <a id="g193"></a>**Port the app to GPUI (Rust) — performance, and maybe Windows** (Rodrigo 2026-10-07) | **Owner, verbatim (2026-10-07):** "see if maybe we can port the app to gpui rust for better performance and maybe that way it runs on windows too?" **What GPUI offers (checked 2026-10-07 via current docs):** a GPU-accelerated hybrid immediate/retained-mode Rust UI framework (Zed's); runs on macOS (Apple silicon + Intel), Linux (Wayland/X11) and Windows x86_64; a component kit exists (longbridge gpui-component). **Web content is the weak spot:** the WebView (Wry-based) is experimental, macOS + Windows only, and renders on top of all GPUI content — the graph is d3-force in a WKWebView today and **G109 rules that d3-force stays**, so a port must either keep a web view for the graph (in its own layer/window) or re-implement the graph natively (G109 would need revisiting). **What a port would cost or lose:** (1) the SwiftUI app and its ~3,000 Swift tests; (2) native macOS integration the app relies on — EventKit (Calendar), Contacts, Apple Notes via osascript, Safari/Chrome reading under the app's own permissions (the backend never reads ~/Library), the menu-bar worm, Liquid Glass chrome, the updater, login items — each needs Rust bindings or stays Swift; (3) direction D's SF type/motion details. CLAUDE.md's key decision "SwiftUI + FastAPI" would change (a dated ruling). **Windows is mostly not a UI problem:** the backend is Python and portable, but macOS-only readers (FDA, EventKit, Contacts, Notes, Wispr) have no Windows equivalent, and G187 (Windows evaluated separately) / G181 (app-free server, one writer) already frame Windows. A Windows *client* of the same API could be GPUI (or another toolkit) without porting the macOS app. **Measure first:** is the app slow, and where? G184 profiles energy; the Sleep page went 12% → ~1% CPU with a CALayer sprite fix (#170), and type-as-you-go FTS answers in ~12 ms — collect a perf baseline (launch time, page switch, graph FPS at 2k nodes, memory) before attributing anything to SwiftUI. **Research plan:** a time-boxed spike porting ONE page (Sleep or Clusters) plus the shell to GPUI against the existing API, measured against the SwiftUI page on the same machine; a separate spike of a Windows GPUI client reading/answering through the API only (no ~/Library readers); a report on options (a) full port, (b) SwiftUI stays on macOS + a GPUI Windows/Linux client over the API, (c) status quo. Relates G184/G187/G181/G109/G180/G50. Triage: **RESEARCH** (no build before the spike report and an owner decision). | UNBUILT · P4 |

| G181 | <a id="g181"></a>**Server mode — run the bank on a server (brew or Docker), the laptop app as its client, remote agents contribute and Sleep runs there uninterrupted; the door to a hosted Cicada** (Rodrigo 2026-10-05: "a version for brew or some other installation where i want to run the memory bank in a server for example, and have it synced with my laptop for example that has cicada too but the app. Such as the agents running in a remote server can also contribute to cicada and maybe even run the conoslidation there uninterrupted. This way we leave it open to provide a cicada cloud service for easier setup.") | **Website/access follow-up (2026-10-06):** On supported package/service installation, add verified server install/upgrade commands and harness access instructions to the website through G182/G158. Prove remote/local clients can read consolidated memory and contribute with scoped authentication; do not equate a running backend with a configured harness. [Requirements](../specs/2026-10-06-release-and-integration-acceptance.md). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Python backend can run from source, not a supported app-free server package/service/install lifecycle. Ship one-primary-writer macOS/Linux service with install/upgrade/smoke checks; choose brew/curl/container packaging after CLI. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g181). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 requests a package that runs without SwiftUI on a server and easy brew/curl or comparable installation. Separate portable core/CLI from macOS readers and app lifecycle; define bank/config/secrets locations, authentication, engine options, bundled/downloaded embeddings, service start/upgrade/backup/uninstall. First target a reproducible Linux/macOS headless install and one server bank with many authenticated clients. Choose one reliable primary install route before supporting brew, curl and containers in parallel. Current pyproject and POSIX locks are not proof of an installable server package. G187 owns Windows feasibility; G132 owns replicated/offline sync, G16 multi-person permissions. No multi-active git writers. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Today** one Mac runs everything (the launchd backend, the app, the stdio MCP); G135 lets cloud apps reach this Mac's memory through a tunnel the person runs, but only while the Mac is awake, and Sleep stops when the lid closes. **Install:** `brew install` from a tap (`<owner>/homebrew-cicada`; Homebrew also runs on Linux) puts the backend, the CLI (G180) and the MCP server on the machine, and `brew services start cicada` keeps it running (launchd on macOS, systemd on Linux); a Docker image for VPS hosts. Headless: no app, no `~/Library` reads. **Sync — one writer, not two banks.** The bank is a git repo, but two full stacks pushing and pulling one bank would break "one git writer per bank" and give Sleep two minds. Shape: **the server is the bank's only writer; the laptop app — and its capture hooks and its browser/Notes/folder readers — becomes a client of the server's API** over an authenticated connection (bearer auth exists, G50; G135's capability tokens), with an offline queue on the laptop that flushes on reconnect; the app's Store and SSE sync already run over HTTP. A bank on this Mac stays the default. **Remote agents** on the server or anywhere contribute through the CLI or the MCP, each with its own harness identity in the provenance. **Sleep on the server** runs on its schedule without interruption — and **ruling 4 still binds**: a scheduled run never uses a Claude or ChatGPT plan, so the server needs a metered key or a local model (a run the person starts may use a plan). **Rails kept:** the app-reads-`~/Library` rail stays on the laptop (readers post bytes to the server); the server keeps its own `secrets.env`; the network gates and the ToS rail apply on the server; the remote listener serves only the API/MCP, behind auth. **Hosted Cicada (G92's question):** server mode is its self-hosted form; a hosted service would run one isolated server per person — their own bank directory and git repo, so the filesystem rule survives — and G92 says holding people's memory is a different product to decide deliberately. **To decide:** API client vs git sync for the laptop; the transport (an encrypted overlay like G132's rail, or public TLS); how the app picks "this Mac" or "a server" (Settings → General); backups; several laptops on one server. Relates **G92**, **G135**, **G132**, **G50**, **G76**, **G88**, **G180**, **G182**. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §5: push-only backup is separately bounded from server mode/two-way bank sync. Primary writer pushes committed snapshots under the git lock to the person's configured destination; no pull, automatic merge or force-push. Report divergence/freshness without blocking capture. Privacy/encryption and deliberate restore remain G92/G132 decisions. No remote creation or push is authorized by the research note.** **Previous execution label (historical):** RESEARCH → APPLY in slices · app-free package, P1. | UNBUILT · P2 |

Separate WSL from native compatibility; backend/CLI only, no SwiftUI port or compatibility promise. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g187). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Owner 2026-10-06:** investigate a Windows-capable headless Cicada; SwiftUI is outside scope. Current direct `fcntl` imports and `flock` use (`api/services/episode_ids.py`, also other file locks) are Unix-specific; macOS launchd installers and local capture/readers are not Windows service support. Audit locks/atomic replace/path casing/process launch/git/model wheels, choose a supported Windows execution route (evaluate WSL versus native) and run the same capture/write/provenance/index/consolidation smoke tests in that environment. Define unavailable macOS-only integrations honestly. Deliver a feasibility matrix and a smallest supported install path with G181; do not claim native compatibility from pure-Python packaging. **Previous execution label (historical):** RESEARCH · P1 feasibility; port after supported headless package. | DECISION · P2 |
| G135 | <a id="g135"></a>**Remote connector — any AI app, and the phone, can use this Mac's memory** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "we should have a good connector working for all ai apps like claude, gemini, etc, ideally for phone too") | **Portability follow-up (owner 2026-10-06):** G110 requires project-context continuation in Grok/other supported remote clients as well as local coding harnesses. Deliver the latest selected project/task context through permitted initialize/read surfaces without a manually composed handoff; validate current account/client support before promising automatic behavior. Restricted sources/workspace access, ambiguous project selection and unavailable delivery must be explicit. A remote connection alone is not full context, automatic capture or a transferred native thread. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Separate off-by-default remote MCP listener with hashed scoped expiring tokens exists. Establish desktop/phone compatibility and write races/scopes in actual supported clients; connection is not automatic capture. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g135). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** The remote MCP listener and scoped capability tokens are implemented. Verify current support and setup for each named desktop/iPhone client during G10; do not promise all phone apps accept connectors. Test read, claim write, source restrictions, revocation/expiry and cross-bank isolation. A query-capable connector does not imply automatic session capture. S3/S4 and disclosed remote/local write races remain separate work with G177/G183. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The problem.** A cloud AI app (claude.ai on the web and the phone, ChatGPT developer mode, Perplexity) can only reach a *public HTTPS URL*; it cannot launch a local process. Cicada's only agent door was the stdio MCP server, which talks to the backend over loopback, and `ConnectView` told the person a hosted connector was possible future work. So every conversation outside a local harness on this Mac was invisible to memory, and memory to it. **The exposure class is new** (round-3 spec, Decision 4). G132's rail was "loopback unless behind an encrypted overlay". This row adds a public door under stricter terms: a **separate listener** that serves only MCP and none of the FastAPI routers; **per-connector capability tokens** — hashed at rest, shown once, scoped, expiring, revocable; tools outside a connector's scopes absent from `tools/list`; **no delete tools**; **off by default**; any `Origin` header refused; and **Cicada never opening a tunnel itself** — the person runs Tailscale Funnel or ngrok, Cicada only detects it (R-R1, R-R3, R-R17, R-R18, R-R32). **Owner review of this class is the one decision taken without the owner.** **What shipped (S0–S2, `feat/remote-connector`; the rulings live in the Track R plan, `docs/plans/2026-09-23-remote-connector.md`).** (1) One SSRF rule, `net_guard`, on every server-side fetch of a URL someone else chose — `save_url`, the link backfill, logos — judged by `is_global`, which also closes a tailnet (`100.64/10`) gap in G59's own guard (R-R10). (2) Agent writes name their author and commit alone: a stdio claim used to be stamped with a placeholder author and never committed, so the next `git add -A` writer swept it under its own trailer (the G85 smear, locally); it now commits by itself as `Cicada-Author: <harness>`. The `_commit_media` single-save bug found while planning — every one-link save raised a `TypeError` and was never committed — is fixed in the same slice (R-R11, R-R12, R-R13). (3) One tool implementation, `mcp_tools` behind a `ToolContext`, shared by both servers and proven byte-identical on stdio by a golden fixture (R-R2, R-R14). (4) An explicit remote primer, chosen by the caller and never by a client name (R-R15). (5) The runtime: scopes, server-minted conversation handles, provenance, fenced read replies, writes that wait for Sleep, limits and an ids-only ledger row per call (R-R22…R-R29, R-R36). (6) The door: tokens, the secret link and the bearer header on one verifier, the embedded listener without an access log, reach detection and the loopback management API (R-R16…R-R21, R-R30…R-R33). (7) The app page: Settings → Agents → *On this Mac \| From anywhere* (R-R7, R-R34, R-R35). **Corrections to the research the build rests on** (each changed a task): **eight** test files collided with the `mcp` SDK's package name, not two; an MCP claim was stamped with the literal `"mcp-agentic-write"` placeholder, not the Sleep model — the real defect was that it was never committed; and uvicorn's stock access log writes a secret link's path — which IS the token — to the backend log, so the listener runs a quiet protocol per connection rather than any process-global logging switch. **What stays open.** **S3:** OAuth "approve on your Mac" (client-ID metadata documents, a dynamic-registration fallback, step-up for `record`), which takes the secret out of the URL. **S4:** the read mirror on G132's always-on box. The **live check from claude.ai and from the Claude phone app** — owner-present, and it needs a tunnel the owner runs. ChatGPT and Perplexity on the phone (unverified in vendor docs; the page says so). A dedicated `harness` contributor kind (R-R26) — done in F2-back (R-B9). Per-connector activity (R-R35, via G118 slice 2's viewer). Bank pinning per connector (R-R30). DNS-rebinding pinning for the fetchers (R-R10). The `answer` scope's verdicts commit as `user` without naming the connector (R-R35's note; G116(b) decides whether a session trailer rides). **Round 4 C8 (2026-09-24):** Grok is a link app (R-AG7 — it runs on xAI's servers and registers a server from a chat message); Settings → Agents opens the New connector sheet preselected for Claude, ChatGPT or Grok, the link still shown once in that sheet (R-AG18); an agent's live ✓ reads a connector's `last_used_at`, touched by every authenticated request, so it lights on the app's first `initialize` and still answers with telemetry off (R-AG5). → answers **G132** fork (b) for this path (per-connector tokens) and fork (e) (live HTTP recall); serves **G76** (the "other harnesses" story) and **G91** (a phone read path without an iOS build); depends on **G75** R12 (a primer never names an argument the schema rejects — now per scope set), **G48** (conversation identity; a remote handle is never resumable), **G118** (evidence spans on remote claims). **APPLY** S3 after the owner reviews the exposure class — L, $0 (no LLM on this path; `cicada_ask` spends the person's plan and is opt-in). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · remote implementation exists; client compatibility unverified. | PARTIAL · P2 |
| G92 | <a id="g92"></a>**Onboarding at scale — decide what Cicada IS as a product before optimizing the funnel** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "think how the deployment to onboard users at scale would work") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Local setup/exports and remote scoped tokens exist. Hosted ownership, backup versus sharing, restoration and selective evidence disclosure remain product/security decisions before broad deployment. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g92). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Keep export, full-bank backup, device sync and selective sharing distinct. Public releases contain no bank or credentials. Before any private-remote backup or multi-user hosting, settle destination disclosure, transport/at-rest encryption and restoration semantics. G16 adds scoped contributor permissions; G132 owns replicated-client freshness. No upload is part of this documentation change. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The architecture already makes one strategic choice, and it is unusually good: compute is BYO.** G74(a) runs consolidation through the *user's own* Claude subscription, and embeddings are on-device (EmbeddingGemma). So marginal inference cost per user is **≈ zero** — no GPU bill, no per-token exposure, no rate-limit pooling. Almost no memory product can say that, and it should be treated as the core of any distribution plan rather than an implementation detail. **The tension:** every other design decision is deliberately single-user and local — filesystem as the single source of truth, one git repo per bank, no database, secrets in `~/.cicada`, an MCP server registered per machine, a launchd backend. **A hosted multi-tenant Cicada is a different product**, not a deployment of this one: it would break the storage decision, and it means holding other people's episodic memory on your infrastructure — the highest-sensitivity data class there is, with the correspondingly worst breach story. **Three honest routes, pick deliberately:** (a) **Local-first distribution** — signed + notarized `.dmg` (Apple Developer Program, notarization, an update channel), G76's paste-prompt install for the developer audience, everything stays on the user's machine. Cheapest, keeps every architectural claim intact, ceiling is "power users who can install things". (b) **Local-first + optional hosted sync** — E2E-encrypted bank sync/backup and a mobile client that reads it; the server never holds plaintext, so the storage decision survives. This is the only route where a subscription is honest, because you would be charging for *convenience and continuity*, not for inference the user already pays for. (c) **Hosted** — abandons the thesis claim; note it and move on unless the goal changes. **The real funnel is permissions, not marketing** — each of these is a drop-off point and all are already known: Gatekeeper on an unsigned app (**G88**), TCC prompts for Contacts (**G81**) and Notes, per-client MCP registration (**G76**), and a Claude/Codex login the user must already have. Instrument nothing until the route is chosen; the install is the funnel. **Prerequisites the 2026-09-01 portability sweep surfaced, neither named elsewhere:** the human observer id is the literal `rodrigo` (`agentic_write.py:325,:331-333`; no `CICADA_USER`/owner setting exists in `config.py`), so a second user's manual claims are silently NOT `user_stated`; and a bank has no manifest, schema version, or export/import-as-archive (`bank_registry.py`/`routers/banks.py` → 0 hits for `manifest\|schema_version`; `POST /banks/{name}/import` imports a chat export, not a bank), while `repos:` stores tilde paths + `socket.gethostname()` device ids with no remap table (`local_refs.py:64-87`) — a bank moved to another machine keeps its pages but loses live repo resolution. The shareable artifact that needs none of this is a compiled skill bundle (**G112**). → depends on **G74(a)** (BYO compute is the premise), **G88**, **G76**; constrains **G81**, **G91**; the D1 bank-export line of `context-passport-roadmap.md` is where the manifest belongs. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §5: G132/G181's push-only backup does not settle disclosure. A private hosted git remote is not E2E encryption and may hold complete history. Decide owner-controlled storage vs explicitly accepted plaintext hosting vs encrypted archive before implementation/upload; retain this row's product/privacy decisions.** **Previous execution label (historical):** DECIDE / APPLY · privacy boundaries for release, hosting and sharing. | DECISION · P2 |
| G143 | <a id="g143"></a>**Open at login and a background service the app can install** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24, round-4 brief, paraphrased: everything should keep syncing while the app is open or in the background, so Cicada must be allowed to run in the background) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Login-item and launchd backend install/status/quiet launch exist. A signed downloaded build's persistence and retiring the old developer login path still require release acceptance. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g143). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Launch-at-login and launchd setup are implemented. Transfer clean downloaded-build/start/restart/uninstall acceptance to G182, and server service management to G181. Do not repeat the old macOS startup implementation; investigate only gaps confirmed on current builds. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **What was there (verified at `ecb59c7`).** The only login-item path was `make login-item`'s System Events script (`app/CicadaApp/login_item.sh`); the app imported no `ServiceManagement`. The backend's LaunchAgent plist lived only inside `install.sh` step 6, so an app-only install had no way to get one. `BackendProcess` spawned the venv's `uvicorn` console script through `/usr/bin/env` — the shebang that breaks when the repo moves, which `install.sh`'s own note forbids for the plist. **What shipped (round-4 D3).** `LoginItemService` over `SMAppService.mainApp` behind a seam; it remembers what the person asked for, so an ad-hoc-signed build whose status never reaches `.enabled` says macOS did not keep it rather than showing "off". `scripts/install-backend-agent.sh` is the one source of the plist (idempotent: rewrite, boot out, bootstrap with three tries); `install.sh` calls it behind its healthy-skip guard; the app runs it from Settings → General after the click, argv pinned to its own checkout, `CICADA_CAPTURE=off`, then stops only the child uvicorn it spawned so launchd's KeepAlive binds the port. Status is a read-only `launchctl print`. `BackendProcess` spawns `python -m uvicorn`. **Answered (round 4 phase B, R-OB18):** Cicada starts quietly at login — a launch macOS makes as a login item opens no window; the menu-bar bookworm, the watchers and the sync engine run, the Dock icon stays, and a Dock click, the menu bar's *Open Cicada*, a reminder tap or a file open brings the window through `AppRouter.showMainWindow()`. `LaunchKind.from(event:)` reads the 'oapp' event in `applicationDidFinishLaunching` (the only moment it is current); an unknown launch is never quiet, and a window the person asked for is never closed. F-06 and Settings → General say it ("Starts quietly: no window, just the bookworm in the menu bar", or "…just Cicada in the Dock" with the menu bar off). The live check needs no logout: a `-CicadaLaunchKind loginItem` launch argument (read from the process arguments, never defaults) can only make a launch quieter, so the orchestrator can exercise the quiet path on the installed build and a plain launch is unchanged. **Open:** a signed build to confirm the login item persists; retiring `make login-item` once it does. → relates **G88**, **G76**, **G117**. **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · app startup exists; release lifecycle verification remains. | PARTIAL · P2 |

[Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g138). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Reviewed recommendations and installer routes already exist in api/data/recommended_skills.json and skill_catalog.py, including browser-harness and macos-harness. Validate install/detection and reading/watching handoff on clean supported harnesses. Ship recommendations or license-permitted artifacts with clear prerequisites and opt-in installation, not arbitrary executable bundles. Video capability is G162/G166. A server does not acquire a person’s desktop/browser permissions; the local agent performs those actions. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Why a catalog:** Claude Code loads every installed skill's description into context (~1% of the window, ≤ 1,536 characters each), so the right number to recommend is few, reviewed, and ones that feed Cicada. **What ships (Track O, server half — Task 7):** `api/data/recommended_skills.json` — nine entries (watch, paper-lookup, the Wispr Flow and Granola MCP servers, skill-creator, the arXiv MCP server, Anthropic's pdf, literature-review, OpenAI's transcribe), each with its source, licence, the reviewed commit and SKILL.md hash, what it needs, which agents, a terms note (YouTube's for `watch`, with "Cicada itself never downloads video"), and the Cicada tool it bridges; `scripts/verify-skills.sh` re-checks them (every page, commit and SKILL.md hash OK on 2026-09-23). `GET /skills/recommended` returns at most five not-installed entries by rank plus the installed ones; state is derived per request from `SKILL.md` files and Claude Code plugin ids — no agent config is opened, MCP entries read `unknown`. Installers follow upstream per agent (Claude Code plugins, `npx skills add` pinned to the reviewed commit, `claude/codex mcp add`); hosted MCP servers are copy-only (their OAuth is the agent's). The backend never installs anything. The handshake gains at most one line per installed, active bridge (`papers → cicada_save_url(url)` today; `CONTRACT_VERSION` 4 — the branch's 3 collided with G140's 3 at the merge of `dev`, so it moved one past both; remote stays at 2, since a remote connection carries no bridge — and the stdio cache key carries the bridge set's hash beside G140's timezone; R12 holds every bridge line against the stdio schema). **Role skills (2026-09-30, G166):** entries may carry `roles` (`reading` \| `watching`), an `invoke` name, a `pageName`, a catalog-authored `pageSummary` and a `reach` line, and the install method **`agent-prompt`** (copy-only: a reviewed text for the person's own agent to run upstream's installer, so a skill whose CLI matters is never half-installed); `browser-harness` and `macos-harness` join at ranks 10 and 11 (pinned, SKILL.md hashes verified by `scripts/verify-skills.sh`, whose `--print-urls` and root-path join are fixed) and are chosen in Settings → Reading the web, How your agent reads (`agent_methods`), not on this page. Rulings R-O23…R-O29 in `docs/plans/2026-09-23-settings-v3.md`. **App half (Task 8):** Settings → Skills — Cicada's own `cicada` and `cicada-librarian` written by `SkillInstaller` into `~/.claude/skills/` or `~/.agents/skills/` with a `.cicada-managed.json` marker (never over a copy that no longer matches it; removes only what it wrote); at most five recommended cards (a client-side cap too) opening a detail sub-page; a consent sheet showing the exact resolved command, the Install button waiting on a checkbox; the installer runs as an argv (never a shell), allowlisted to `claude`/`codex`/`npx`, `CICADA_CAPTURE=off` forced, stdin closed, 300 s cap, terminated on cancel. A lint holds the skill-folder paths to that one file. **Bridges (F2-back R-B14):** `watch → cicada_record_watch` and meetings → `cicada_save_episode` (one `speaker:<name>:` line per utterance, never `user:`) are active; documents stays off — no evidence kind names a document's author, and an unmarked episode reads as the owner's. **Not verified:** that re-adding an already-added Claude Code marketplace exits non-zero (the step tolerates either); the `installed_plugins.json` shape beyond its top-level keys (two accepted, anything else reads as not installed). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · recommended catalog implemented; fresh-install checks remain. | PARTIAL · P2 |

But for now i want to use the macos-harness, the browser-harness and claude-video to watch videos, as selections in settings, amongst the other default options models can use through their harnesses.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Catalog role/method selection exists; arbitrary Add/import of user skills and validation are not implemented by that selector. G138 owns reviewed recommendations; this row owns adding/importing an existing user skill, choosing reading/watching roles, validation, permissions and removal. Keep imported installed skills separate from automatically learned G112/G55 procedures. Do not auto-execute an imported skill or make desktop-only tools server dependencies. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Now:** `agent_methods.JOBS` is a closed table (`reading` and `watching`, both shipped 2026-09-30; the person picks `watch`, `browser-harness` or `macos-harness` for watching from the catalog, one choice per job) and the skills a job offers are catalog entries with a reviewed pin (G138). The choice is a standing instruction to the person's own agent, never authority, so it changes what Cicada *says*, not what Cicada *does*. **The ask:** a person can add a skill of their own (a folder with a `SKILL.md`, a git URL, or a name their agent already has), tell Cicada which jobs it serves, and pick it in the same "How your agent reads / watches" selections, with a page in the graph tagged `agent-skill` like the shipped ones. **Constraints any design must keep:** (1) the backend never installs or runs anything and reads no agent config beyond the `SKILL.md` presence check (R-O24, R-O26, R-O27); (2) a person-added skill has no reviewed commit or hash, so it is shown as "added by you", never as reviewed, and its terms are the person's to read; (3) the clause names the skill only as data the person typed (the neutral-copy lint scans templates, not data); (4) a "job" the person invents needs a tool contract (what the agent should call to record the result), so v1 is *which skill*, not *which new job*; (5) the page it gets is created by the person's own action (`Cicada-Author: user`), not imported silently from a folder scan; (6) no personal data in the repo: the example is a placeholder skill name, real values redacted. **Open questions (DECIDE):** where the list lives (`$CICADA_HOME`, machine-wide, vs a bank file, since a skill folder is machine-specific); whether a person-added skill may also serve as a *bridge* (record tools) or only as a method; import from Claude Code's or Codex's own skill folders (a listing of names only) vs a typed name; per-agent choice (a skill installed for one harness only). | G112 | <a id="g112"></a>**Portable skills — compile procedural memory into SKILL.md bundles anyone can load on their own plan** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, on WikiSkill: "relevant to the capture of skill learning and make it as portable as possible so I can have other people use it on their own with their ai plans and stuff"; the Era-of-Experience framing now in CLAUDE.md) | **Tool-call capture — kept for future RESEARCH (owner 2026-10-07).** Offered capturing agents' tool calls as procedural evidence for this row, the owner kept the G105 rule (the person's turns + the agent's final replies only) and asked that the idea stay here for later research: "maybe the tool stuff keep it in the backlog to further research in the future". The sketch to evaluate then: per session, an *action trace* of calls, not outputs — tool name, scrubbed and capped arguments, success/exit code, repo-relative files touched, time — as its own evidence kind, never the person's words; raw output stays in the harness transcript, referenced by session id and position, never copied into a bank; Stage 4 and this row would read only the trace to find repeated successful procedures. Open risks to settle first: secrets in arguments, size on long sessions, Sleep cost, and the G105 seam (transcripts are read only by `transcript_capture`). Not scheduled; revisit on the owner's word. **Start-prompts linkage (owner 2026-10-06):** G73 owns raw objective briefs, candidates, variants and user-facing reuse. This row may compile a validated start prompt into a cited portable skill with declared prerequisites and use evidence; prompt save/copy never waits for the compiler. Preserve original/variant provenance and model/project limitations. [Start-prompts acceptance](../specs/2026-10-06-start-prompts.md). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Pattern detection writes new prose skill pages only; existing pages are skipped and source_episodes/related are empty. Ground and update skills, then compile cited SKILL.md/artifact bundles that work in another harness. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g112). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Keep learned procedural memory distinct from the installed-skill catalog (G138/G178). Compile grounded experience into standalone SKILL.md bundles plus versioned artifacts, with usable provenance, declared prerequisites and no dependency on Cicada’s runtime to read them. Include incremental grounded updates rather than create-only behavior. Acceptance: a different harness/model family reuses a synthetic SQL/script artifact safely; stale or failed artifacts take the R2 correction path (G55). **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **What exists today (verified 2026-09-01, five-agent grounding sweep, critic spot-checked):** `skill` is producible by Stage 1 (`schemas.py:45-56`; `entity_extractor.py:56,:72,:96`) and by Stage 4 (`skill_extractor.detect_patterns` :57-101 — one LLM call over this cycle's changes + pairs co-occurring in ≥3 episodes, output `{skills:[{name, description, evidence_entities, confidence}]}`), and its only metric is a count (`skills_detected`, `schemas.py:946` → the Sleep-view chip). **The Stage-4 writer is create-only and ungrounded:** `inbox_generator.py:248-270` writes a page only `if not skill_path.exists()` (:254), dedups on `sanitize_id(name)` alone (:252), stamps `source_episodes: []` / `tags: []` / `related: []` (:265-267), body = the raw description (:270), and DISCARDS `evidence_entities`. Stage-4 output bypasses `entity_resolver` and conflict detection entirely (`sleep_cycle.py:781-786,:819-824`), so a re-detected pattern is a silent no-op that never bumps `last_referenced`, a near-duplicate name makes a second page, a contradicting preference is never a conflict, and the page only ever moves via durable decay (`conflict_resolver.py:146-232`) into the generic "No recent mentions" nudge. Contrast the Stage-1 branch (`conflict_resolver.py:257-317`), which stamps episodes, composes a v2 body and merges on update; `source_rewrite` cannot re-ground a skill because its `source_episodes` is empty. **Nothing exports skills:** the only installable skills are two hand-written files (root `SKILL.md` = recall policy; `skills/cicada-librarian/SKILL.md` = consolidation loop) — neither is generated from memory, `install.sh --skill` (:339-347) and `ConnectView.swift:75-76` copy only the root one, nothing installs `cicada-librarian`. `_procedures/` and `_preferences.md` are scaffolded in every bank (`bank_registry.py:45-55,:228-234`) and read by nothing; MCP `initialize` carries no `instructions` (`mcp/server.py:413-431`); `cicada_write_claim` cannot create a `skill` page (`agentic_write._infer_entity_type` :48-79 has no skill signal). `context-passport-roadmap.md:22` rates Skills "✅ Strong" — an overstatement; the honest rating is *captured, not grounded, not exported* (corrected in that file). **Why this is not G72:** G72 is the inventory + lifecycle of skills the user already HAS on disk (WikiSkill's raw→wiki→skills loop, R2's gate, the negative-transfer finding). G112 is the third layer WikiSkill names and Cicada lacks: compiling what memory has learned INTO skills, and shipping them so someone else can load them on their own plan. **Design.** (1) **Ground the skill page first ($0):** route Stage-4 output through `entity_resolver` like Stage 1; on update merge `source_episodes` from the evidence episodes and bump `last_referenced`; write `evidence_entities` into `related` (and edges); give the page the v2 layout so `source_rewrite` and hubs work on it; let a Stage-4 preference that contradicts an existing skill page raise a normal conflict item. (2) **Compile, don't author:** a deterministic `skill_compiler` turns a `skill` entity into a SKILL.md bundle — frontmatter `name`/`description` written as a TRIGGER block (G76's finding: the description is what a harness matches on), body = the preference/procedure text plus a `## Evidence` list of the claim ids it rests on with their `observer`/`source_trust`, and a `Cicada-Origin:` line naming the bank commit it was compiled from — provenance only, never a runtime dependency. WikiSkill's executor-wiki-access result (−2.8) is the rule: the compiled skill must stand alone on a harness that has never heard of Cicada. (3) **Redact by construction:** a compiled skill carries the RULE, never the episodes — no `source_episodes`, no conversation text, no third-party names unless the user pins them; `## Evidence` cites claim ids, not quotes. That is what makes "other people use it" safe: a skill bundle is shareable where a bank is not. (4) **Surfaces:** `GET /skills/{id}/export` (one bundle) and `GET /skills/export` (zip of every `active` skill with confidence ≥ 0.6); `install.sh --skill` and the Connect page install the compiled bundles beside the two hand-written ones; per-harness targets are the three the ecosystem converged on — `~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, `~/.codex/skills/`, and an `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` block for harnesses without a skills dir. (5) The always-on primer half (top-N preferences <200 tokens at conversation start, `docs/goals/d2-research/procedural-preference-memory.md:9-34`) is NOT this row — it is G75's `initialize.instructions` payload, and it should read the same compiled bundles rather than a second format. **Rails:** a compiled skill is proposed, never pushed (R2's gate; G72's negative-transfer finding); the compiler is deterministic and free; Stage-4 grounding is the one paid change and it reuses the existing call. **Sequencing:** (1) is a bug fix and ships first; (2)+(3)+(4) are one PR; (5) waits for G75. → completes the WikiSkill mapping in **G72** (that row = inventory of existing skills; this = compiling new ones) and the SKILL.md-export line of **G55** (executable procedures ride the same compiler); serves **G76** (install both skills + the compiled ones), **G75** (primer payload), **G92** (a shareable skill bundle is the first zero-bank onboarding artifact), **A4** (what "enrich skill capture" should have meant); consumes **G61** (`sources:`), **G66** (durable class), `entity_body` v2, `source_rewrite`; bounded by **R2** (human gate) and **G48**'s no-transcript-content rail. **2026-09-23 (Track O):** Settings → Skills is being built (G138); a compiled bundle's install will use the same marker-owned writer as `cicada`/`cicada-librarian` (`SkillInstaller`, Task 8). Constraint for the compiler: spec-only frontmatter (`name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools`) with provenance under `metadata: { cicada-origin: <commit> }`, never a top-level key (claude.ai rejects unknown keys); `name` equals the folder name, lowercase-hyphen, ≤ 64 characters. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §3: preserve linked procedural artifacts in standalone bundles. Ground/update skills first: the current writer still creates only absent pages with empty `source_episodes` and `related` (`inbox_generator.py:363-385`). Compile portable links, provenance and deliberate redaction; selected bundles may ride G186. G55 carries verification and R2 execution governance; stored memory is not automatic execution authority.** **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY · learned portable skills, P2. | UNBUILT · P2 |
