| G74 | <a id="g74"></a>**Sleep has no engine — agent-driven consolidation** (Rodrigo 2026-08-31: "sleep is lowkey bs… it has never run because I have no credits; can the same agent contribute to the memory?") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Plan-backed consolidation and deterministic tail steps exist. Tier-0/model-free semantic writing and the larger structural engine ladder are not complete; no API-credit prerequisite for the first trial. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g74). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** The title’s historical “no engine” diagnosis is superseded: provider connections and plan engines exist. Do not make API credits or a new structural engine a prerequisite for G10. Remaining Tier-0 model-free writing and consolidation behavior must still preserve the five-stage claim/provenance/conflict pipeline; a shell skill must not bypass it. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Verified:** the live bank's last real `Sleep cycle` commits are 2026-06-18 — every episode since (imports + today's captures) sits `processed: false`; the LLM stages silently no-op without an engine while the structural steps (migrations, warm_logos, refresh_open_questions) still run. Two concrete engines, both credit-free: **(a) the connected plan (fastest)** — implement G49's reserved `llm_mode="agent"` rung as `claude -p` subprocess calls through the user's already-connected Claude Max (G50 card shows Connected; compliance verified in [`subscription-first-portability.md`](subscription-first-portability.md): official scripted-use mode, token never touched, throttle-detect + stop), so the nightly Sleep just works; **(b) the in-session agent (the user's framing)** — the agent already talking to Cicada over MCP does consolidation itself: a `cicada_consolidate` tool (or librarian skill) hands the agent batches of unprocessed episodes + the extraction contract, the agent returns structured entities/claims through the existing trust-gated `write_claim`/staging paths, `Cicada-Author` records the actual model, G48 stamps the session — the 2026-07-14 "988 episodes → 442 claims" librarian run already proved this path with zero keys. Both rungs record to the G51 ledger (fixing the all-zeros dashboard, which was itself evidence Sleep never ran). Guard rails: engine choice surfaced honestly in the Sleep page ("no engine — connect a plan or run an agent consolidation"), never a silent no-op again — the current silence is the bug. **(Absorbs G80, merged 2026-09-01 — "the deterministic rung — keep the graph growing when no model is available"):** the argument, stated precisely so it isn't misread as "regex beats LLMs" — regex cannot resolve "Mongo"/"MongoDB"/"the database we switched to" (Stage 2's irreducibly semantic job); what a deterministic rung wins on is three **operational** properties: always **available**, **deterministic**, and **cannot hallucinate into versioned memory**. **The key mapping onto the north star:** the *curation* stream (a save, a bookmark, a paper, a `saved-because` reason) is largely pre-structured — the user already did the selecting — while the *experience* stream (chat prose, meetings) is not; so a deterministic rung keeps curation alive with no engine and cannot keep experience-extraction alive, which is exactly the shape of the 2.5-month outage: nothing was lost (`processed: false` is durable), but the product was dead. **Evidence this is cheap, not a rewrite (verified 2026-09-01 by grep): Sleep has 11 stages and only 5 call a model** — Stage 1 extraction, Stage 2 disambiguation, Stage 3 conflict/synthesis, Stage 4 skills, Stage 5.57 link enrichment. The other six already run today with no engine and zero LLM refs: Stage 5 nudge/inbox generation (`inbox_questions.py`), Stage 5.5 wikilink→`mentions` edges, Stage 5.55 media edges, Stage 5.56 the claim layer (`claim_reconciler.py`), Stage 5.6 hub tier + `_index.md`, Stage 5.7 `graph_builder.regenerate_edges_from_claims`; `promote_targets.py` and `vector_index.py` are likewise model-free, and the default embedding backend is on-device (EmbeddingGemma) — semantic clustering is already available with zero engine and zero cost. **What to build (exploration, then a slice):** a **Tier-0 structural write path** running whether or not an engine exists — harvest structure the user already supplied (URLs, domains, page titles, repo/file paths, hashtags, explicit `[[wikilinks]]`, Telegram `saved-because` reasons, platform collection/board names from G71, calendar attendees), recurrence counting against the promotion thresholds, embedding-based near-duplicate candidate generation, and structural decay/versioning (already works). **Tier-1** (still model-free): normalization + fuzzy match proposing `merge_suggestion` inbox items rather than performing them. **Tier-2** stays the LLM's: typed extraction, disambiguation, contradiction detection, pattern distillation. **The honest tension:** consolidation *is* the thesis claim — decay, versioned beliefs and per-belief provenance need a written structured graph, so Cicada cannot adopt zero-LLM writes wholesale without becoming RAG with extra steps; what it should adopt is the discipline that the graph's growth must not depend entirely on a component that can be unavailable. **Open questions:** does a Tier-0 entity earn a distinct `source_trust`/confidence band; does a later LLM pass upgrade a Tier-0 entity in place or supersede it as a claim; should Tier-0 run at capture time rather than only inside Sleep. **2026-09-29:** the plan-engine cycle becomes journal-backed and optionally parallel in **G163** (SL-1 the journal, SL-3 parallel reading); scheduled cycles stay one batch, no small model, no auto-continue (ruling 4 unchanged unless spec Q1 is approved). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · engine ladder exists; Tier-0 / structural work remains. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G117 | <a id="g117"></a>**First-run onboarding in the app — a release blocker, not a polish item** (Rodrigo 2026-09-02: "work on the onboarding for the app… as a pending task for release") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Owner settings, empty states and setup wizard exist; G145/G169 complete later pages/seeding. Verify identity-change reconciliation and fresh-install flow; the conversational interview remains G54. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g117). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Paged Welcome and onboarding are implemented (G145, archived). Keep this row only for unclosed sign-in/consent/demo-owner guards and identity-change behavior; confirm each against current code before implementation. Interview seeding remains G54; seeded owner-page live verification is G169/G10. Historical first-run absence and open-PR labels below are not current state. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** Today a fresh install lands on an empty Graph tab with no bank, no engine, no capture channel and no explanation — the person has to already know that `install.sh` made a launchd backend, that consolidation needs `CICADA_LLM_MODE`, that memory arrives through MCP/Telegram/imports, and that nothing appears until a Sleep cycle runs. Every one of those is discoverable only from CLAUDE.md or the README. **Evidence:** `ContentView.swift` has no first-run branch (grep `firstRun\|Onboarding` → only `ConnectView` and `CommandBox`); Settings holds Agents and Plans & keys but nothing routes a new user there; the demo bank built for the README screenshots (2026-09-02) had to be hand-assembled because nothing in the app can create a populated bank. **Scope (build, $0):** (1) a first-run sheet shown when no bank has entities — three steps, each with live state read from the API: *connect a plan or engine* (`GET /connections`, reuse `ConnectionsView` rows; `auto` picks agent → local → byok). **Rodrigo 2026-09-04 — this step is a real choice, not a checkbox, and the range is the point:** onboarding must present the ways a person can actually pay for consolidation and let them pick one — an **OpenRouter key** (one key, any model, the cheapest way in), a **provider API key** (OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini — the byok rung that exists today), a **subscription they already hold** (Claude Pro/Max through the `claude` CLI, ChatGPT/Codex through its own login — the agent rung, and the one worth defaulting to, since it is money already spent), **Claude with extra usage** (a Max plan that bills overage rather than throttling — the tier override in `PUT /connections/{id}/prefs` already models this and the picker must say what it costs), and **local Ollama** ($0, offline, weakest). Each option states its cost model in one line before it is chosen, not after — the G51 honesty rule ("subscription = flat $/mo, usage = real per-model $, local = free") applied at the moment of choosing rather than in a dashboard nobody opens. The picker is the same surface **G122** puts on the Sleep page; build it once, show it in both places, and let the onboarding step write `CICADA_LLM_MODE` so nobody edits `api/.env` to get started, *pick a capture channel* (`GET /sources/channels`; MCP registration status from `install.sh`, Telegram, an import drop, a bookmarks sync), *run your first Sleep* (`POST /sleep/trigger` with the live `sleep` SSE state, ending on the Graph tab); (2) an empty-state per tab that says what will fill it and offers the one action that does (Graph → import or capture; Inbox → "questions appear after a Sleep"; Feed → add a source; Activity → "nothing recorded yet"); (3) a **demo bank** the sheet can create in one click (`POST /banks` + a seeded synthetic set, reusing the 2026-09-02 demo-bank generator once it is checked in as a script — fictional people/projects only) so the graph is never blank on first launch and the person can try Sleep/inbox/decay before wiring their own life in; (4) the persisted `hasOnboarded` flag lives per bank in `UserDefaults`, and the sheet is re-openable from Settings. **Constraints:** no new dependency; no network beyond the endpoints named; the sheet reads the same `Store` snapshots as every other view (never blank, never a second source of truth); copy follows the Claude Code question rules (one action per step, recommended first, never a modal that blocks an existing user — it only appears when the active bank is empty); portability rails — no owner name, no author-machine path, the demo bank ships with placeholder names exactly as the test fixtures do. **Not in scope:** the paste-prompt install itself (G76), the strategic what-is-Cicada decision (G92 — this row assumes route (a), local-first), the onboarding *interview* that seeds memory from a conversation (G54; a natural step 4 later). → blocks a downloadable 1.0 alongside **G76** and **G90**; consumes **G50** (connections UI), **G62** (channel state); relates **G108** (landing page), **G107** (status mascot as the sheet's guide). APPLY, M **Owner, 2026-09-03 — identity capture:** the first-run sheet also asks the person's name (and optionally handle/email, never sent anywhere) and ties it to the **owner entity** — a `person` page that renders as *Name (you)*, the way your own contact reads in WhatsApp — which replaces the hardcoded `observer: rodrigo` literal (`settings.observer_owner`, see G115/G121) and becomes the `observer` every user-stated claim carries. The demo bank ships with a placeholder owner so the sheet has something to show. **Shipped 2026-09-05 (`feat/onboarding`):** `owner_identity.resolve_observer` (R1's four-rung precedence, `~/.cicada/owner.json`, migration-safe against a pre-G117 `entities/rodrigo.md`) replaces the hardcoded `"rodrigo"` literal at all five sites (`inbox_service`, `telegram_capture`, `agentic_write`, `mcp/server.py`'s tool schema and description); `GET/PUT /settings/owner` writes the owner entity page (`owner: true`, `decay_class: evergreen`) and the app renders it, and its graph node, as "Name (you)" (R2's not-agent-not-external rule keeps every self-stated claim from misrendering as External the moment the resolved observer stops being the bare string `"rodrigo"`); the four-step first-run sheet (identity → engine → one channel → first Sleep) replaces the old single-step Connect guide, re-openable from Settings; every tab that can be empty (Graph, Inbox, Feed, Sources) now says so with one honest action instead of a blank canvas; a checked-in, deterministic `demo_bank.py` (R7 — hand-authored, no LLM, no randomness) backs a one-click `POST /banks/demo` so the sheet is never staring at nothing; three install/copy gaps shipped alongside (`install.sh`'s `-s user`, the README `/sleep/trigger` bearer header, and `sleep_cycle`'s byok failure copy no longer diagnosing a key nobody entered when no engine was ever chosen). **Open remainder, not this row's scope:** the onboarding *interview* that seeds memory from a conversation (**G54**, the natural step 4 later); entity-merge-across-identity-change (R3's disclosed gap — a person who re-onboards under a different display name gets a second page, never reconciled with the old one). **2026-09-29:** the owner-page question left open above is closed by **G169** — every bank now starts with a seeded `owner: true` page ('Name (you)' rendered from the flag, a neutral placeholder sentence, nothing carried from another bank), built in PR #140 (not merged). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · close only outstanding first-run seams. | VERIFY · P3 |
| G133 | <a id="g133"></a>**A watched folder, and papers with why they matter to you** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "in this project i have a bunch of md files explaining ideas, referencing papers, etc. I'd like for this to just live in cicada as well. with papers with summaries and why they are relevant to me") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Watched folders, document parsing and paper personal-reason handling exist. Reproduce garbled synthetic titles and verify citation/source lifecycle; folder context policies are a later named extension. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g133). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** A folder of the owner's own markdown — ideas, plans, reading lists — had no way in except pasting it into a chat, and a paper reached memory only as a bare bookmark with a web preview: no authors, no abstract, nothing saying why it was kept. **What shipped (round 3 Track L; plan `docs/plans/2026-09-23-local-sources.md`, rulings R-LS1 … R-LS30).** (1) *One stager for every source-keyed writer:* the G20 stager moved to `api/services/episode_staging.py` — an `EpisodeDraft` keyed by `source_id`, the hash over the scrubbed body, an edit rewritten in place with `processed: false`, a rename kept by content hash, a deletion tombstoned (`source_deleted_at`), never unlinked; a multi-turn source records G118's `turns: [{offset, ts, speaker}, …]` sidecar (first written here as `turn_index` rows, because `turns:` is the transcript's integer count; the merge with G118 slice 2 kept one key — `evidence.turn_stamps` reads the hook's int as no stamps). (2) *The app reads the folder, the backend parses bytes:* a security-scoped bookmark, FSEvents and an mtime+size+sha manifest post only what changed, with relative paths; the backend never opens the folder. One episode per file, split on H2 only past 48,000 characters. Files under an agent glob (`archive/**`) land `processed: true, processed_by: parser`, `evidence_kind: assistant` — an agent's research sweep is never the owner's words (R-F2) — and flipping a glob re-queues them. The folder anchors a `project` page by name, which learns `paths: [{path, device}]`. (3) *Papers with no LLM:* arXiv ids and DOIs are parsed deterministically into `media` pages with `media.kind: paper` — no new type, the G2 closure held — keyed `media-arxiv-<id>` / `media-doi-<hash>`, an existing bookmark upgraded in place. The owner's own line about a paper becomes a `saved-because` claim with a span into his file, and its section an `about` link through the zero-LLM matcher: that span *is* the why-it-matters. Details come from the arXiv API (≤ 50 ids a request, ≥ 3 s apart) and Crossref (public pool, no email sent), never from arxiv.org pages, under the ToS rail; the abstract is a world-tier `describes` claim (`external`, authored by `cicada`) shown *under* the personal tier (G121). A paper no file cites any more gets a G129-shaped `removal` item, never a silent delete. Built and tested on synthetic fixtures only; the owner's folder is ingested after merge and its counts are not recorded here (privacy rule). **Open:** per-folder filtering of the source detail page (every folder row shares the `folder` origin); no backfill of `turn_index` on older episodes (readers accept its absence). **F1:** paper claims stored `folder:<id>:<section>` as their context, which the M5b facet rule turned into two empty graph satellites per annotated paper and a raw legend row. They now write `general` with unchanged ids (the id names the slot). A folder sync projects their `cited-in`/`about` edges, so a paper sits by its project before any Sleep cycle, and a one-shot `cicada`-authored repair migrated existing banks (R-FX4 … R-FX7). *Revisit:* a per-folder context the owner picks. **F2-back:** a rules change re-derived only the files the app re-posted; saving rules now re-derives every existing episode in place (R-B6, R-B7). A belief Sleep formed from a file later declared an agent's keeps its minted span kind — the owner's call (R-B8). **DS-3b:** 'Written by an agent' is a subfolder checklist, not a typed glob. **2026-09-29:** `papers.never_scraped` and the paper and video hand-offs are restated as the reading-the-web spec's rail (**G164**); a paper or video link found inside an imported chat (**G165**) is routed to those hand-offs, never fetched by the Reader. **Previous execution label (historical):** ✅ round 3. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G134 | <a id="g134"></a>**Note-takers as a source, Wispr Flow first — meetings keep their speakers, dictation only when asked** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "make sure to support note taker apps like wisprflow or others.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Wispr speaker capture, dictation opt-in and deferred todo claim replay exist. Todos-to-Inbox, other note-taker adapters and recording consent remain; old wording must not ask to rebuild todo claim replay. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g134). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** G95 named meetings the one intake class the pipeline was not built for: every episode was single-observer, so a pasted transcript would have credited every line to the owner. **What shipped (round 3 Track L, R-LS21 … R-LS24).** The app opens Wispr Flow's local SQLite read-only (one transaction, a busy timeout) through a column whitelist held on **both** sides — audio, screenshots, accessibility text, pasted text and raw ASR are never selected, and the tests plant canaries in every one — and posts a projection; the backend parses. Meetings and notes by default; dictation history is opt-in, grouped per UTC day and merged server-side through the day's marker lines and `turns` sidecar, with password-manager apps denied. An utterance is a `speaker:<label>:` line and a new evidence kind, `speaker`; it is `user` evidence **only** when its label is one of the owner's listed names. Wispr's own summary, notes and to-dos are `assistant:` lines (a model wrote them), and a to-do becomes a `committed-to` claim on the owner page at `observer: agent`, 0.5. Secrets and one-time codes are scrubbed on **every** episode writer by one module, `episode_scrub.py`, pinned by a test that fails for any writer minting an id without it (R-N3). Settings → Integrations gains **Voice & meetings** and the Sources grid VOICE & MEETINGS. **Open, on purpose:** to-dos into the Inbox (needs a `todo` inbox kind — the G60/G113 surfaces); Granola, Fireflies, Otter and Notion adapters (they reuse the `speaker:` seam; Wispr Flow's own Granola import covers a migrated person today); a consent surface for recording other people (G95). **Previous execution label (historical):** ✅ round 3. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G142 | <a id="g142"></a>**Apple Calendar through EventKit — every calendar on the Mac, not only the ones with an ICS link** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24, round 4 brief: "turn on whatever already works including calendars") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** EventKit app reader and byte-posted calendar parsing exist. Real occurrence/privacy/source attribution acceptance remains; Google account OAuth/event citations are G155, not implied by EventKit. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g142). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** Calendars reached Cicada only as ICS subscriptions (`calendar_registry.py`). A calendar with no published ICS link never arrived, and even a published one needed its URL found and pasted per calendar. The Mac's Calendar app already holds every account. **The design (round 4 D2, contract C6).** The app reads EventKit after one standard macOS prompt: every account, a rolling window, re-read on `EKEventStoreChanged`. The backend never opens `~/Library` (the Awake rail). The app posts the WHOLE window to `POST /sources/calendar-local/sync`. `calendar_local.py` stages each event through `episode_staging` keyed `calendar-local:<id>` (the external identifier, plus the occurrence start for a recurring event), so an edit rewrites its episode in place and re-queues it. An event gone from the window is tombstoned (`source_deleted_at`), never deleted, but only for a calendar the request named. Notes are scrubbed first and then cut at 2,000 characters, so a passcode straddling the cut is redacted whole. A link keeps scheme, host and path only (a meeting passcode lives in the query). Attendees stop at 50. A demo bank gets 409. Each sync makes one `user` commit (`capture/calendar`), scoped to the episodes it wrote. `GET /sources/channels` always lists `calendar-local` ("Apple Calendar"), and the Sources catalog has its row. ICS subscriptions are unchanged. **Open:** a recurring event is one episode per occurrence in the window, so watch the episode count after the owner's first sync; attendees stay names in text, and Sleep's promotion rules decide whether one becomes a person page. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ backend built (feat/r4-foundations-back); the EventKit reader ships on feat/r4-foundations-app. | VERIFY · P3 |
| G150 | <a id="g150"></a>**Backlogs live in memory — a project's backlog is first-class memory: filed by agents over MCP and by the person in the app, shown on the Projects page, found by ⌘K, every note signed** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "this back and forth where i tell an agent to put it in the backlog, that backlog itself i believe should go in cicada project memory, dont you think? with the brief task and an appended notes/description and maybe some other things that might be relevant to how we show and store them.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Per-project backlog files, governed writers, proposal triage and app views exist. Remaining description/link/order editing and repository-public mirroring need separate slices/decisions, not a new entity type. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g150). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The problem.** "Put it in the backlog" meant an agent editing a markdown table in some repository — this file is the example — invisible to Cicada, to every other agent and to the person's own app, with nothing saying who added a row or a finding. **The design** (round 4; plan `docs/plans/2026-09-24-r4-backlog-in-memory.md`, rulings R-B1…R-B27). One markdown file per item at `<bank>/backlog/<project-id>/<item-id>.md`: `id`, `title` (the brief task), `project`, `status` (open · doing · done · dropped), `triage` (apply · research · decide), `paid` (💸), `created`/`updated`, `added_by`, `session`, `links`, `order`, and a `notes: [{at, by, session}]` sidecar; `## Description` holds the reasoning and `## Notes` append-only `### <day> · <who>` entries — a status move is itself a signed note, and a note is edited only through git history. Never an entity page: never extracted, never decays, and the writer never touches `entities/`. Ids are `<PREFIX><n>` like G-ids — the page's `backlog_prefix:`, else the prefix the project's items share (an imported G-row list continues its sequence), else the name's initials (`Orchard` → `ORC`) — max+1 (G114), claimed by an exclusive create. **One writer**, `api/services/backlog.py`, behind REST (`GET/POST /projects/{id}/backlog`, `GET/PATCH /backlog/{project}/{item}`, `POST …/notes`, `POST /projects/{id}/backlog/import`; 409 while Sleep runs; `Cicada-Author: user`), MCP (`cicada_backlog` read; `cicada_add_backlog_item` and `cicada_add_backlog_note` record — the harness as author, the conversation as `Cicada-Session:`, refused while Sleep runs and in a demo bank; a remote connection without `sources` never reads the person's own words), the importer and the demo. Everything is scrubbed; a second open item for one idea is refused and pointed at the first. The contract (CONTRACT_VERSION 7) tells an agent to file the brief task and the reasoning when the person says "put it in the backlog", and later findings as notes on the same item; `_state.md` v4 counts each project's open items for the primer's Current line; `cicada_project` lists the top five. The Projects page's Backlog section (Open · Doing · Done · All) opens an item as the third column; ⌘K has a Backlog group. `backlog_import.py` and `scripts/import-backlog.sh` file G-row tables and `### G<n>` sections keeping ids and statuses — an id already filed is skipped, so a second run changes nothing. **Open.** (1) v2: Sleep's Stage 1 proposes an inbox item "Add to the backlog?" when a conversation says so and no agent filed it, graded by G113 like every resolution. (2) A note's model and effort: the wire carries `authorModel`/`authorEffort` (null in v1); round 4's per-turn join fills them. (3) Whether a backlog write should count as a mention of its project for decay — v1 says no. (4) **DECIDE (owner):** once this very backlog lives in the owner's bank, does `docs/goals/` in this public repository stay its public mirror — and which way would it sync — or become a pointer? (5) Editing a description, triage, links and order in the app (the PATCH already takes triage, `paid` and links). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ v1 built on `feat/r4-backlog-in-memory`. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G154 | <a id="g154"></a>**Contacts sync — macOS Contacts through one prompt, enriching the people already in memory** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "we should have the ability to sync contacts too") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Contacts opt-in reader and existing-person enrichment exist. Derived identity claims/merge confirmation and deeper matching remain; keep raw contact details outside public docs. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g154). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **This is G81 moving into scope, not a second idea.** **G81** holds the reasoning (identity anchors for `person` pages, dedup on a shared email, `sources:` for people, speaker resolution for meetings) and the privacy rail (a contact is a third party's PII landing in a permanently-historied store); round 4 decision 12 puts it on the Import page (G145, *Calendar & Contacts*). **Shipped (round 4, first slice; plan `2026-09-24-r4-sources-2.md`, R-SR8 … R-SR10, R-SR16):** the **app** reads the Mac's address book through the Contacts framework after one standard prompt (`NSContactsUsageDescription`; `ContactsReader` over a `ContactStore` seam) and posts the whole book to `POST /sources/contacts-local/sync` — per card its names, **which** facts it holds (employer, job title, email, phone, birthday — booleans) and a thumbnail ≤ 64 KB. **No email address, phone number, employer name or note ever crosses the wire or lands in a bank**, which settles G81's open question: there is no value to hash or to leak. The backend (`contacts_local.py`) matches a card to **exactly one** existing `person` page by its given + family name in either order, folded like search, against the page's name and aliases; a one-part name never matches, and two pages sharing a name or two cards on one page are skipped and counted. It **never creates a page**. A match adds G61 sources `{ref: addressbook://<card id>, kind: app, predicate, added_by: cicada}` for `works-at`, `role`, `email`, `phone` and `birthday`; the sync owns exactly those entries (adds, keeps, removes) and never touches one the person or an agent added. The thumbnail is cached at `$CICADA_HOME/pictures/<bank>/contacts/<id>.<jpg\|png>` — `contacts_local.photo_path(bank, id, ext)`, the one path **G146**'s picture ladder reads — never in a bank, and the page carries `contacts_photo: {sha, ext}` so the graph's ETag moves. 409 while Sleep runs (Stage 5 rewrites entity pages); demo-gated; one `user` commit per sync (`capture/contacts`). **Cadence (decided):** on Connect; on launch only when `currentHistoryToken` moved or this bank's last post is a day old; on `CNContactStoreDidChange`, debounced 30 s; on a bank switch and on Sync now. An empty read is never posted. **A deleted card:** its entries leave the page on the next sync and git keeps the history; nothing else was stored. **Next slices:** claims with a `contacts` origin for employer, role and birthday values (the values would then cross the wire — decide that rail first); a card shared by two person pages as a `merge_suggestion`; nicknames; the notes field (Apple's `com.apple.developer.contacts.notes` entitlement); postal addresses, relations and social profiles; speaker labels for meetings (**G134**). Telemetry ids only. **On onboarding's Import page (G145 seam 4, 2026-09-25):** a *Contacts* row under *Calendar & contacts* — its tick is `ContactsReader.connect()` (the one prompt, then the first read), its untick `disconnect()`, and its idle line says macOS asks once and no page is made per contact. → builds **G81**; feeds **G146**, **G159**. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ first slice (round 4) · 🔲 claims, merges. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G160 | <a id="g160"></a>**Browser tab groups — what the person is working on right now** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "some browsers like chrome support making tab groups. if we are able to detect this, awesome too, gives information too") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Tab-group reader and stable snapshot episode staging exist. Validate observational attribution and useful current-work semantics; an open tab is not a personal assertion or evidence of a completed task. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g160). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Shipped (round 4, first slice):** Chrome's OPEN groups, read by the app from the default profile's `Sessions/` SNSS file (commands 25 and 27 plus each member tab's current navigation; the metadata pickle grew in M88 and M113, so the reader stops cleanly at a short pickle), behind their own switch (`cicada.browserWatch.enabled.chrome-tab-groups`, off until turned on), FSEvents debounced 10 s and floored at 60 s, a snapshot digest per bank; `POST /sources/tab-groups/sync` stages one snapshot episode per group (identity: saved guid, else folded title + colour, else the session token), rewritten in place, tombstoned per browser and profile. Only clear version-3 files that carry the initial-state marker are read; incognito is never on disk; no page contents. **Follow-ups, in order:** (1) **Chrome's session encryption** — `kEncryptSessionStorage` is on by default at stage `write_both_read_only_clear` (clear files still written); a later stage stops writing clear files, and then this reader has nothing to read: the trigger is the row saying so on a real install, and the answer is not decryption (that is Chrome's Keychain key) but saved groups (2). (2) **Saved and synced groups** — the profile's `Sync Data/LevelDB` (`saved_tab_group-dt-<uuid>` protobuf rows; copy the directory, open the copy read-only; the saved colour enum is offset by one from the live one). (3) **Brave, Comet and Dia** — likely Chrome's session code unchanged; verify on a real install, then add each to `tab_groups.BROWSERS` and `BrowserReadable.tabGroups`. Vivaldi's tab stacks are its own format. (4) **Safari Tab Groups** — `SafariTabs.db` in Safari's container (Full Disk Access; the macOS schema needs checking against a real file). (5) **Arc** folders (`StorableSidebar.json`, no colour per folder), **Firefox** (`sessionstore.jsonlz4`, mozLZ4 + JSON), **Edge** Workspaces (mid-migration — skip until settled). (6) The product question the first slice leaves open: whether a group whose name matches a project feeds that project's `now` (G141) instead of, or beside, its own episode. (7) **Whose words a tab title is** — the snapshot has no turn markers, so `evidence.speaker_kind` reads every line as `user`; the group's name is the person's, a tab's page title is not (the same gap calendar and bookmark episodes carry). Decide whether a snapshot source declares a kind before a chip says "You said" over a page title. **On onboarding's Import page (G145 seam 4, 2026-09-25):** *Open tab groups* is a sub-row under Chrome with its own tick (`TabGroupWatcher.enable()` / `disable()`, never Chrome's bookmark tick); no Sessions file yet reads "Chrome hasn't saved any open windows on this Mac yet" and keeps the switch on. **Rails:** app reads, backend parses; the switch is its own consent (live work data, distinct from bookmarks); only titles and links; profile selection follows G119. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ first slice (round 4) · 🔲 follow-ups. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G161 | <a id="g161"></a>**What came in, by name — a list under each source of exactly which items it captured** (Rodrigo 2026-09-28: "in the page where i'm syncing everything, i'd like to see a list of the things that where captures, so out of the 229 notes for example, see which ones it detected. Maybe it can be a small list that opens below each thing.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Named channel-item lists and episode rows exist. Saved-link rows intentionally point to media pages; exposing the saving conversation is a separate provenance/navigation seam, not a missing item-list API. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g161). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** Every source row (`SourceRow` — onboarding's Import page, Home's Getting started, Integrations, the Sources detail column) says *how many* came in (`SourceRowText.countLine`, the channel's `parts`), never *which*: "229 notes" cannot be checked against the person's own library, and a wrong import (a folder that should have been excluded, a calendar that should not be read) is invisible until Sleep has read it. **The shape:** a small disclosure under each row listing the items by their own title and day, newest first, a bounded page with "Show all" into the Sources detail column, each row opening its episode in the Reader. **Constraints:** read-only and engine-free; one read endpoint per channel, derived at read from the episodes a channel stages (its `origin` / `source_id` prefix / index), never a Store domain (ETag + in-memory cache, like provenance); titles only on the wire — no bodies, no Contacts facts beyond the matched page's name, ids in `.help`; the same list everywhere a row is drawn. → relates **G124** (Sources page), **G145** (onboarding Import), **G126** (Integrations), **G118** (the Reader). **Shipped (2026-09-28, PR #134):** `GET /sources/channels/{id}/items?offset&limit` (≤ 200, `api/services/channel_items.py`) — derived at read from the set each row's count means: the notes index (the latest episode per note), calendar and tab-group episodes by `source_id` (never a tombstoned one; an event's own day), the bookmark seen-set joined to the url index (the browser's media pages when no seen-set exists yet), a channel's media pages by its catalog origins (hidden ones left out, as the Feed does), a chat export's or Wispr Flow's episodes, a folder one row per file; Contacts lists the matched pages' names and nothing from a card. Titles scrubbed and cut to one line of ≤ 120 characters, never a body; ETag over `sources`+`episodes`+`entities` with the shape, channel and page; 404 for a channel the bank does not know. The app's `CapturedItemsList` ("What came in", collapsed and remembered per viewer per channel) sits under onboarding's Import rows (a preview — rows open nothing under the onboarding layer), every Integrations source row, and is the Sources detail column's content for a source without saved links; twenty rows, then "Show more"; a row opens its episode in the Reader, a saved item in the Feed, a person in Clusters; `ChannelItemsCache` is in memory only and emptied on a bank switch. **Disclosed drifts:** the list's `total` is its own — a notes index keeps a note deleted in Notes, a calendar keeps an event that left the window, one page can hold two bookmarked URLs, and `files`' row counts the whole url index while its list is its card's. **Not done:** Home's Getting started rows (the card is transient) and the "Show all" hand-off from a row to the Sources detail column. **Previous execution label (historical):** ✅. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G163 | <a id="g163"></a>**Sleep page v5 — a progress bar you can trust, reading everything that is waiting, and reading faster on purpose** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29: "if we are using the chatgpt plan, maybe even the codex harness or the plan directly we can have an option for parallel subagents with small models so consolidation can happen faster, and a reliable progress bar that shows what has been read and consolidated would be good too." And: "an improved design for the consolidation (sleep) page") | **Incremental-processing linkage (owner 2026-10-06):** Coordinate accepted-reading journal keys and crash/plan-pause recovery with G104 source/turn/chunk revisions. Reusing accepted output after a failed batch differs from skipping already-committed prefixes of a grown thread; cache invalidation and atomic checkpoint advancement must cover both. This does not unpark declined parallel reading. [G104 research](../research/2026-10-06-incremental-consolidation.md). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Drain, pause/continue, committed batches and run history exist. In-progress reading journal is not implemented; Home still needs run-level last-read semantics. Parallel reading was declined and stays conditional, not an automatic priority. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g163). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Why the bar cannot be reliable today (spec §0, by reading the code, not by running it).** Stages 1–4 write nothing to disk; Stage 5 files everything and marks episodes processed (`sleep_cycle.py:1370-1379`). A cancel, a plan stop, a crash or a restart throws away every paid Stage 1 answer, and a plan stop after some episodes were read also makes Stage 2's first judge call raise. So the fix is the cause, not the paint. **The design ([`2026-09-29-sleep-page-v5-design.md`](../specs/2026-09-29-sleep-page-v5-design.md), revision 2; every critique adopted, none shown wrong).** (1) *A journal* keeps every paid LLM answer a cycle bought, so 'read' and 'filed' are separate durable states that survive a pause, a plan stop, a restart and a crash; it stores an answer only after the calling stage accepts it, only for Sleep calls, with an episode index of chunk keys (what 'read' and 'resumable' count) and the model that wrote each claim carried onto the claim (the 'records the model' copy waits on that). (2) *A drain* — 'Read everything' — is a person-started run over a frozen list of what was waiting; a drain hold plus one `is_active()` helper replaces the `status == "running"` checks (scheduler and trigger included); the drain is pinned to its bank with a fixed-bank `Settings`, `activate` answers 409 while a drain is reading, failures map by exception class (an empty answer is a content failure, not 'signed out'), a 3-attempt cap and a no-progress pause keep it finite, and both decay engines and the in-cycle link reads are skipped per batch and run once at the end (a backdated import must not decay mid-drain, see **G170**). (3) *Triggers:* a no-body `POST /sleep/trigger` is one batch from every door; a drain starts only from the sheet's body and continues only through `POST /sleep/run/continue`. (4) *Defaults:* one batch of 25, no body sent, `workers=3`, no small model, no auto-continue; parallel reading, the small model and auto-continue are opt-in and never run on a schedule. (5) *Reserve:* covers Claude's weekly window when the stream reports it; copy says the run 'stops starting new reads; running calls finish'; overshoot is unmeasured on both plans, so 6 and 10 workers are not offered until measured (EX-2). (6) *Usage folded in:* drain cost sums `llm_call` rows by `drain_id`; the call count is a fact; plan percentages stay on the engine menu and Details only (ruling 12 not widened); `PriceLintTests` is new work. (7) *First run:* the owner's page (see **G169**) fills visibly — after the first batch the run stops to ask ('your page has N beliefs, read the rest?'), a count of claims on the `owner: true` page, engine-free. **Slices:** SL-1 the journal, accept protocol, episode index, per-episode outcomes, authorship stamp and honest bar; SL-2 the drain controller, hold, sheet and pauses; SL-3 parallel reading (gated on EX-2, EX-3, EX-5, the authorship stamp and the owner's answers); SL-4 the drain-tagged cost summary; SL-5 later (read-ahead, per-stage model map, re-reads). Experiments EX-1…EX-5 run before any default is chosen. **Risks added by the review:** R-14 `activate` is refused during a drain; R-15 a weekly reserve is unenforceable when the stream does not report it; R-16 a stage that forgets `accept` means no replay (fails safe). **Open questions for the owner (spec §8):** Q1 may a run continue itself after a plan reset — a ruling 4 amendment, recommend yes as a per-run off-by-default checkbox, two continuations, 36 hours, never scheduled; Q1b is a first-run drain on a consumer plan acceptable at all (order of 12,000–21,000 judge calls for a ~1,200-conversation export, arithmetic on an old figure; vendor terms not re-read); Q2 when may 6 and 10 workers be chosen (only after EX-2 on both plans and sign-off); Q3 which stages use the small model (Read and Sort only); Q4 reserve defaults (Claude 10% on both windows, 25% for a weekly-only ChatGPT window); Q5 keep the Mac awake during a run; Q6 the first drain of a bank starts with one batch and then asks; Q7 may the Sleep page gain run controls beside Consolidate — a G125 R10 amendment; Q8 keep plan figures on Details and the engine menu only. → extends **G107**, **G125** (R10's one trigger), **G51** (ruling 12), **G122**, **G74**, **G104**; the video tail is **G162**. Triage: SL-1, SL-2, SL-4 APPLY; SL-3 DECIDE + measure 💸. **Owner decision 2026-09-29 (TODO ruling 13): "i dont want to cap the max episodes per sleep, why would we cap them? its just progress that cicada has to go through."** This supersedes the design's drain-only-from-a-sheet rule (V5-15/Q7) and its hold / `is_active()` / `run.json` / `continue` machinery: **Consolidate is the drain.** *Built (branch `feat/consolidate-reads-everything`, backend half):* a person-started `POST /sleep/trigger` freezes the waiting ids and reads them in batches of `sleep_max_episodes_per_cycle` (default 25, now "how often progress is saved"), each batch filed and committed by Stage 5 (`Sleep cycle <date> (batch k of n)`), so a cancel or a plan stop loses at most the batch in progress; a plan limit is a pause with the vendor's sentence and reset time; scheduled cycles stay one batch (ruling 4); decay and Stage 5.57's page reads run once per drain, the tail once per run; `GET /sleep/status` gains a measured `drain` block (batch k of n, filed of frozen, stop reason); **the app half is built (same branch):** the Sleep page's sentence tail ("Batch k of n · f of frozen filed."), the plan-stop and cancel rungs, Cancel copy that says the batch still reading is dropped, a Swift decode over the pinned `sleep-status-drain.json`, and the bank-switch 409 shown as the server's sentence. **Not built, still design:** the journal (SL-1: a cancel before Stage 5 still discards the batch in progress), the reserve, auto-continue after a plan reset (a ruling 4 amendment), parallel reading (SL-3), the drain-tagged cost summary (SL-4), per-batch grouping in Past nights, and narrowing the 409 guards (**G177**). ~~Known and disclosed: a first batch that always fails is retried first on every run~~ — fixed below (retry once, then park). **Owner decision 2026-09-30 ("I like the sleep agent v5 designs would you be able to apply them? Door: the intake card's Read now reads everything"): the approved boards are applied on top of the drain (backend half built on branch `feat/sleep-v5`, spec §11; the app half follows).** *Built (backend):* (1) **Reading options** — `GET/PUT /sleep/run-options`: batch size 10/25/50 (50 is the ceiling: a commit records at most 50 sessions), the opt-in *continue after a plan reset* switch (off; **TODO ruling 15**), the *leave room in my plan* reserve (off), each snapshotted when a run starts. (2) **Scheduled cycles read everything waiting too** (**ruling 16**; ruling 4 unchanged — still no plan, and an unattended run on a key spends with no limit Cicada sets, said in words); the scheduler reads nothing while a paused run waits (its engine-free tail still runs, review fix 2026-09-30) and counts *readable* conversations. (3) **Per-conversation outcomes** — Stage 1's swallowed failures are classified by exception class (`sleep_drain.classify_episode`): the engine's trouble (signed out, throttled, exhausted, model not found, an unnamed CLI failure, a provider 5xx or refused request, the network — and a batch where every conversation failed with an unrecognised error) stops the run after the batch commits what it read and is never counted against a conversation; a conversation's own (empty answer, timeout, unparseable) gets **one more try in the very next batch and is then parked** (`$CICADA_HOME/sleep/<bank>/parked.json`, released when the file changes or on `POST /sleep/parked/retry`). An empty answer from a plan CLI that exited cleanly is now `EngineProtocolError`, not "signed out". (4) **Honest live progress** on `GET /sleep/status`'s `drain` and the SSE event: `calls` (only grows; a discarded batch's calls stay counted), `elapsedMs` / `pausedMs` (measured, never a remaining time), `batchState`, per-stage `stages` (Read / Sort / Decide fill only from finished work; Notice and File carry no number), `byOrigin` (frozen = filed + read + waiting + could-not-be-read + parked + skipped, by construction), a live `arrivedSince`, `ownerPage` beliefs (start / after the first batch), `firstRun`, `startedBy`; `GET /sleep/queue` serves the per-conversation rows from frontmatter only. (5) **Pause / Continue / End this run** — a paused run is a sidecar record (`run.json`), not a state of Sleep (`status` stays `idle`, nothing is held): `POST /sleep/trigger {"continue": true}` resumes it under the same run id with its counters carried, a no-body trigger stays a fresh run, `POST /sleep/run/end` forgets it, a backend restart turns a run that was reading into a paused one (`restart`). (6) **The reserve is a soft stop** — Stage 1 stops *starting* reads at the line, what was read is filed, the rest is not an attempt; a line, not a guarantee, and a window the engine never reports is named `enforced: false`. (7) **Past nights groups by run** — `SleepHistoryEntry` carries `drainId` / `batch` / `batches` and a `run` summary from a machine-local per-run file (never summed from the visible page), and `GET /sleep/runs/{id}` sums cost by `drain_id` over every call. (8) `SleepEnginePreview.billing` (`plan \| charged \| local`) so the copy never names a provider. **Owner decisions applied:** "Read faster" is **not built** (no parallel reading, no small-model map, no "Reading model" or "At once" — SL-3 and EX-1…EX-5 stay open and now need the owner's go); the reserve is off by default and set in the engine menu; continue-after-reset is an opt-in switch, off, a narrow ruling-4 amendment (ruling 15). **Still not built:** the journal (SL-1: a cancel, a Pause or a hard plan rejection before Stage 5 still discards the batch in progress; the board's "Saved reading" section and "read and kept" wording wait for it), parallel reading (SL-3), the app half of all of the above, a spending cap for unattended metered runs, keeping the Mac awake. **Previous execution label (historical):** ◐ drain built 2026-09-29 (backend + app); Sleep page v5 built 2026-09-30, backend and app (the boards on top of the drain: Reading options, Pause / Continue / End this run, the run's counts and stages, per-conversation What's waiting with Retry, Past nights by run, the doors routing to the page while paused, Keep plan free in the engine menu); **still open: the journal (SL-1, "Saved reading") and parallel reading (SL-3 "Read faster", EX-1..EX-5 — the owner declined it 2026-09-30, it waits for his go)**; the app's Home "Last read" and `newestConsolidation` still pick the newest batch, not the run. | PARTIAL · P3 |

So in parallel maybe research about extremely fast scrapers we can implement because we either need the agent's freedom to look at the page itself and even interact if needed, or the scraper that just gives what's on the site. maybe this can be different modes we can offer, and by default we set one...") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Reading settings and a public HTML fetch path exist; the full default Reader ladder is not established just by exposing its selector. Implement/verify the actual public-reader method wiring and page/citation handoff. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g164). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Answer: yes to a fast reader, as the default, and no to giving consolidation a browser** ([`2026-09-29-reading-the-web-design.md`](../specs/2026-09-29-reading-the-web-design.md), revision 2, 22 critiques adopted). **What exists (spec §3, read in code):** one fetch path, `link_enrichment.default_fetch`, on the ToS rail (4 s, ≤ 512 KB, no cookies, never behind auth) — and, found while reading, **no `robots.txt` handling at all**, an OG-plus-bs4 extractor, and a second transport (save-time `media_ingestor.enrich`, `_TIMEOUT` 5 s) the ladder must also cover. **The ladder, on the same bytes:** R0 an honest User-Agent, `Accept: text/markdown` and robots.txt; R1 metadata through one shared parser; R2 main content to markdown (extractor bench: trafilatura recommended over readability-lxml, S1's bench on the person's own saved pages to overrule published numbers); R3 a distinct `needs_js` status (never retried by the clock); R4 an optional local browser engine, later (**G167**). A metadata-tier result is written with no summarizer call. **Two axes, two defaults:** automatic reading defaults to *Reader*; reading with an agent defaults to *Off* (**G166**). **Honesty rules:** `blocked` copy says 'looks again in a month'; `fetch_reader_v` makes `blocked` and `failed:empty_body` pages eligible once after a fix; Settings copy goes through `LeavesMacNote` because a page excerpt does reach the summarizing engine. **Rulings proposed:** R-RW1…R-RW9 plus R-RW10 (robots.txt), R-RW11 (backoff by status) and R-RW12 (nothing unattended without a person's choice); each a dated TODO ruling on approval. **Open questions (spec §14):** D-RW1 approve R-RW1…R-RW3, R-RW10, R-RW11 and the extractor (trafilatura, pinned, today's bs4 extractor as the fallback); D-RW7 honour robots.txt (recommend yes, at the cost of some pages reading `blocked: robots` that read fine today). → extends **G102** (the metadata tier is the 'cheap slice' extended to unread pages), **G61** phase 2 (the reader and the agent queue are its rungs 1 and 2; S4's `default_fetch` reuse becomes the ladder, so G61 S4 and this row share one implementation), **G133** (`never_scraped`, the paper and video hand-offs), **G105**/**G149**. Slices S0 spec and rulings, S1 the ladder, S5 Settings. Triage: APPLY (💸 only through the existing summarizer). **Previous execution label (historical):** ❓ design written 2026-09-29, awaiting the owner's review. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G165 | <a id="g165"></a>**Reading the web, part 2 — links inside imported chats: import stays network-free, pages are minted when read** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29, in the same session: research fast readers "because we either need the agent's freedom ... or the scraper that just gives what's on the site") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Import parsing remains network-free and links can be recorded. Roles and classes decide which links are worth reading (§6.1). **A lane that is actually separate:** 10 a night, only when the person chose *A few each night*, with its own scan; `_candidates` and `scan_backfill` now exclude chat origins; every count is over the readable set, not the raw one. **Consent, stricter than the first draft:** the card offers *Read them when I say* (the default; nothing fetched until Read), *A few each night*, *Don't read links from chats*; the unattended option is never pre-selected, and any import that shows no card (the deprecated shims, an API re-import, a live conversation from the Stop hook) behaves as *Read them when I say* (R-RW12). **The catch-up job:** its own `reading_jobs` registry with a single-flight 409 (not `intake_jobs`), counts from a cached, ETagged `GET /reading/queue` and `GET /episodes/{id}/links`, a progress bar that cannot lie, routes carrying `refuse_capture_into_demo`; the Feed's 'Read' line comes from an additive `MediaSourceItem.read`. **Open questions (spec §14):** D-RW3 the chat-link default when nobody answered (`manual`, as drawn); D-RW4 chat-shared links in the main Feed list (recommend) or behind an origin filter; D-RW6 add `processed_by: cicada` (audit every reader first) or write no media episode for chat-link pages. → extends **G102**, **G164**, **G20**/G118 (the sidecar), **G133**; live conversations are slice S2b. Slices S2 sidecar, S2b live, S3 chat pages and the read job. Triage: APPLY 💸. **Previous execution label (historical):** ❓ design written 2026-09-29, awaiting the owner's review. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G102 | <a id="g102"></a>**Site recon — read a saved page to derive RELATIONS, not to summarize it** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, restating an earlier ask: "from the bookmarks and websites is important to do some recon to see what the site is about to be able to derive relationships with other nodes") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Public link intake and source checks exist. Relation-focused site recon and personal-reason grounding still need an observed case and governed output; fetched summaries alone do not complete the row. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g102). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Partly built, and the gap is specific.** Saved links already get OpenGraph enrichment at ingest (`link_enrichment.py`, `media_ingestor.enrich`) — that is why Feed rows carry titles, sites and favicons. What does **not** exist is reading the page to derive *graph relations*: today a bookmark lands as an island with a title, and nothing connects it to the concepts, tools, people or projects it is actually about. **The framing that makes this worth building: the output is ENTITIES, not a summary.** A summary is a nicer card; extracted entities routed through Stage-2 resolution are new edges — which is what turns 789 saved links from a list into part of the graph, and is a direct prerequisite for **G79**'s cross-stream retrieval ("you saved this three months ago and it answers what you are deciding now" is impossible while saves are unlinked). **Constraints, both learned the hard way here:** (i) it is a network fetch per URL, so it belongs on the **Sleep tail** behind the same opt-out gate as connectors — never at capture time, never blocking a save; (ii) the **ToS rail** applies exactly as it did for LinkedIn and X — some sites may be fetched and parsed, some may not, and the rule is already written down: no scraping behind auth, no circumventing a block. Also relevant: **G86**'s junk-title finding (148 Google consent interstitials collapsed onto one entity) means recon must skip consent/interstitial pages or it will happily extract entities from a cookie banner. **Cheap first slice:** for already-enriched items, run extraction over the OG title+description that is *already stored* — zero new fetches, immediate edges, and it proves the resolution path before any crawling is added. → extends **G47/G71** (the saves that need relating), **G22** (media previews), **Stage 1/2** (extraction + resolution as the consumer); prerequisite for **G70** (design identity) and a direct enabler of **G79**; gated by **G86** (junk titles) and the connector network gate. **Cheap slice shipped (2026-09-02, `feat/link-summaries`).** Corrected finding first: Stage 5.57 already globbed the whole bank — the zero-`describes` result came from the pass running only after Stage 5 (never on an idle night), taking the 20 most-recent pages, and never retrying an `enrichment_attempted` page. Shipped: `link_enrichment.backfill` on the Sleep tail (`link_enrich_backfill_per_cycle`/night, oldest-first, dated fetch backoff, junk retired via `classify_page`) + `POST /maintenance/enrich-links?limit=N`; recon in `link_recon.py` — the existing Stage-1 prompt over stored title+description, 8 links per call, surface-form attribution, `entity_resolver.match_existing` (the Stage-2 judgment without `resolve()`'s creates/clarifications), `about` claims on the media page projected by Stage 5.7, unmatched mentions as pending candidates; `GET /sources` carries `description`/`about`. **Not shipped (this row stays open):** page fetching beyond OG+visible-text (JS-rendered pages, YouTube transcripts, PDFs), any `about` edge from the target's side, in-app recon controls, and relating a link to a *pending* candidate once it promotes (today the candidate carries the link's episode as its source, so promotion backfills context but not the edge — a follow-up). Final review (2026-09-03) closed four of the disclosed gaps: the endpoint now holds a process-local lock (409 on overlap, alongside the R11 Sleep guard); `GET /sources` strips the ```claims fence before excerpting (the row was carrying serialized claim YAML on every backfilled page); recon leaves an existing pending candidate untouched rather than replacing a conversation's provenance with a blurb's; and `llm_calls` counts answered calls only, so an engine abort no longer stamps a model on a zero-LLM commit. Still disclosed, not fixed: a page whose extractor returned a malformed non-engine response is stamped `recon_attempted` after one try — only engine failures (R9) leave pages unmarked. **2026-09-29:** the metadata tier of the Reader ladder (**G164** R1) is this row's 'cheap slice' extended to pages never read, with no summarizer call; links found inside imported chats (**G165**) become saved-page candidates through the same recon. The 2026-09-29 review also found the fetch path has no `robots.txt` handling (R-RW10) and that saved-link ids could collide (**G172**, fixed in PR #139). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ cheap slice shipped. | PARTIAL · P3 |

| G1 | <a id="g1"></a>**Multiple memory banks / "memory projects"** (→ **M6, committed, next-up**) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Named banks and switching exist; cross-bank references requested in the original row are not implemented. Restore the row; coordinate references with G16/G132 rather than implying bank CRUD completes it. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g1). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Several named memory banks so the live consolidated graph is never erased: a **project dropdown in the Memory/graph page** to switch the active bank; **save the current graph under a name**; **create a new (empty/seed) bank** to test against (e.g. a bank seeded from imported chat exports + the big-model M5d consolidation). | G132 | <a id="g132"></a>**Sync across devices — a Claude Code session on the home server lands in the MacBook's bank, and can read it back** (Rodrigo 2026-09-06: "the ability to have a sync across devices … I have a home server, an old laptop I installed Ubuntu on, and I want to be able to have it so that if I have Claude Code sessions running on that server, they sync with the cicada on my MacBook") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Remote clients can reach one bank; replicated multi-device/server banks, offline edits and convergence are not implemented. Start with G181's primary writer; push-only backup is simpler and separate. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g132). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 requests multiple devices/servers. First separate a single headless bank reached by several clients (G181/G135) from replicated banks with offline writes. Do not turn git pull/merge into semantic memory sync: contradictory facts can merge cleanly. Design capture routing, conflict handling, reconnect/freshness and restore with one primary git writer per bank. Push-only private-remote backup is an optional narrower feature, never a pull/force-push mechanism; G92 settles privacy. G16 owns selective multi-person sharing. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **What "one machine" means in the code today, so the row argues from evidence rather than from the word "sync".** The Stop hook posts to a loopback literal with only the port overridable (`api/hooks/capture.py:135`); the endpoint then opens the transcript itself against *its own* home — `harness_root` dispatches to `Path.home()/.claude/projects` (`transcript_capture.py:75`, `session_stats.py:57`) — and `validate_transcript_path` (`transcript_capture.py:94-111`) resolves the path strictly and refuses `not_a_file`/`outside_root` before any read, so a hook firing on another machine cannot be served even if it reached the backend: **a path is meaningless across machines, by construction.** The backend binds loopback everywhere it is started (`install.sh:348`, `api/config.py:127`, `BackendProcess.swift:71`), the app's base URL is a constant (`APIClient.swift:973`), and the MCP server carries three `127.0.0.1:8000` literals and no base-URL setting (`mcp/server.py:717,799,1923`). Auth is one machine-global secret, `~/.cicada/api_token`, with no per-device scoping (`auth.py:6,88`); the CORS regex (`api/main.py:146`) is a browser control, not a network one. Install is launchd-only (`install.sh:325-375`, `scripts/doctor.sh:107`, README "macOS 14+") — yet the backend imports nothing macOS-specific and every dependency is cross-platform (`api/pyproject.toml:7-22`), so a Linux node is packaging, not a port. **Nothing in the codebase runs `git fetch`, `pull` or `push`**; the one remote read is `git remote get-url origin` for an avatar (`git_service.py:380`). And no record says which machine a conversation happened on: a captured episode carries `origin`, `harness`, `session_id`, `project_dir` (`transcript_capture.py:253-264`) but no device, and `_find_session_episode` keys on `(episodes_dir, harness, session_id)` alone; the only device-id concept is `local_refs.current_device_id()` (`socket.gethostname()`, `local_refs.py:80`), used for `repos:` and, as G92 already notes, with no remap table; `resumable` is an `isfile()` on the backend's own disk (`session_stats.py:72`), the resume descriptor shells out to `claude --resume` (`routers/conversations.py:213`) and the handshake promises it to every agent (`handshake.py:72,122`) — true only on the device that holds the transcript. **Two facts decide the shape.** (1) The extractor is already pure and portable — `transcript_extract.extract` takes lines and `scrub_secrets` runs inside it (`transcript_extract.py:426,131`), with the per-turn and per-session caps — so the deterministic read can happen on the machine the transcript lives on, and only scrubbed, capped turns need ever cross a boundary. (2) **Episode ids are proven safe for one writer only**: `next_episode_id` is max-suffix+1 over the *local* `episodes/` glob (`episode_ids.py:70-85`), inbox numbers are minted the same way (`inbox_generator.py:459`), and three in-bank files are read-modify-write state a three-way merge would silently mangle (`sync_state.json` — `sync_state.py:36`; `_merge_rejected.yaml` — `merge_rejections.py:15`; the bookmark seen-set — `bookmark_seen.py:43`); `_state.md` is a projection to regenerate, never merge, and the index is gitignored (`bank_registry.py:67`). Two writable clones of one bank therefore collide on the first same-day capture — and `markdown_parser.write` overwrites on collision. **Three shapes were designed and judged against the rulings (2026-09-06, a sonnet Workflow run on this row alone: seven readers over the code and the backlog → three designers, one per framing → two judges, one on the rulings and one as the owner); both judges ranked them the same way.** **Lead — distributed Awake, one Sleep (the network is the transport).** One bank, one backend, one Sleep writer, all on the MacBook, unchanged. The satellite (the Linux box) runs the *same* extractor locally under the *same* `validate_transcript_path` checks against its own home, and POSTs turns — never a path, never raw bytes — to a new `POST /capture/transcript/turns` on the primary, bearer-authed like every route, over an overlay network (Tailscale/WireGuard). The write half of `capture_transcript` (id mint, `_find_session_episode`, hash dedup, `processed: false`) is factored into one writer both endpoints share, so **G104 and G114 hold by construction: ids are still minted on the primary, at write time, under the existing lock.** A disk spool on the satellite (`~/.cicada/capture-spool/`, FIFO, the full payload on every retry so hash-dedup makes a resend safe) drains on the next Stop or on a systemd timer, so a MacBook closed for a week loses nothing; the hook still exits 0 inside its 3 s budget. Sleep never runs on the satellite — enforced by not installing the scheduler there, not by convention. **The rail this amends, said out loud:** "the backend reads the transcript" becomes *"the transcript is read on the machine it lives on, by Cicada's own validated extractor, and only extracted turns cross a machine boundary."* The rail's purpose — no path smuggling, no arbitrary bytes, a parser decision rather than a model's, no transcript mining — survives intact; only its locality claim generalises. **Second slice — the read side.** `cicada_recall`, `cicada_handshake`, `cicada_write_claim` and `cicada_save_episode` read or write bank files directly (`mcp/server.py:954,1547,1266,2029`; `handshake.py:250`), so "point the MCP at a remote URL" is not a config change — those tools have no HTTP twin. The cheap answer is git-transport's substrate without its write side: a **read-only mirror** on the satellite (`git fetch` + `reset --hard` on a timer; never a commit, never a push) makes the file-reading tools work unmodified, with semantic recall needing the embedding model and a locally rebuilt index (keyword fallback until then); the three HTTP call sites get one `CICADA_API_BASE`; satellite-side writes (`save_episode`, `write_claim`) go to the primary over HTTP or are refused with a clear message, never committed to the mirror — a second commit-capable copy is exactly the collision and split-brain class above. **Runner-up — git as the transport** (a bare repo on the always-on box; the satellite commits episodes and pushes; the primary fetches before Stage 1 and pushes after `_finalize`; the queue is a `processed: false` filesystem walk (`sleep_cycle.py:1336`, `bank_index.py:62`), so merged-in episodes need no plumbing). Best offline and security story — no shared secret crosses machines — and the natural end state if the satellite ever becomes a full node; but its first slice is the heaviest (git networking the codebase has never had, a per-device id floor that is a G114 exception, merge handling for the read-modify-write files, a Linux install), so it stays the substrate for the mirror, not the lead. **Rejected — the server as primary** (the always-on box runs backend + Sleep; the MacBook becomes the satellite): it inverts the stated scenario, ships raw transcript bytes before scrubbing, and needs four surfaces (installer, doctor, `BackendProcess`, `APIClient`) before a single episode lands; its one insight — bytes, not paths — is already in the lead. Note for whoever revisits it: an unattended cycle on a headless box is *safe* by construction (`engine_select.py:226,254` return `byok` before the registry is touched — ruling 4), so that topology is not blocked by quota, only by the scenario. **Device identity is the small prerequisite all three share:** every episode gains `device:` (the `current_device_id()` value, the convention `repos:` already uses), `project_dir` is read relative to it, and the resume descriptor and handshake say *resumable on `<device>`* instead of promising `claude --resume` on a machine that cannot honour it — G48's conversation identity grows a fourth field. **Rails a build must keep** (both judges' must-nots, deduplicated): the satellite never mints an episode id and never runs Sleep; nothing unscrubbed crosses a machine boundary; the backend is never reachable beyond loopback without an encrypted overlay — the bearer is one unscoped secret and plaintext LAN HTTP leaks it to the whole segment; a network failure never delays the hook or drops a transcript silently; no hostname, home path or overlay address is hardcoded (env-var config only — the portability rail); and read parity is never claimed while only the write path works. **Decide before building (the owner's forks):** (a) *thin satellite forever* (capture + read-only mirror — G114's single-minter invariant untouched) or *a full second node* with its own writable clone and Sleep (git-transport's shape, needs the id-floor exception and a merge policy); (b) copy the one bearer to the server, or introduce per-device tokens now — `auth.py` has no such concept, and the recommendation is per-device before any third device or any non-loopback exposure; (c) the overlay is a prerequisite the owner provisions (an existing Tailscale/WireGuard), not something Cicada installs; (d) whether the satellite ever needs the companion app (assume no: Claude Code only); (e) whether minutes of mirror staleness is acceptable for recall, or live HTTP recall (new endpoints for every direct-file tool) is worth its plumbing. **Install:** `install.sh` and `doctor.sh` grow a Linux/systemd branch (venv, the hook registration that already exists, a user unit + the spool timer) rather than a fork — the same portability mission as G76: "other people on their own plans and harnesses" includes their own machines. → extends **G92** (a shape its three routes did not name: the person's *own* devices and no third party — route (a) stretched to a second owned machine, and the local-first substrate route (b)'s hosted E2E sync would sit on top of, which is why the E2E question there does not arise here) and **G93** (which named "continuity across devices" as chargeable *because* it is what a user cannot trivially self-host; this row makes the self-hosted half free, which narrows what that monetization case can still point to — re-read G93 against this row before pricing anything there); depends on **G105** (the hook is the capture point on every machine), **G104**/**G114** (both hold by construction for the lead shape because minting stays on the primary; the runner-up needs the explicit G114 exception noted above), **G48** (device joins session/harness/project_dir); constrains **G76** (install grows a Linux branch), **G117** (a second device is an onboarding step), **G131** (a SessionStart primer on the satellite needs the mirror fresh); relates **G16**/**D4** (many people on one bank is a different problem from one person on many machines, but the same git substrate would carry both). **DECIDE** (a)–(b), then **APPLY** slice 1 (two labels on purpose: the fork gates a slice that is already sized) — M, $0: capture is file I/O and HTTP, no LLM anywhere. **Cross-reference (2026-09-23): G135** ships per-connector capability tokens and a public, MCP-only listener for cloud AI apps — a different exposure class from this row's owned-device overlay, but the same token store serves a satellite's bearer later (fork (b)); this row's rails are unchanged. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §5: explore push-only backup separately from device sync: one primary writer pushes committed snapshots to an explicitly configured owner-owned private destination under the git writer lock; no pull/merge/rebase/force-push. Stop/report divergence, retain local capture on remote failure, show last backed-up commit. Restore is separate. Destination disclosure/encryption stays DECIDE with G92; private hosting is not E2E encryption. Existing device-sync forks remain.** **Previous execution label (historical):** RESEARCH → DECIDE · P4, after single-writer server mode. | UNBUILT · P4 |

Specify a primary-writer collaboration model first. | G116 | <a id="g116"></a>**Two rulings the inbox redesign needs before its Phase 3 — who authors a rule-executed write, and whether an in-conversation resolve names its conversation** (raised by the G115 study 2026-09-02; both are contract questions, not builds; DECIDE, $0) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** User-authored resolution exists. Rule-executed authorship and optional in-conversation session linkage still require the documented decisions before Phase 3; S3 source checks cannot silently resolve a belief. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g116). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **(a) Rule execution and the author trailer.** G115's rule file lets the person write `decay(type=project): archive` once. Executing it at Stage 5 means Sleep archives an entity with no human in the loop *at that moment*, on a ruling the human wrote earlier. CLAUDE.md reserves `Cicada-Author: user` for manual/companion/media-save writes and `cicada` for system-maintenance writes with no model and no user in the loop (G85 precedent: the decay-only commit). The UI-port design proposed `user`; the buildability and friction judges both flagged it as a contract stretch. **Recommended, not yet ruled:** `cicada`, with the rule string as provenance in the manifest line (`entities/<id>.md: status archived (trigger: inbox/decay/resolved:archive, rule: decay(type=project): archive)`) so `GET /contributors` counts it as the system and `GET /entities/{id}/history` shows exactly which ruling fired; `_infer_change_type` still yields `statusChange` (R2). Until ruled, G115 Phase 2 ships `never` and `defer:N` rules only — a rule may suppress a question, not answer one. Evidence: `git_service.commit_resolution` (`:923-955`) hardcodes `authors=["user"]`; the decay split-out in `sleep_cycle._finalize` shows the `cicada` path exists. **2026-09-23:** G61 phase 2 adds a second caller — a source-settled inbox item is a check-executed write with no human in the loop at that moment; its spec recommends the same answer (`cicada`, rule string in the manifest), to be ruled once for both. **(b) `Cicada-Session:` on a user commit.** The conversation-first design proposed adding the trailer when a resolve arrives through the MCP with a `session_id`, extending "user-action commits stay session-less" with "an in-conversation resolve is a user action that has a conversation". **Recommended, not yet ruled:** keep the contract — `session_id`, `harness` and `surface` travel in the shipped `resolution` ledger event's refs (ids are allowed there; the event exists at `inbox_service.py:~456-477` with `item_id`/`entity_id`/`action`/`option_key`/`verdict`/… and these are additive keys), and `via_conversation_match` is computed from them, which answers "was this answered inside the conversation that raised it?" without giving a second meaning to a trailer every reader currently treats as "which conversations a Sleep consolidated". Revisit only if a git-only reader needs the join (e.g. `GET /entities/{id}/history` wanting to link a user resolve to a resumable conversation). **Constraint for both:** whichever way (a) goes, a rule-executed write must be attributable to its rule string in git; whichever way (b) goes, the ledger must carry the session ref. (The study's third owner question — close a silent clarification/merge question after 180 Sleep-counted days, recommended yes — is G115's, not this row's.) → gates **G115** Phase 3; touches **G85** (author contract), **G48** (session trailer contract), **G113** (ledger refs). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | DECISION · P4 |
