| G72 | <a id="g72"></a>**Agent-skills manager page + paper-guided skill lifecycle** (WikiSkill, Tang et al., Google Research, 2026-08-27 — [arXiv:2608.27454](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27454)) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Reviewed recommendations and install detection exist. A complete installed-skills inventory, deduplication and paper-guided lifecycle/evolution manager are not implemented. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g72). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Rodrigo's global agent skills are scattered, duplicated and silently drifting across three roots — **verified 2026-08-31 by read-only listing:** `~/.claude/skills/` = 3 personal (`context7-mcp`, `macos-harness`, `langfuse` — the last a **symlink out to `~/Developer/langfuse-skills/`**), `~/.claude/plugins/cache/*/skills/` = ~61 live across 7 installed plugins **plus 14 stale files from superpowers 6.2.0 still on disk beside 6.3.0**, and `~/.codex/skills/` = **92** dirs — of which **41 collide by exact name with the compound-engineering plugin's 41, and the copies have drifted** (`ce-plan/SKILL.md` differs byte-wise between the two roots); `~/.codex/prompts/` holds a third `ce-*` copy. Meanwhile `macos-harness`/`browser-harness`/`qa` (the heavily-used ones) exist in **one harness only**. **Slice A — inventory page, key-free, no LLM, buildable now:** a `skill_inventory` service walking the three roots (+ `installed_plugins.json` for marketplace/version/gitCommitSha provenance), a new **Skills** page under the G68 sidebar showing inventory by root, per-skill provenance and mtime, a **overlap/bloat panel** (name collisions, stale plugin versions, symlinks escaping the harness root, never-modified-since-install), and **per-harness sync state** (claude-only / codex-only / identical / drifted) with a diff — reuse the `DiffView` landing in **G67**, and the `/graph`-style ETag so a 156-entry walk costs a 304. **Slice B — Sleep-side candidate detection:** a Stage-4 tail step hashes/diffs same-named `SKILL.md`s and n-gram-overlaps distinct ones, emitting **existing inbox kinds** (`merge_suggestion` for near-dupes keyed on the sorted pair; a decay-flavoured question for a superseded plugin version or a skill untouched since install) — no new discriminator, no new resolve path. **Slice C — represent skills as memory:** each discovered skill → a `skill` entity (closed 10-set, no schema change) related to a `directory` entity for its harness root, its on-disk path as a `path`-kind **G61 `sources:`** ref; the paper's `PURPOSE.md` (skill → motivating evidence) is already Cicada's `source_episodes` + wikilinks. **What the paper actually gives us:** three-layer separation (`raw/` immutable traces → `wiki/` compounding pattern pages, *never rolled back* → `skills/` reversible), which maps 1:1 onto episodes → Stage-4 patterns → `skill` entities; and the ablation that **persistent knowledge is the whole gain** (Skill Proposer with wiki access 48.7 → 63.7 avg, +15.0) — i.e. Cicada's consolidation layer is the part worth having, not the optimizer. **Explicitly speculative / NOT committed:** the WikiSkill evolution loop itself. Its acceptance rule is `R(val) > R_best` on a labelled validation split, and a *personal* memory system has **no `D_val`** — there is no ground-truth score for "did this skill make the session better." Also missing: **`skill-impact.md`'s equivalent — nothing on the machine records which skill fired on which task with a pass/fail outcome** (verified: `~/.cicada/telemetry/` logs model+cost per LLM call, not skill invocation; Claude Code transcripts and `~/.codex/logs_2.sqlite` (38 MB) are unparsed). So any Cicada loop substitutes a **human gate**, which is exactly **R2**'s already-adopted governance (failure ledger + bounded, user-confirmed rewrites) and **G55**'s "skills never self-edit" — the paper *supports* R2: it accepts only ~1 in 3 proposed skill creations and ~1 in 4 edits (Table 4). **Two findings that constrain the design:** (i) **negative transfer is real** — a 4B-evolved skill dropped Gemini-3.5-Flash on SpreadSheet 50.5% → 18.1% by encoding harness-specific workarounds, so "push `macos-harness` to Codex" must be a **proposal with a diff, never an auto-push**; (ii) giving the *executing* agent wiki access **degraded** final skill quality (63.7 → 60.9), which cuts against Cicada's instinct to surface everything via `cicada_recall` — flag as an open question, not a decision. **Not verified:** whether Codex resolves `~/.codex/skills/` the same way Claude Code resolves `~/.claude/skills/` (assumed from directory shape + `SKILL.md` presence, not from docs); whether the 41 drifted copies are user edits or version skew; whether writing into harness roots from Cicada is safe (**Slices A/B are read-only — write-back is a separate, later decision**). → extends **G55** (executable skills / R2 governance — this is its inventory + provenance prerequisite), **A4** (procedural-memory capture), **R2**; consumes **G61** (`sources:`), **G67** (DiffView), **G68** (sidebar slot), **Stage 4** (pattern detection, new input); relates to **G49** (skills shipped with the plugin). **Owner, 2026-09-03:** "a skills page — tied into the WikiSkill paper. There are skills I use a lot in Claude Code, for example the browser harness or the macOS harness; I'd like these documented in my skills too, and in that page see which skills we use the most." So the page has two halves: (a) **skills Cicada compiled about you** (G112 — the `skill` entities, each with its evidence, exportable as a SKILL.md bundle); (b) **skills you use in your harnesses** — read the *names and descriptions* of `~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` and the plugin skills the harness exposes, plus the harness's own usage counters (Claude Code keeps `skillUsage` in `~/.claude.json`: count + last used — the same class of harness-owned data the Activity page already reads from `~/.claude/stats-cache.json`; transcripts stay unread, G48), ranked by use; a used-a-lot external skill can be *adopted* into memory as a `skill` entity with a `uses` claim and the harness as observer, so Cicada's wiki knows your toolset the way it knows your projects. Rails: names/descriptions/counts only; nothing executes a skill; the page never edits `~/.claude`. **2026-09-23 (Track O / G138):** resolved 'Not verified': Codex reads user skills from `$CODEX_HOME/skills` and `~/.agents/skills` (`openai/codex` `codex-rs/ext/skills/src/host_roots.rs`); Claude Code hot-reloads `~/.claude/skills/`; each installed skill costs listing budget, so recommend few. **Rail amended (R5 D2):** the app may write into `~/.claude/skills/` and `~/.agents/skills/` for Cicada's own bundles only — per click, marker-owned, never overwriting a changed copy; third-party skills install only through the agent's own installer after consent. Slice A (the inventory) is still open and would be a tab on the same Settings → Skills page. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G77 | <a id="g77"></a>**Voice-first Cicada + the "output packet" contract** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, forwarding [@KingBootoshi](https://x.com/KingBootoshi)'s voice-agent build: "add this as inspiration of things to discuss as a to-do") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Voice/meeting intake exists for Wispr, not an always-available voice interface or an output-packet contract for other agents. Define the product interaction before new capture channels. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g77). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The idea worth stealing, restated:** a voice UX must be fast, fast means a small model, and a small model is not smart enough to compress an intelligent agent's work without losing the point. His fix inverts the usual one — **don't push intelligence down into the voice layer; make the intelligent layer emit a packet so plainly written that the small model cannot mistranslate it.** The voice agent becomes a reader/typist, not an interpreter, and the packet is the contract between them. **Why this lands squarely on Cicada:** Cicada already produces exactly the artifacts that would fill such packets — inbox questions (G60 already carry `question` + `options[{key,label,description}]` + `allow_other`/`allow_defer`, i.e. a *closed-choice* structure a dumb reader can voice verbatim and an ASR can map back by key), `/ask` answers with citations + gap analysis, Sleep-cycle summaries, decay nudges. The G60 question object IS a first draft of an output packet; the gap is that nothing renders one for *speech* (no plain-language rendering, no per-option pronounceable key, no "what changed since we last spoke" digest). **Sub-ideas to discuss, not committed:** (i) a `packet` projection of any inbox item / ask answer / cycle summary — short sentences, no jargon, explicit enumerated choices, a single question at the end; testable by round-trip (small model reads packet → user's spoken reply → key recovered without the big model in the loop); (ii) **the green light** — a presence/status surface saying "Cicada is awake / consolidating / has 3 things to ask you", the ambient version of the Sleep page; (iii) **speaker-verification as auth** — his neural net that recognizes only his voice, i.e. an *observer identity* signal; Cicada's claim layer already has `observer:` and `source_trust` (`user_stated` vs `mcp`), so a verified-voice channel is a trust tier, not a new schema; (iv) the FaceTime/daemon adapter pattern (answer calls only from one number) as a capture channel alongside Telegram (G62 channel registry + the G71 `ADAPTERS` seam is the shape); (v) delegation — voice states an intent, a slow smart agent (G74(b) librarian / a Hermes-style daemon) does the work, packet comes back; the `processed:false` queue is already the async hand-off contract. **Open questions:** does the packet belong in the bank as memory (it is derived — probably NOT, same argument as logos/screenshots), or is it purely a rendering? Does a spoken correction write a `user_stated` claim directly (it should — that is the highest-trust input Cicada can get)? What is the latency budget end-to-end before it stops feeling like a person? → extends **G60** (question objects = proto-packets), **G53** (state dictionary = the digest source), **G74(b)** (the slow-agent half of delegation), **G62** (a new capture channel), **G54** (the interview beat this would make voice-native); relates **G75** (the handshake is the text twin of "here is how to talk to me"). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | UNBUILT · Later |
| G81 | <a id="g81"></a>**Contacts connection — real identity anchors for `person` entities** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "allow connection with contacts (name/email/phone numbers and stuff)") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Contacts reads and matching-person enrichment exist. Broader identity anchors, duplicate reconciliation and confirmation semantics remain; coordinate with G154 rather than importing every contact as a new person. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g81). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Today a `person` entity is a name and whatever a conversation said about them; there is no anchor tying "Aitor" in a chat to an actual human. Connecting the address book gives every person node a stable identity: **canonical name + email(s) + phone(s) + organization**, which unlocks (i) *dedup that actually works* — two pages for the same human collapse on a shared email rather than on fuzzy name similarity, the weakest input Stage 2 has; (ii) **G61 `sources:` for people** — an email or a LinkedIn URL is literally "where to look this fact up"; (iii) the **recorded-meetings** ambition now in `CLAUDE.md` — a transcript's speaker labels are worthless until they resolve to known humans, so this is a prerequisite, not a nicety; (iv) relationship inference the graph cannot currently make (same employer, same domain in an email address, a shared org). **Route:** macOS-native first — `CNContactStore` (Contacts framework) read-only, which is a **TCC-gated permission** the user grants once; note the app is currently unsigned (see the runtime row), and TCC prompts behave differently for an unsigned/ad-hoc-signed bundle — verify before promising a smooth grant. A one-shot vCard/CSV import is the keyless fallback and the portable path (Google Contacts export, iCloud vCard). Follow the **connector seam** that G71 established (`api/services/connectors/`, one adapter module, `ADAPTERS` registry, `base.run_sync`, `record_credentials_changed`) so this is one more adapter rather than a bespoke integration. **The hard part is not the API, it is the privacy rail — and it is different from every prior source.** Every other stream is the user's own data; a contact record is **a third party's PII** (someone else's phone number), landing in a **git-versioned, permanently-historied** store. Rails to decide before building: does the full record enter the bank at all, or only a minimal anchor (canonical name + a stable hash for matching) with the raw email/phone held outside the bank under `~/.cicada/` like logos and screenshots (the "derived/external artifact" precedent — although a contact is *source* data, not derived, so the precedent argues by analogy only)? Sensible default: **only what a fact needs** — an email used as a `sources:` ref is justified; a home address ingested "because it was in the record" is not. Also: contact records must never be swept wholesale into episodes (that would put an address book into the extraction queue); matching must be a **proposal** (`merge_suggestion` inbox kind, already exists) and never an automatic merge, because a wrong person-merge silently fuses two humans' histories — the most damaging error this graph can make. **Open questions:** whether to sync continuously (contacts change) or import once and re-check on demand; whether a contact with no conversational trace should create an entity at all (it would flood the graph with hundreds of inert people — probably NOT: a contact should *enrich* an existing person entity, not mint one); how deletion propagates when the user removes a contact. → prerequisite for the recorded-meetings half of the project goal; extends **G61** (`sources:` on people), **G71** (connector seam), **G60** (`merge_suggestion` as the proposal surface), **Stage 2** (identity resolution's best possible input); relates **G77** (voice identity/observer trust) and **G79** (curation stream). **(Absorbs G46, merged 2026-09-01)** — the original ask specifically wanted read-only AppleScript/EventKit enumeration of Contacts.app feeding entity resolution as corroborating metadata, not raw episodes, and covers most family-milestone birthdays. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G85 | <a id="g85"></a>**Decay is attributed to a model, re-charges the same interval every run, never strengthens, and its rate ignores how established a belief is** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "i see decay something changed by the model, and i belive it should be not something of the model itself but as time passes by… its decay depends on how established the fact is and it can go from evergreen ad very established to something that isnt, and vice-versa") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Watermark, outage protection, true decay author and spacing exist. Reinforcement still uses max(existing,incoming), not a frequency increment; any change must distinguish evidence strength from recall ranking. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g85). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The user is right, and the diagnosis splits into four distinct defects.** **(1) Attribution is wrong, the math is not.** The decay loop (`conflict_resolver.py:128-181`) is pure arithmetic over frontmatter and `datetime.now()` — `decay_amount = rate * days_since/7`, no LLM anywhere. But authorship is decided **once per commit for the whole batch**: `sleep_cycle.py:798-804` builds `authors` from `settings.litellm_model` regardless of any change's trigger, and `git_service.py:399-400` applies that commit-level trailer to every entity line. So a deterministic time computation is stamped with whichever model happened to run that cycle — 978 such lines across the two 2026-06-18 cycles, which also inflates that model's counts in `GET /contributors`. **Fix:** split `trigger: sleep/decay` changes into their own commit authored `Cicada-Author: cicada` — the literal already reserved for system-maintenance writes with no model and no user in the loop. **(2) Decay is not actually continuous in time — it re-charges.** `conflict_resolver.py:145-151` SUBTRACTS `rate * days_since/7` from stored confidence but the write branch (`:336-341`) never advances `last_referenced` and stamps no watermark, so the next run reads the same `last_referenced` and subtracts the same full interval again. **Confidence is a function of how many times Sleep ran, not of elapsed time** — this is the concrete defect behind the objection. Same shape in the claim engine (`claim_reconciler.py:478-483`). **Fix:** make it computed, not accumulated — keep a stable `baseline_confidence` and derive `confidence = baseline * exp(-rate * weeks_since_last_referenced)`; or stamp `decayed_through:` and measure from `max(last_referenced, decayed_through)` so each interval is charged exactly once. **(3) Rate ignores establishedness.** `decay_class` is assigned once at page creation (`:211-222`) and never revised — the update branch merges episodes, tags, aliases and body but never touches it. An entity referenced in 50 conversations decays at the rate it was born with. `source_episodes` is the natural establishedness signal and is already accumulated. **Fix:** in the update branch, promote the class one step toward `durable` when the page crosses a distinct-conversation threshold; keep `evergreen` off-limits to the agent per the existing `agent_class` rail. **(4) Bidirectionality is half-built.** Recovery exists (`:248-258`) but is a one-shot floor — `max(current, 0.6)` — gated on LLM re-extraction, so a belief reinforced ten times still lands on 0.6 and can never climb. **Fix:** make reinforcement a bounded additive increment symmetric with decay, both directions reading the same `decay_policy` resolver. → this is the row that makes "time as a signal" honest; touches **G66** (decay classes), **G80** (all four fixes are deterministic — no engine needed). **(3) answered by G147 (2026-09-24)** — not by promoting the class (that would rewrite a label the person can set, G66 §1.7, and commit on every threshold crossing) but by a derived spacing factor over distinct ISO weeks of `source_episodes`, recomputed every cycle and never stored. (4) stays open. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G89 | <a id="g89"></a>**Substack — following already works; make FEEDS first-class instead of building a connector** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "add to backlog an ability to connect substack if possible") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** RSS/ICS feeding exists, including public newsletters. First-class feed management and any authenticated Substack capture are different scopes; respect public fetch gates. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g89). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Researched against primary sources and verified live; the headline is that a Substack connector is the wrong shape.** Subscribing to a publication is already supported end-to-end — `feed_registry.py` + `feeds.yaml`, `GET/POST/DELETE /sources/feeds`, `POST /sources/poll-feeds`, `media_ingestor.parse_rss` → the same dedup/ingest pipeline as bookmarks — and the app's RSS tile literally reads *"A blog or Substack Cicada checks for new posts"* (`AddSourceSheet.swift:64`), with the backend docstring saying the same (`sources.py:216`). **What Substack actually exposes (verified 2026-09-01):** `https://<pub>.substack.com/feed` is documented and live; RSS 2.0, **full body for free posts** (an ACX item ran 57k+ chars in `content:encoded`), **exactly 20 items with no pagination** — a recent window, *not* an archive, so history cannot be backfilled from it; custom-domain publications serve `/feed` on their own domain and the `*.substack.com` form 301s to it. **Paywalled posts appear with full metadata but a ~142-byte stub body** containing only a "Read more" anchor — a reliable detection heuristic, and a real ingestion hazard today (that stub would land as a normal media entity with an empty body). **There is no authenticated text feed at all**: Substack's private per-subscriber RSS is **podcast-only**, so the sanctioned machine-readable route to paid text is *none*. **A reader's own subscription list has no sanctioned route either** — no OPML export (help-centre search for "OPML" returns 0 results), no reader data export (Exports are publisher-side, for a publication you own), and no documented API. The only mechanism is `GET substack.com/api/v1/subscriptions` with a session cookie: undocumented, cookie-gated, no stable contract — **the same unsanctioned shape already ruled out for the X archive and LinkedIn enrichment**, so it stays out. Defensible alternative: the user copies their list from `substack.com/library` by hand once, and each publication is subscribed via its public feed — a user action, not automated access. **Saved posts, likes and Notes have no export or API**; Substack's own MCP server states it "cannot access profile data or Notes activity". The official **Developer API is a creator-profile lookup keyed on a LinkedIn handle** (no post content, no reader data), and the Substack MCP server is publisher analytics gated on being an Admin of a *Bestseller* publication — neither is usable here. **So the work is feed quality, and it is all currently missing:** (i) **`parse_rss` keeps only 4 fields** — link, title, body, categories (`media_ingestor.py:993-998`) — and drops `pubDate`, `dc:creator`, `<enclosure>` and the channel `<title>`, so an ingested post has **no publish date, no author, and no publication**; (ii) `RawItem.origin` is never set for feed items, so an RSS post is indistinguishable from a pasted URL in `/origins` (contrast `"chrome-bookmark"`, `"tiktok-saved"`); (iii) **the publication is not an entity** — only individual posts, so "what does this writer keep saying" is unanswerable and G79's cross-stream retrieval has nothing to hang on; (iv) **feed polling is NOT on the Sleep tail** — `sleep_cycle._poll_connectors_safely` iterates only `connectors.ADAPTERS`, so feeds refresh only when a human presses Poll (calendars share this gap); (v) a feed record is just `{url, tags, added, last_polled}` — no title, no etag/last-modified (so every poll refetches in full), no per-feed error, no enabled flag, hence no failure surfacing on the channel row; (vi) no bulk/OPML import path for adding many feeds at once; (vii) paywall-stub items need skipping (or marking) rather than ingesting. **Also inconsistent:** feeds are gated **opt-in** (`CICADA_ALLOW_FEED_FETCH == "1"`) while connectors are now opt-out — one of the two should move. → extends **G47/G71** (the ingest family and the connector seam that feeds should join), **G61** (a publication is a natural `sources:` anchor), **G79** (a followed writer is curation, and the publication entity is what makes it relatable); relates **G80** (all of this is deterministic, zero-LLM work). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ feed-following half already shipped (M4 — `feed_registry.py`, `GET/POST/DELETE /sources/feeds`, `POST /sources/poll-feeds`); the metadata-quality work (i)-(vii) above is the open remainder — see TODO Wave D.. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G91 | <a id="g91"></a>**Share-to-Cicada — a system share target for links and documents from any app** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "the importance of the companion app so that links and stuff like documents are easy to share from any app to this app") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** No native system share extension was found. Paste/drop/import already exist; a macOS share target and any iOS companion require their own packaging and app-group/privacy design. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g91). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Today capture is *pull* (paste a URL, drop a file, run an import) or the Telegram bot for mobile — there is no way to be reading something anywhere on the machine and push it in. A macOS **Share Extension** makes Cicada a first-class target in every app's share menu, which is the difference between "capture when I remember to" and "capture at the moment of interest" — the moment the *reason* is still in your head, which is exactly what G71's save-with-reason wants and what an after-the-fact import can never reconstruct. **Shape:** an `.appex` inside the app bundle declaring `NSExtensionActivationRule` for `public.url`, `public.file-url`, `public.plain-text` and PDFs; the extension POSTs straight to the already-running local backend (`POST /sources/save` for links, `/sources/upload` for files) — no app-group plumbing needed because the backend is a localhost service, which is a genuine advantage of this architecture. Add a one-field "why are you saving this?" note in the extension sheet so the `saved-because` claim (G71, already built) gets populated at the only moment the answer is cheap. **Verified constraints, and they are the whole risk:** `grep NSExtension app/` returns nothing today — no extension target exists; SwiftPM does not build `.appex` targets, so this rides on `bundle.sh`'s manual assembly (already the packaging path) or needs an Xcode project; and **Launch Services only registers a share extension for an app that is installed and code-signed** — ad-hoc signing may suffice locally but this is where the unsigned-app trade-off in **G88** stops being cosmetic. Sequence it after G88 for that reason. **iOS is a separate build, not a flag** — the Telegram bot remains the mobile answer until then. → depends on **G88** (installed, signed app), completes **G71** (reason-at-capture), relates **G62** (channel registry — the share sheet is a new channel with its own origin) and **G37**. **(Absorbs G37, merged 2026-09-01)** — the ask was first flagged as "Coming soon" in `SyncSetupView`, Track D3. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | UNBUILT · Later |
| G94 | <a id="g94"></a>**Life-data streams (wearables, Spotify, GPS/saved places, Strava) — aggregate into experience, never dump the samples** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a way where its easy to port data from other things like werables… spotify, GPS (google maps saved places), strava… CIcada should be done in such an agnostic way that it is able to ingest all this data and in turn communicate in a way thats easy for the human and agents to understand") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** No aggregate life-data pipeline for listening, wearables or GPS was found. Define rollups and provenance first; do not accumulate raw samples as memory claims. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g94). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The agnostic ingestion the user asks for is real and mostly already there** — `RawItem` + `origin` + the `ADAPTERS` connector seam + url_index dedup are source-agnostic by construction, and Google **Takeout parsing already exists** (`media_ingestor.parse_youtube_takeout*`), which is the same export vehicle that carries Maps saved places. Sanctioned routes per source: **Spotify** (real Web API, OAuth — saved tracks, top artists; plus a GDPR export for history), **Strava** (real API, OAuth), **Google Maps saved places** (Takeout), **Apple Health / wearables** (an on-device export XML, or HealthKit from a future iOS app — no server API), Oura/Whoop/Garmin (each has its own API). Each is one more adapter module in the existing registry — **that part is not the hard problem.** **The hard problem is representation, and getting it wrong would wreck the graph.** These streams are *quantitative time series*; Cicada's model is entities + claims + episodes, i.e. *semantic*. Dumping 8,000 heart-rate samples or every song play into a markdown knowledge graph produces a graph that is 99% noise and a Sleep queue that never drains — the same category error as dumping raw transcripts, which this project already refused. **The rule this row exists to fix:** ingest **aggregates and notable events, never samples** — "slept under 6h for nine days straight", "ran a personal best", "first visit to Lisbon", "listened to one album obsessively for a week". Raw samples stay outside the bank entirely (the `~/.cicada/` precedent for logos, screenshots and telemetry: derived-or-bulk artifacts of the outside world are not versioned memory), or are not retained at all. The closed 10-type set mostly absorbs the result without extension — **`location` already exists** for places visited, `media` for albums/tracks, and a workout or a sleep week is an *episode* with claims rather than an entity. Resist adding types; that instinct is what the type set is for. **Why this is worth building at all — and it is the strongest argument in the backlog for it:** the value of life data in a memory system is **correlation with experience, not the data itself**. "You were in Lisbon that week, slept five hours a night, and that is when you decided to switch projects" is exactly **G79**'s cross-stream retrieval, and no fitness app can say it because none of them hold the conversations. The summarization step — turning a numeric week into one sentence a human and an agent both read the same way — is **G77**'s output-packet idea applied to quantitative data, and it is deterministic enough to run without an engine (**G80**). **Sequence:** start with ONE source that is export-based and already half-supported (Maps saved places via Takeout → `location` entities), prove the aggregate-not-samples rule end to end, then add an OAuth adapter (Strava or Spotify) once the representation is settled. → extends **G47/G71** (ingest family + connector seam), **G79** (the north star this most directly serves), **G77** (packet-style summarization), **G80** (aggregation is engine-free), **G70** (the same "derived artifact lives outside the bank" rail). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | UNBUILT · Later |
| G95 | <a id="g95"></a>**Meetings and human↔human conversations as an ingestion source** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "have meetings/conversations be part of ingestion, so transcriptions from conversations can also come into this as part of human experience, wether its just a transcript, a recording, etc. useful info can come from this such as the people involved, things said, ideas discussed") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Wispr meetings preserve speaker labels and assistant-written notes. General meeting upload/recording, who-was-present semantics and other-person consent remain open; G179 handles selected message threads. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g95). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Named in `CLAUDE.md` as part of what Cicada is for, and **the one intake class the pipeline was not designed for**. Every episode today is *single-observer* — the user talking to an agent, or the user saving something. A meeting is **multi-party**, and that changes three layers, not just the parser. **What already exists and can carry it:** the ingest pipeline is source-agnostic (`RawItem` + `origin` + `processed: false`, Sleep extracts regardless of source), and — verified — the claim layer **already models third parties**: `observer: str = "agent" # agent \| rodrigo \| external:<name>` (`api/services/claims.py:68`), with three `external:*` observers live on the current graph (`eugenio-marchiori`, `konrad`, `milan`) and a per-observer trust multiplier already in the decay engine (`claim_reconciler.py:51-53`, `user_stated` fades ~3× slower). So "who said this" has a home; nothing needs inventing. **What does not exist:** no transcript parsing of any kind (no VTT/SRT/diarized-text reader — the `transcript` hits in `entity_extractor`/`session_stats` are about agent sessions), and **no audio transcription anywhere** — the Telegram voice-note transcription described in prose is **not implemented** (`grep -i whisper api/` → nothing). **Design:** (i) **Text first** — accept a transcript (`.txt`/`.md`/`.vtt`/`.srt`, or a paste) with optional speaker labels, `origin: meeting` (or per-tool: `granola`, `zoom`, `otter`, `voice-memo`), landing as one episode. This alone delivers most of the value and needs no new infrastructure. (ii) **Recordings** — transcribe **on-device** (whisper.cpp / MLX Whisper), matching the EmbeddingGemma precedent of a keyless local default; **the audio itself never enters the bank** — it is bulk binary and belongs under `~/.cicada/recordings/` or nowhere, exactly the rail that governs logos, screenshots and telemetry. Only the transcript is memory. (iii) **Speaker → observer**: a labelled speaker maps to `external:<slug>`, so a statement by someone else is stored as *theirs*, not as the user's belief — and **their** assertion about themselves is high-trust while their assertion about the world is not. This is where **G81 (contacts)** pays off: speaker labels are worthless until they resolve to real humans, and without it every meeting mints a fresh half-identified `person`. (iv) **What to extract**: attendees → `person`; ideas discussed → `concept`; decisions and commitments → claims with `valid_from` (the bi-temporal layer is already built for exactly this); action items → the existing inbox. No new entity type — a meeting is an episode, and the closed 10-type set absorbs the rest. **The rail, and it is heavier than any other source's:** recording a conversation involves **people who never opted into your memory system**. Spain (and many jurisdictions) require all-party consent for recording; beyond legality, storing another person's words in a permanently-historied git repo and letting an agent recall them is a different ethical class from saving your own chats. Non-negotiables: consent is captured per meeting and stored with the episode; a participant can be excluded or redacted after the fact (which git history makes genuinely hard — decide the redaction story *before* building, not after); third-party PII stays minimal per the **G81** rule; and nothing about a meeting is ever surfaced outside the user's own machine. **Sequence:** transcript-drop → speaker-to-observer mapping → on-device transcription → live capture (a much bigger problem: macOS system-audio capture needs ScreenCaptureKit/TCC or a virtual device, and meeting-bot integrations are a different product surface — explicitly out of scope for now). → the human↔human half of the project goal in `CLAUDE.md`; depends on **G81** (identity anchors), extends **G20/G47** (import family) and the claim layer's `external:` observers; relates **G77** (a meeting summary is the packet idea's natural home), **G94** (same aggregate-not-raw rail), **G79** (a meeting is pure experience-stream). **2026-09-23 (G134):** the first multi-party intake shipped — Wispr Flow meetings as `speaker:<label>:` turns with the per-turn `turns` sidecar, evidence kind `speaker`, `user` only by the owner's listed names; consent and the other note-takers remain here. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G111 | <a id="g111"></a>**Ingest newsletters (TLDR, TLDR AI) and answer "what landed today that matters to me"** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "process emails from newsletters like tldr ai, and tldr… so i could ask my agent about relevant things from today that might be relevant to me and my research") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Public RSS newsletters work. Mailbox newsletter capture and a personalized what-matters-today digest are not implemented by RSS ingestion. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g111). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Read the ask precisely: the ingestion is the easy half and it is not what was asked for.** The request is a *question* — "relevant to me and my research" — so the deliverable is a **relevance filter over a firehose**, not another capture channel. Cicada is well placed for exactly that and badly placed for nothing else here: the graph already encodes what the user works on and cares about, so scoring an incoming item against existing entities/claims is the differentiator. Raw ingestion alone produces a second inbox the user will not read. **Do the cheap path first and it may be the whole path.** TLDR publishes web archives and per-edition RSS — **VERIFIED 2026-09-01 by direct probe:** `https://tldr.tech/api/rss/ai` (TLDR AI) and `https://tldr.tech/rss` / `/api/rss/tech` (TLDR) are live RSS 2.0 feeds, 20 items each, **one item per edition** (`<link>` = `https://tldr.tech/ai/YYYY-MM-DD`, `<guid isPermaLink>`, `<pubDate>`, **no `<description>`/`content:encoded`** — the feed carries only the edition headline), and each edition page is **static server-rendered HTML** (~75 KB, no `__NEXT_DATA__`, JSON-LD present) holding **19 `<article>` blocks** per edition — one per story, each a headline suffixed "(N minute read)", a 2-4 sentence summary and exactly one outbound `href` with `utm_source=tldrai` — plus a sponsor `<article>` with a `(Sponsor)` suffix and `doubleclick` tracking links that a parser must drop. So the mailbox is never needed: the feed gives the edition stream, and the per-story split G111 needs is a ~40-line `<article>` splitter over the edition page, with the story's canonical URL (utm stripped) as the item id. No mailbox access is needed at all, because `feed_registry.subscribe_feed` (`api/services/feed_registry.py:80`) plus the existing `POST /sources/poll-feeds` and the Sleep-tail poll already do the whole capture job with **zero new consolidation code** — the module's own docstring says a poll is "identical to a manual `POST /sources/rss` paste". Verify the archive/RSS surface before writing a single line of email code. **Email is a categorically larger surface than RSS and must not be adopted casually.** An IMAP/Gmail connector reaches *everything* in the mailbox, not the newsletters — a scope the secrets posture (`~/.cicada/secrets.env`, 0600) can hold but the *blast radius* of a bug cannot. If email is genuinely required, the only two acceptable shapes are (i) a **dedicated forwarding address / label-scoped** read (`label:tldr`), never a whole-mailbox grant, or (ii) the user forwarding issues by hand. Either way it is a peer adapter under `api/services/connectors/` honouring the documented `ADAPTERS` contract (`connectors/__init__.py:35,69`) — `base.run_sync` already owns the not-connected skip, the `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` background gate, error/skip recording and the chunked `ingest_batch`; do not hand-roll a second polling loop. **The parsing unit is the ITEM, not the email — this is the structural point.** One TLDR AI issue is ~8-10 *unrelated* stories, each a link plus a written summary; ingesting the email as one media item produces a page about nothing. It is the same one-source-many-items shape G71 already solved for saved-content exports, and it should reuse that machinery: a parser emitting a per-item breakdown, previewable through the identical `?preview=true` path before anything is staged. **The trap, and it is in the code today: these must NOT be evergreen.** `decay_policy.INGEST_SOURCES` (`api/services/decay_policy.py:49`) is `{media, bookmark, rss, pdf, ingest}` and everything on those paths is evergreen — decay-exempt, never archived, no decay nudge — with the stated rationale that *"a saved bookmark does not become less true by going unmentioned"*. That reasoning is sound because **saving is a deliberate act**. A newsletter arrives unbidden: the user chose the subscription, never the item. Routing 10 items/day through the `rss` path at evergreen would permanently inflate the graph with things the user never selected and would quietly break "absence of mention is a signal" — the one mechanism that keeps the graph honest. **So newsletter items need a decaying class (likely `volatile`, since news is stale in weeks) and therefore either a new writer-source tag outside `INGEST_SOURCES` or an explicit per-write override.** Decide this BEFORE the first ingest run, because retro-fixing decay classes across thousands of pages needs another one-shot migration. **Promotion is the natural filter, not a new mechanism:** an item mentioned once stays a chunk in the vector index; an item whose topic the user actually discusses crosses the existing promotion threshold on its own. That is the entity-promotion model doing precisely the job it was designed for, applied to a feed instead of a conversation — so the anti-pollution answer is mostly *already built*, provided the decay class is right. **What is genuinely new:** a daily-digest surface answering "what landed today that touches what I am working on", which is `POST /ask` (**G52**) pointed at a time-boxed slice and ranked against the graph rather than a free-text question — and worth checking whether it belongs in the Feed page or as a Sleep-time nudge rather than yet another screen. **Open:** whether unread/unscored items should ever reach the graph at all, or live only in the index until something promotes them. → extends **G71** (the connector adapter contract + the per-collection preview shape) and the shipped RSS/feeds half; bounded by **G66** (decay classes — the evergreen trap above is the binding constraint); serves **G52** (Ask is the surface the request actually names); relates **G105** (deterministic, non-agent-judgment capture — a newsletter parser is the same argument) and **G101** (what raw content is retained vs consolidated). **2026-09-29:** links inside a newsletter or an imported chat read through the same Reader ladder and read-time page writer as **G164** / **G165**, so a newsletter parser here could reuse that fetcher instead of adding its own. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G119 | <a id="g119"></a>**Other browsers — Arc, Firefox, Edge and Opera (bookmarks; open tabs where the browser exposes them)** (follow-up from the 2026-09-02 Safari track) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Chrome/Safari plus several Chromium-family readers exist. The specifically requested Arc/Firefox/Edge/Opera support is not established for every bookmark/tab format; advertise an adapter matrix, not all browsers. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g119). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Same shape as G30: the app reads the profile file, the backend parses bytes, `folders:` selection and the tree preview come for free via `bookmark_sync.folder_tree`. **Round 4 (C9, 2026-09-24) — the Chromium family shipped (backend and app, PR #109):** Brave, Vivaldi, Comet and Dia sync through the one Chromium parser (`bookmark_sync.CHROMIUM_BROWSERS`, `POST /sources/sync-bookmarks`'s `chromium: [{browser, dataB64}]`), each under its own origin (`<browser>-bookmark`) and channel (`<browser>-bookmarks`, listed once it has synced), **default profile only** (R-SR1: reading every `Profile N` silently would bring in a profile nobody ticked, and the removal diff has no profile scope — profile selection is this row's next slice). Bundle ids and default `Bookmarks` paths were verified from public sources, never a real profile. **What is left:** Arc (`StorableSidebar.json` — spaces/folders/tabs in one JSON, its own parser); Firefox (`places.sqlite`, `moz_bookmarks` ⋈ `moz_places`, WAL-mode — reuse `safari_tabs.load_tabs`'s temp-copy + sidecar pattern; its tab groups live in `sessionstore.jsonlz4`, not `places.sqlite` — a different reader than this row first budgeted); Edge and Opera (Chromium, but their paths are unverified until checked on a real install); and profile selection. Rail: no network, no LLM, idempotent through `url_index`. → **APPLY**, S each. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G120 | <a id="g120"></a>**Attention frequency — track who and what you keep coming back to (channels, accounts, authors, people) and let recurrence shape the graph** (Rodrigo 2026-09-03: "in social media like YouTube or Twitter there's always a certain number of channels or people who I love to see their tweets and stay informed. Do you think frequency in mentions also has to influence something in the memory graph, or at least should we track it?") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Mention-week spacing exists via G147. Attention/recurrence for evergreen curated media is a separate signal; repeated exposure must not become an inferred preference automatically. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g120). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists:** frequency already drives one thing — decay (`conflict_resolver`: confidence drops proportional to how often an entity USED to be referenced, CLAUDE.md Temporal Decay), **2026-09-24 (G147): this premise was false until G147 — decay was flat per class. Now distinct mention weeks slow entity and claim decay; the source-recurrence layers (1)–(3) below stay open.** and the promotion rule counts conversations (2+ mentions across conversations). Nothing counts **sources**: a saved video carries `media.url` and an OG title, a tweet carries an author handle in its URL, a newsletter carries a sender — but the *channel/account/author behind the item* is never made an entity, so 40 saves from one YouTube channel are 40 unrelated media pages and the person the owner follows most is invisible to the graph and to retrieval. **Design (three layers, all engine-free):** (1) **Source attribution at ingest** — `media_ingestor` derives a `source_ref` `{platform, kind: channel\|account\|author\|domain, id, display}` from the URL/metadata it already has (YouTube channel from the page's OG/`author_name`, X/Twitter handle from the path, Substack/newsletter sender from the feed row, podcast show, GitHub org, plain domain as the fallback); stored on the media episode + entity frontmatter, no fetch. (2) **Recurrence ledger + promotion** — a per-bank `_attention.yaml` (or a derived index — decide; must be rebuildable from the pages, never a second source of truth) counting saves/mentions per source_ref per week; when a source crosses the existing promotion threshold (≥ 2 saves across ≥ 2 weeks, or ≥ 3 saves) Sleep creates a `person`/`company`/`concept` entity for the channel/author with a `follows` claim (`observer` = user-derived, `source_trust: observed`, confidence from recurrence), `evergreen` never — it decays like any belief so a channel you stop watching fades honestly (the counter-signal is the whole point). Media pages get an `about`/`from` edge to it. (3) **Surface** — Feed gets a "You keep coming back to" strip (top sources by recent recurrence, with the count and last-seen date), the entity page shows the recurrence sparkline, `cicada_recall` weights a source's items by recurrence, and G111's "what landed today that matters" uses it as the ranking signal. **Rails:** counts only — no engagement scraping, no fetching follower lists, nothing behind auth (G102 ToS rail); ids/enums in any ledger (G113); a recurrence claim is `observed`, never `user_stated`, and the inbox may ask "still following <source>?" through the ordinary decay path rather than a new kind; portability — platform parsers are the same adapter shape as `connectors/`. **Not the same as:** G86 (feed dedup), G94 (life-data aggregates — that is samples→aggregates from wearables; this is recurrence over what you save and read), G111 (newsletters — a consumer of this signal). → feeds **G111**, **G93** (retrieval weight), **G118** (a `follows` claim cites the saves that earned it), **G81** (a recurring author is an identity anchor); consumes **G47**/**G71** (saved-content importers already carry the URLs). APPLY, M **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G121 | <a id="g121"></a>**World facts vs personal facts — a place (or company, tool) page must be anchored on why it matters to the person; encyclopedia facts are a dated cache an agent re-verifies, never truth** (Rodrigo 2026-09-03, looking at a location page whose summary and Key Facts were metro lines and court names: "what if something changes in that location and an agent in the future references the memory instead of researching the place? Should information about a place be just why it's relevant to the user?") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Typed claims and dated evidence exist. Personal-reason anchoring versus refreshed encyclopedia-cache facts and visible freshness semantics are not fully implemented by a date field alone. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g121). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Diagnosis:** the extractor fills a `location`/`company`/`tool` page with whatever the model knows — none of it observed in an episode — and writes it with the same `source_trust: agent_extracted` and confidence as things the person actually said. Two failures follow: the fact goes stale silently (a renamed venue, a closed line), and an agent reading the page cannot tell a remembered fact from a looked-up one, so it may act on the cache instead of checking. `claims.py:71` knows `user_stated\|agent_extracted\|agent_reflected\|external`; nothing marks *model knowledge*. **Ruling to adopt:** a memory is about the person's experience; the world is not Cicada's to remember. Every page has two tiers: (1) **personal claims** — the relation to the person (visited, plans, works-with, saved-because, decided) with the episode span (G118); authoritative. (2) **world facts** — convenience context; allowed only when tagged `source_trust: model_knowledge` (new value, decay multiplier ≥ `external`), `decay_class: volatile`, dated by `recorded_at`, and paired where possible with a `sources:` pointer (G61) to where the current truth lives (the venue's site, a docs URL). **Changes:** (a) the Stage-1 prompt asks for the relation first and marks anything not grounded in the text as model knowledge — a page whose only claims are world facts is a promotion-rule violation (nothing personal was observed) and stays a candidate; (b) `entity_extractor`/`claim_pipeline` accept the new trust value; existing pages get a one-shot backfill that re-tags claims without a source span as `model_knowledge` (dry-run count first on the live bank — expect most `location` Key Facts to flip); (c) the entity card renders the two tiers apart — "Why it's in your memory" above, "Context (as of <date>, from the model's knowledge — verify before relying on it)" below, with the sources link; (d) `cicada_recall` / `cicada_ask` label world facts as cached-on-<date> in their output, and the G75 handshake carries the rule in one sentence: *personal facts are authoritative; world facts are a cache — check before acting*; (e) the inbox never asks a decay question about a world fact (it simply fades). **Rails:** no fetching to refresh world facts in this row (that is G102's tail, gated); ledger ids only; the backfill's commit is `Cicada-Author: cicada` with the exact pages named. → depends on **G118** (a span is what separates the tiers), **G61** (sources), **G66** (decay classes); feeds **G75** (handshake), **G93**/**G52** (answer labelling), **G53** (state dictionary carries only personal facts). APPLY, M **2026-09-23 (G133):** the paper card is the first surface with both tiers — "Why it's in your memory" (spans in the owner's own notes) above "Context (from arXiv, as of <date>)" (an `external` `describes` claim authored by `cicada`). **2026-09-23 (G61 phase 2):** the world/personal marking lands at the predicate level first — `locus: world\|artifact\|person` in the vocabulary — and "a cache an agent re-verifies" gets its mechanism in G61's escalation ladder (a checked source can settle only a machine-held `world` fact, never a personal claim); this row's own rail still holds — the fetching lives in G61, gated, not here. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |

Nothing shows *the set of apps in your life* as one page. **The line between the two surfaces (ruling):** Feed's `+` catalog stays the place for a one-shot act (drop an export, sync a folder); **Settings → Integrations** is the place for a *standing* connection (sign in once, polled on the Sleep tail, disconnect here). Both are projections of the one channel registry (`channel_registry` + `connectors.ADAPTERS`), so a connector added once appears in both. **The page:** categories with one row per app and its live state (connected · last sync · items · error), logo-first: *Chat & agents* (Claude Code, Claude Desktop, ChatGPT, Codex, Cursor), *Browsers* (Safari, Chrome; G119 Arc/Firefox/Brave), *Social & saved* (X, Reddit, Pinterest, Instagram, TikTok, LinkedIn), *Entertainment* (YouTube — subscriptions/watch-later via Takeout, playlists via export; podcasts via RSS), *Calendar* (any ICS; Google/Apple via their ICS links), *To-do & notes* (Apple Reminders/Notes via export or AppleScript, Todoist/Things via export or API — new adapters, G13's intake), *Health & activity* (Strava via OAuth API, Garmin via export, Apple Health via export — **G94's rule: aggregates, never samples**; weekly summaries become claims, raw samples never enter the bank), *Feeds* (RSS/newsletters, G111), *Messaging* (Telegram). Each row: Connect (OAuth/keys/export instructions per adapter contract), what Cicada reads (one honest sentence, incl. cost — the X row already states pay-per-read), Disconnect (forgets secrets via `base.forget()`). **New adapters are the work, the page is the frame:** ship the page over the existing adapters first, then add Strava (OAuth, activities → weekly aggregate claims), Todoist/Reminders (tasks → G13 candidates), YouTube subscriptions (Takeout parser — no API key needed), Garmin/Apple Health (export parsers). **Rails:** the adapter contract in `connectors/__init__.py` (CHANNEL_ID, LABEL, FIELDS, LOGIN_MODE, SECRET_NAMES, is_connected, forget, sync) is the only way in; secrets in `~/.cicada/secrets.env`; unattended polls behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH`; no scraping behind auth (G102); life data as aggregates (G94); each new app is its own small row when it starts. → frames **G94**, **G111**, **G119**, **G120** (a source's attention count is a natural column here), **G13**; extends **G71**/**G62**; feeds **G117** (onboarding step 2 is this page filtered to 'connect one thing'). The page ships over the existing registry; Strava/Todoist-Reminders/YouTube-subscriptions/Garmin-Apple-Health remain their own future rows. APPLY (page S/M; each adapter S–M) **Track L (2026-09-05) — real marks; R7 of the 2026-09-02 Safari-import plan is reversed.** That plan ruled "browser marks are drawn, not downloaded" and shipped `ChromeGlyph`/`SafariGlyph`. It was a *plan* rule, never a `TODO.md` ruling, and it named its own escape hatch ("drop `Resources/logos/chrome.png` in and flip the tile's `logoName`"); both `PlatformTile` and `OriginMark` already preferred a PNG. The hatch was taken because the drawn Chrome was wrong on four independent axes: all four wedge colours were the pre-2015 Google palette (`#DB4437/#F4B400/#0F9D58/#4285F4` vs the current `#EA4335→#D93025 / #FCC934→#FBBC04 / #34A853→#1E8E3E / #1A73E8`), the wheel was rotated ~90° (red centred at 2 o'clock instead of 11), the centre disc was ≈0.34 r against the real ≈0.45 r, and every wedge was a flat fill where the mark uses two-stop gradients. `SafariGlyph` was Apple's own compass SF Symbol tinted an invented `#00A2E8`. **The rule that replaces it:** an app whose files Cicada reads off this Mac is installed by construction, so its icon comes from `NSWorkspace` at runtime (nothing redistributed, never stale — this is also how R-L3 keeps Apple's marks out of the repo); everything else is a maintainer-fetched, licence-recorded, committed PNG (`scripts/fetch-logos.sh` + `logos.manifest.json` + `LOGOS.md`, nominative use only). **Drawn brand marks do not come back** — a wrong-coloured logo that appears only when an asset is missing looks like a logo and isn't, which is worse than an honest SF Symbol. Reserved and not yet claimed: the Firefox and Brave marks are committed for **G119**'s channels. **Track S (2026-09-05 evening) — the line this page draws is now visible on the card.** Standing vs one-shot was structural (Integrations vs the Feed's `+`) and invisible once a source had a card; `SourceLiveness` now reads the same `channel_registry` `actions` the two surfaces share and prints `Polls nightly` for a standing connection against `Imported once` for a one-shot import, so the distinction is legible on the Sources page without opening Settings. **Track O (2026-09-23):** every Integrations row is a Settings search anchor (`channel:<id>`, `harness:<id>`, `exportOnly:<id>`) — typing an app's name in Settings lands on its row; the page's content is unchanged. **DS-3b:** real harness marks; Manage and Connect are sheets. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ page shipped, adapters open. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G140 | <a id="g140"></a>**What the Instinct comparison changed — memory quality borrowed on Cicada's rails, and the video watch record** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23, on Dhravya Shah's article reverse-engineering the memory of the Instinct iMessage assistant: "I think maybe some things here can be of use. See what and see if we can work on implementing." — and on a video-watching skill: "Could match well for storing and having info about videos. Maybe getting the metadata or whatever else.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Instinct findings are folded into provenance/video/quality rows. Watch-record infrastructure exists; preserve each named incomplete slice under its implementing ID rather than treating a comparison document as shipment. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g140). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The article, in one line.** Instinct keeps git-tracked markdown, injects a ~4.25k-token LLM-written profile (life context, autonomy calibration, communication style) plus a compaction recap, retrieves by grep over files that carry `aliases`, reconciles once a day, and forgets only explicit negations; the author's supermemory replication adds a static/dynamic profile, time-based expiry and auto-included history. Everything about Instinct is inferred from outside the product. **Adopted (Track Q, `feat/memory-quality`, plan `../plans/2026-09-23-memory-quality.md`, rulings Q-R1…Q-R17):** (1) *aliases and word-by-word matching* (Q-R1) — MCP recall's keyword leg was a whole-query substring that never read `aliases`; it is now `search_service.lexical_entity_hits` (the G136 hand-off); on a bank whose FTS file is still building, the leg answers from names, aliases and tags only, and a body- or claim-only match appears once the background build lands; (2) *relationship labels resolve* (Q-R1) — current claims are recall's third RRF leg through `claim_subject_hits`, mapped to their subject, and `mcp_tools._rrf_fuse` IS `search_service.rrf_fuse`; (3) *old versions, bounded* (Q-R2, Q-R3) — recall shows claims closed in the last 30 days as `was X until D → now Y` (≤ 2 a page, ≤ 5 in all; "withdrawn" and "ended D (its stated end)" where that is what closed them) and `cicada_get_perspective(history=true)` lists every earlier claim (≤ 20, off by default and byte-identical when off); the dead `include_superseded` on `vector_index.search_claims` is removed (it could only surface a state no writer produces); (4) *a static/dynamic profile without prose* (Q-R13) — the primer's now-view splits into Standing (the person's page and one-liner via G117's resolver, their timezone per request and never stored, "How to work with me" = standing `skill` pages by confidence alone, long-standing durable/evergreen pages) and Current (projects, pages in focus in the last 14 days, people, conversations), read off the G66 decay classes; `_state.md` schema v2; a remote connection gets the rows that describe the person (the one-liner, timezone, How to work with me, long-standing, in focus) only when it holds a `read`-scope tool — a `record`-only connection was promised "save notes, links and facts", not a profile (final review); (5) *a timeline an agent can read* (Q-R4) — `cicada_timeline(since)` reads the commit manifests on demand, ids and counts only, nothing stored; (6) *explicit negation, with provenance* (Q-R5) — `cicada_retract_claim(subject, claim_id, reason, evidence?)` lets an agent withdraw a claim IT wrote: the claim closes, a born-closed `retracts` record keeps the reason and any cited words, nothing is deleted; a human or Sleep claim is refused, a remote connection owns only its own `remote:<id>` claims, and the pre-G135 `mcp-agentic-write` placeholder counts as any local agent's only on an `mcp`-origin claim (the same placeholder lands on Telegram's user-stated claims). Records are bookkeeping, never beliefs, and every surface that lists claims drops them through one test, `claims.is_record`: MCP history, the app's claim endpoints, the `/search` claim leg (not indexed at all; search-index schema 2 rebuilds an older index) and an episode's citations; (7) *time-based expiry* (Q-R6, Q-R7) — a claim may carry `expected_end` (or be a G17 `due` date) and Sleep's engine-free tail closes it the day after, in a `cicada`-authored `Expiry <date>` commit (trigger `sleep/expiry`); a page already dirty before expiry runs is skipped that night, so neither a failed commit's restore nor a good commit can take a person's uncommitted edit. Contract v3 (local) and v2 (remote) name the new tools, and a test holds R12 for every argument either primer names (Q-R14; it found `cicada_recall_detail(id)`). **Video (R5, Q-R8…Q-R12):** `cicada_save_url` keeps a note given for an already-saved link and names the episode to cite; an agent's note is written as the agent's words; `external:<name>` is a value the observer schema accepts; `cicada_record_watch(url, summary, excerpts=[{t, quote}], chapters?)` records a watch as one episode + one `describes` claim whose spans are `assistant` (the summary) and `media` (each quote, time derived at read) — excerpts only, never a transcript, and Cicada never downloads a video; Vimeo/Loom descriptions are kept and chapters parsed from them; link enrichment's zero-LLM `describes` claim takes only the description's first paragraph, chapter stamps dropped, ≤ 400 characters (final review: a 4,433-character description had been copied into the claim whole) — the full text stays on the page. **Known gap:** a claim id is minted from its fact, so withdraw → restate reuses the closed claim's id; the withdrawal picks the open copy and mints a fresh record id, but the root fix — `write_claim` not reusing a closed claim's id — is still open. **Already better in Cicada, kept:** commit trailers naming author, engine and session vs bare reconciliation commits; typed, bi-temporal claims with trust tiers vs bullet lists of dated prose; supersede-never-delete vs prose rewrites; evidence spans with hash staleness (G118) vs no link from a fact to its sentence; temporal decay (G66) vs no automatic forgetting; one episode per session (G104) vs one document per day; capture with no intake ceiling vs "come back tomorrow"; a dated, deterministic cursor (`_state.md`) vs an unstamped profile with a measured ~2-day lag; hybrid retrieval vs grep (Instinct's own probes miss a paraphrase). **Rejected, and the rail that rejects it:** an LLM-written profile (G53: a cursor, never a copy; stale by construction; a daily cost); redundant fan-out writes (G101's stable address — Instinct itself reports stale duplicates); a "compress and remove incidental details" pass (destroys G118 spans; recall already truncates at read); read-only agent memory (the two-way port); supermemory as a backend (hosted and closed, replaces git — breaks G50/G76/G92 and the trailers); a daily-only intake with a token cap; an in-context todo board. **Open:** P9 — the article's 13-point rubric as a $0 retrieval eval on the demo bank (expected id in recall top-k for single fact, alias, paraphrase, typo, relationship label, superseded-not-current, abstention, two-hop; the LLM-judged half on demand) — the harness **G78** asks for; P3 secret and one-time-code scrubbing on every episode writer (Local-sources track, R-N3 — including the two writers this row added, `watch_record._write_episode` and `media_ingestor.write_note_episode`); P10 an open-loops cursor row; P11 a "last time in this project" pointer; typo tolerance; Stage-1 extraction of stated ends from prose (a paid prompt change); the person's own "that's off" closing a user-stated claim and entity-level forget through a `removal` item (P7's broader half); an app surface for the timeline, the watch record (▶ m:ss chips, a Watched pill, chapter seek buttons) and withdrawals; the remote primer's older rows — project one-liners, people ids and conversation titles, there since G135 — still reach a connection with no `read`-scope tool (only the G140 rows were gated). → extends **G53**, **G75**, **G93**, **G118**, **G136**, **G66**, **G17**, **G22**, **G113**; feeds **G78**. **2026-09-23:** P10 (an open-loops cursor) and P11 (a "last time in this project" pointer) are delivered by **G141** for projects: `_state.md` v3 `now`/`next`, `cicada_project`, and `GET /projects`. G141's `ClaimModel` also gains the missing `expectedEnd`. "An app surface for the timeline" here means this row's bank-wide `cicada_timeline`, which the Projects page does not replace; it stays open with the watch-record and withdrawal views (Tracks P/O). **2026-09-29:** the open 'app surface for the watch record (▶ m:ss chips, a Watched pill, chapter seek buttons)' is folded into **G162**: five honest states (none, transcript, watched, watched-and-transcript, recorded/method-not-given), a `basis` on `record_watch`, and Watch / Transcript enqueue in the Feed. `record_watch`'s rails (no download, excerpts only) are unchanged. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ adopted parts shipped; P9 open. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G155 | <a id="g155"></a>**Google services — Gmail, Google Calendar events and Drive, so a belief can cite a specific event or email** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "connect google services. google services can be something in the backlog tho for now, but its good to reference specific calendar events, emails, etc.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Calendar through EventKit/ICS works. Direct Gmail/Drive OAuth ingestion and citations to specific messages/events are not implemented by that calendar reader. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g155). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The point is citation, not intake.** The ask is that a belief can point at *the* event or *the* email it came from — G118's spans, not copies — so an agent answering "when did I agree to that" lands on the message. **What exists:** Google Calendar reaches Cicada today only through an ICS URL (`calendar_registry.py`, opt-in feed polling) or, from round 4, through macOS Calendar when the Google account is on the Mac (D2, EventKit) — neither carries a stable Google event id to cite; Google Takeout is a one-shot export the registry already names (`channel_registry.py`); there is no mail reader (**G111** argues why email must not be adopted casually: a mailbox connector reaches everything, and the blast radius of a bug is the whole inbox). **The burden to know before building:** Gmail read scopes and full Drive access are Google **restricted scopes** — an app that requests them for other people needs Google's OAuth verification and an annual third-party security assessment, which a local-first open-source app shipping one client id cannot carry lightly; a per-person client id (bring your own Google Cloud project) avoids it at a real setup cost. **The no-OAuth alternative:** a **Takeout** import (Mail as mbox, Calendar as ICS, Drive files) through the one intake — no scopes, no verification, the person's own export, re-importable (G20 in-place updates); the cost is freshness. **Constraints:** the ToS rail and the credentials rule (`~/.cicada/secrets.env`, never in a bank, never logged); scrub before stage (`episode_scrub`); an email is someone else's words too — a line from a sender is `speaker` evidence, never `user`; narrowest scopes first (`calendar.events.readonly`, `drive.file`), Gmail last and only with a filter the person writes. **Rodrigo 2026-09-28: "open the backlog entry for the google direct connection"** — asked after syncing a new bank, so the direct connection is wanted, Calendar first. **The first slice, scoped:** Google Calendar only, read-only (`calendar.events.readonly` — a *sensitive* scope, not a restricted one: public use needs Google's OAuth verification, but no security assessment; until verified, a client id serves at most 100 listed test users), as a standing connector in the existing registry (`api/services/connectors/base.py`, Connect/Manage in Integrations, disconnect through `base.forget()`), signed in with PKCE in the browser the way OpenRouter's sign-in works (R-AG10). Each event staged through `episode_staging` keyed `google-calendar:<calendarId>:<eventId>` so a belief cites *the* event (its `htmlLink` as a `sources:` ref), edits rewritten in place, a cancelled event tombstoned; incremental reads through the API's `syncToken`, polled on the Sleep tail behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH`, a Sync now ungated. Attendees are `speaker`-side facts, never the person's words; descriptions scrubbed and capped like `calendar_local.py`'s. **Open for the owner:** one shared Cicada client id (verification on the maintainer) vs. bring-your-own Google Cloud project (no verification, real setup cost). Until it ships, adding the Google account under System Settings → Internet Accounts already brings its calendars in through macOS Calendar (D2) — without a citable Google id. Gmail and Drive stay RESEARCH under the burden above. → relates **G111** (newsletters, the email caution), **G126** (Integrations), **G118** (citations), round-4 D2 (calendars). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲 APPLY (Calendar slice) · RESEARCH (Gmail, Drive). | PARTIAL · Later |
| G156 | <a id="g156"></a>**Browser history sync — what the person actually read, not only what they kept** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "for browser's i'd give the ability to sync history, you can have that in the backlog and for handoff to the next session") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Bookmarks and tab groups work; history-based reading/attention capture and its privacy/rollup controls are not implemented by bookmark polling. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g156). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists.** Browsers sync bookmarks (`bookmark_sync.py`, `bookmark_seen.py`) and Safari's iCloud tabs (`safari_tabs.py`), each channel gated on the person's own act (`cicada.browserWatch.enabled.<channel>`, the Track I T1 rule); history is read nowhere. **Where it lives:** Chrome keeps it in a per-profile SQLite `History` file (`urls`, `visits`; times in microseconds since 1601), Safari in `~/Library/Safari/History.db` (`history_items`, `history_visits`; seconds since 2001), which needs Full Disk Access like the bookmarks file does. **Privacy first — this is the most sensitive stream a browser has:** a history is every page, including the ones a person would never keep. Rails any build must respect: the **app** opens the file read-only (a copy, since the browser holds a lock) and posts a projection — the backend never opens `~/Library` (the rail); off until the person turns it on per browser, never ticked by default in onboarding; a **domain denylist** applied app-side before anything leaves the file (banking, health, adult, private-window leftovers, and the person's own additions), plus a minimum dwell or revisit count so a stray click is not a memory; titles and URLs only — never page bodies fetched from history, never behind auth (ToS rail); scrubbed (query strings carry tokens) before staging; aggregated into one episode per day per browser rather than one per visit, so the extraction queue is not flooded; deletion in the browser tombstones (G20). **Why it matters:** the north star's *experience* half — what held attention, not what was curated — and G128's "what actually held your attention". **Handoff:** next session — decide the unit (a day digest vs. per-domain threads) and the denylist's default, then plan. → extends the browser channels (**G126**, Track I T1); relates **G128**. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲 RESEARCH. | UNBUILT · Later |

## Research — dedicated adapters and unproved premises
