| G78 | <a id="g78"></a>**Re-evaluate gbrain (and its eval harness) against Cicada** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "search if gbrain's approach is actually better for what we try to do, or if there is something he does better that maybe we can learn from"; also [`gbrain-evals`](https://github.com/garrytan/gbrain-evals) "to see if any of that can apply for us") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** A prior comparison dossier exists; reevaluate only on a named failure/measurement trigger. Do not replace derived recall storage from a competitor's headline. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g78). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Cicada already has a 2026-era analysis at [`../inspiration/gbrain.md`](../inspiration/gbrain.md) — "the convergent twin: markdown+git, self-wiring typed graph, overnight enrichment, MCP" — and the D3 ruling ("answer not pages") was taken partly from it. **This row is the re-check, and it has a new object:** `gbrain-evals` did not exist when that analysis was written. **Two deliverables.** **(1) Approach diff, honestly scored.** Re-read the current gbrain source (not the old notes) and answer, per axis, "who is actually better and why": zero-LLM-on-write (its regex wikilink cascade) vs Cicada's LLM extraction — gbrain's write path costs nothing and never hallucinates, which is *strictly better on the axis Cicada is currently dead on* (G74: no engine ⇒ no writes at all; a regex/structural promotion path would have kept the graph alive these 2.5 months, and that is an argument for a **zero-LLM fallback rung**, not just a cheaper engine); dedup/citation-repair as background chores; its `think` synthesis vs Cicada's `/ask`; typed-graph conventions vs the closed 10-type set; decay (Cicada's differentiator — verify gbrain still has no temporal-decay analogue); claims/bi-temporality (Cicada's other differentiator); provenance depth (Cicada's `Cicada-Author`/`Cicada-Session` trailers + git blame are, as far as the prior analysis found, unmatched). **(2) Eval harness applicability — the higher-value half.** Cicada's thesis needs a defensible Results chapter and today has `benchmarks/` with a **manual four-dimensional scoring rubric** (`sections/experiments.tex`) — i.e. a human scores every answer, which does not scale and is the weakest link in the thesis. If gbrain-evals provides task sets, an automatic judge, or a recall/precision protocol for a *personal markdown memory graph*, adopting or adapting it (with attribution) directly strengthens Table 1/2/3. Assess: what does it measure, on what corpus, with what judge; is the corpus synthetic (portable) or personal (not); can it run against Cicada's API without a rewrite; does it have anything for the two things Cicada claims and others don't (**decay behavior over simulated time**, and **provenance/attribution correctness**) — if not, that gap is itself a thesis contribution to state. **Method:** read-only clone/read of both repos, no dependency adoption in this row; output = a written comparison in `docs/inspiration/gbrain.md` (updated, dated) + concrete "adopt / adapt / reject, and why" rulings, each cross-linked to the row it would change. **Explicitly allowed conclusion:** "gbrain is better at X and we should copy X." → re-opens **D1** (storage backend) and **D3** (answer-not-pages, already decided); feeds **benchmarks/** + the thesis Results; relates **G74** (the zero-LLM fallback argument), **A5** (gap analysis, taken from this lineage). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | CONDITIONAL · Research |
| G82 | <a id="g82"></a>**Hub pages are not addressable — the entity card half-works for them and only History admits it** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, running dev: "nodes like architecture have a couldnt load history error") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Hub builders and graph cards exist; verify the current generated-hub addressability failure before changing them. Historical screenshots are not a current reproduction. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g82). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Root-caused, verified live.** `api/routers/entities.py:189-191` hardcodes `memory_path / "entities" / f"{id}.md"` and 404s **before git is ever called**. Hub pages don't live there — they're `hubs/topic-architecture.md`, and `graph_builder.py:215` mints their id as `hub:<stem>`, so `entities/hub:topic-architecture.md` can never exist. Confirmed by curl: `/entities/hub:topic-architecture/history` → 404 while `/entities/awake-sleep-architecture/history` → 200 with 2 entries; the data is fine (`git blame hubs/topic-architecture.md` = 141 attributed lines across 3 commits). **Why only History complains:** the other tabs swallow the identical 404 — `Store.swift:531-532` returns nil on throw so the header keeps rendering the graph-node stub, and `EntityDetailCard.swift:1355` uses `try? … ?? []` so Perspectives/Timeline render as merely empty. History is the one tab whose state machine separates error from empty, so it is the only honest surface. **Scope is systemic, not one line:** eleven sites in `entities.py` (61, 113, 189, 205, 230, 301, 375, 421, 481, 494, 519) and four in `git_service.py` (211, 352, 590, 898) hardcode the same wrong directory. `GET /entities/{id}`, `/history`, `/context` and `/claims` all 404 for hubs. **Both layers must move together:** deleting only the router guard flips the symptom to a false "no history", because `git_service.py:352` independently rebuilds `entities/{id}.md` and returns `[]`. **Fix:** one shared `resolve_entity_file(memory_path, id) -> Path \| None` mapping a `hub:` prefix to `hubs/<stem>.md` and everything else to `entities/<id>.md`, plus a repo-relative twin for git; route every read-only site through it. Never special-case the string in the view. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | CONDITIONAL · Research |
| G128 | <a id="g128"></a>**Being somewhere is an experience Cicada cannot capture — visits, places and what actually held your attention there** (Rodrigo 2026-09-03, forwarding [MemPal (arXiv:2502.01801)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01801): "might be relevant for spatial memory understanding, and research data capture from video, so if I go to a museum, what are the artworks that influence me most") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Location entities exist, not a consented visit/attention capture channel. Decide sources/rollups and distinguish observation from intent before implementation. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g128). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap:** every capture channel today is a screen — conversations, links, bookmarks, feeds. A morning in a museum, a walk, a conference floor leaves *nothing* in the bank unless the person types about it afterwards, so the stream (Era of Experience) has a hole exactly where lived experience is richest. `location` is already an entity type but nothing writes one from being there. **What the paper actually shows, and the ruling it suggests:** MemPal (Maniar, Chan, Zulfikar, Ren, Xu, Maes; MIT Media Lab, Feb 2025) puts a wearable camera on 15 older adults in their own homes, runs the egocentric video through a multimodal LLM, and keeps **an automated text diary of activities** — not a 3D map, not stored frames — then answers "where did I leave X?" by voice; object-finding measurably improved against no aid. The transferable result is the storage choice: **a text diary indexed by time and context was enough for spatial recall**. So Cicada should not build a spatial index or a scene graph; a *visit* is an episode with a place, and the things in it are ordinary entities with claims. Video, if it is ever used, is a **transducer to text at the edge and is then discarded** — the same shape as G105's block-level rule and G94's aggregates-never-samples rule. **Three tiers, cheapest first, each shippable alone:** **(a) Visits from what you already capture — zero new hardware, no camera, do this first.** The camera roll already answers "where was I and what struck me": photos carry EXIF time + GPS, and *the act of photographing is the attention signal* — in a museum you photograph the works that hold you. A `visit` episode is built by clustering a day's photos by place and time (Apple Photos / an exported album, the same import shape as the browser channels: the app reads, the backend parses), with the place resolved to a `location` entity; each photo becomes a media item on that visit, captions and any voice memo attached, and nothing is uploaded anywhere. Sleep then extracts the works/people/ideas named in captions or recognised text through the ordinary Stage-1 → Stage-2 path. **The camera roll is also the first step toward spatial understanding, and it is worth saying why: the photo is already the index.** Time and GPS put you in a place; what is *in* the frame says what held you there. Run that extraction **on-device in the app** (which already holds the file-access half of the division: the app reads `~/Pictures`/PhotoKit, the backend parses text) using Apple's Vision framework, in this order of yield: **(i) text (`VNRecognizeTextRequest`) — the highest-value and least speculative.** In a museum the wall label *is* the metadata: artist, title, year, medium, and people photograph the placard beside the work. OCR of that placard turns a JPEG into a real `media`/`concept` entity with an artist `person` beside it, rather than a filename. The same request covers signs, book covers, conference slides, menus and receipts, so it pays off far beyond galleries. **(ii) scene and object classification (`VNClassifyImageRequest`)** for a coarse, honest label (painting · sculpture · street · food · whiteboard) — enough to say *what kind of thing* a visit was made of, never asserted as fact beyond a confidence. **(iii) image feature prints (`VNGenerateImageFeaturePrintRequest`)** to collapse the eleven near-identical shots of one painting into one item, and to notice the same work photographed again years later — which is recurrence (G120) measured on pixels rather than words. **(iv) faces: detect, never identify.** `VNDetectFaceRectanglesRequest` can say a photo has people in it; naming them is the owner's act, not the machine's — a name arrives only by the owner tagging it, and it resolves against contacts (G81). Strangers in the background of a gallery shot are never clustered, never named, never stored as a face. **What Apple/Google do that cannot simply be borrowed, stated so nobody plans on it:** PhotoKit exposes **no public People API** (the People album's face clusters and names are Apple's, not a third party's to read), and **Visual Look Up** — the feature that names an artwork or a landmark from the photo itself — is not available to third-party apps. So the equivalent of "Apple knows this is a Rothko" is, for Cicada: the placard's OCR text, plus the owner confirming the guess through the ordinary inbox question. That is slower and more honest, and it produces a citable claim (G118) instead of an opaque label. Reverse-geocoding a coordinate to a venue name is a network call (`CLGeocoder`) and therefore opt-in per the connector rule, or skipped in favour of the coordinate plus the owner naming the place once. **What is stored:** the derived text and the asset's local identifier — never a copy of the image, never a face, never an upload. **(b) In-the-moment notes tied to a place.** Telegram `/note` exists; give it (and G91's share sheet) an optional place, so "this room stopped me" lands on the visit rather than floating free. **(c) Continuous or wearable video — RESEARCH, not a build.** Only after (a) and (b) prove the visit shape. Open questions the row exists to answer: what runs on-device (frame → text) so no frame is ever stored or sent; what cadence is enough (MemPal's diary is periodic, not per-frame); and the two hard constraints that are not technical — **bystander privacy** (a camera in a public gallery records other people, who have not consented and are not the owner's memory to keep; the answer is probably that only the owner's own utterances and derived descriptions survive, never faces, never a frame) and **venue rules** (many museums forbid recording; a channel that quietly breaks a venue's rules is not shippable). Also worth measuring before building: whether (a) alone answers the museum question well enough that (c) is not worth its cost. **"Which artworks influenced me most" is a query, not a capture feature.** Once visits exist it decomposes into three signals already designed: **attention frequency** (G120 — how often a work or artist recurs across visits, photos and later conversation), **provenance** (G118 — the evidence for "this moved me" is the caption you wrote or the sentence you said, cited as a span), and **decay** (G66 — a work you never mention again fades, and that silence is the honest answer to "influenced"; the ones that keep coming back are the ones that did). The strongest signal is not dwell time in the room, it is **re-mention weeks later** — which the graph already measures. **Rails:** no frame or raw sample enters a bank (G94); nothing is uploaded to a third party; place data is the owner's, so a visit is as private as an episode and never leaves the machine; bystanders are never identified; capture is opt-in per channel like every other. → extends **G94** (life-data streams — this is its place-and-time half), **G91** (share sheet as the in-the-moment path), **G77** (voice is the natural capture verb when your hands are busy looking); consumes **G120** (recurrence), **G118** (why you say it moved you), **G105**'s edge-extraction rule; feeds **G93** (a cross-stream question like this one is exactly what it is for). RESEARCH for (c), APPLY for (a)/(b), M **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | UNBUILT · Research |
| G131 | <a id="g131"></a>**Cicada should replace the harness's own auto-memory — research whether we can do it better, and what it would take** (Rodrigo 2026-09-04: "as part of a backlog do you think maybe we can replace the auto memory feature with cicada? … document the idea of researching if we can implement our own version of auto memory that is better for cicada") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Bounded handshake and recall hooks exist. Replacing every harness auto-memory workflow is not established; compare continuity and clock behavior only after G10 gives a concrete baseline. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g131). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What the competitor actually is, stated precisely, because the shape is uncomfortably close to ours.** Claude Code keeps a per-project memory at `~/.claude/projects/<sanitised-cwd>/memory/`: one markdown file per fact, YAML frontmatter (`name`, `description`, `metadata.type` ∈ user \| feedback \| project \| reference), `[[wikilink]]`s between files, and a `MEMORY.md` index whose one-line-per-fact list is injected into context at **every** session start. That is a markdown knowledge graph with an always-loaded cursor into it — i.e. **`entities/` plus `_state.md`, built by someone else, sitting next to the bank and invisible to it.** On this machine it measured 36.8 KB / ~9.2k est. tokens before a manual prune. **The case that Cicada wins on merit, not on ownership — four things it already has that auto-memory structurally lacks:** **(1) Decay.** Auto-memory never ages. Nothing marks a fact stale, nothing notices it was superseded, and the only remedy is a human re-reading the directory — which is exactly what happened on 2026-09-04, when four of thirteen files (a July audit, a shipped design assessment, a pause-point its own index line called superseded) had to be hand-deleted after months of being loaded into every session as if current. G66 solves this by construction, and this is the sharpest argument in the row: **a memory system with no clock is a memory system that lies with increasing confidence.** **(2) Provenance.** An auto-memory file records `originSessionId` and nothing else — no author, no episode, no evidence span, no git history. Cicada has `Cicada-Author:`/`Cicada-Session:` trailers, `source_episodes`, and G118 spans, so a belief can be traced to the sentence that produced it. **(3) Contradiction handling.** Auto-memory's instruction is "check for an existing file that already covers it" — a prose convention, enforced by nothing. Cicada has the claim layer, bi-temporal validity and the inbox: a competing value opens a question instead of quietly creating a second file. **(4) It is one harness's private store.** It does not exist for Codex, it does not travel between machines, and it is not the person's to take with them — which is the portability point the whole project is built on. **What auto-memory has that we do not, and it is the only thing that matters: zero-cost injection.** Its index is in context *before the first token*, with no tool call and no latency, so the agent starts already knowing. Cicada's recall costs a round trip and depends on the model choosing to make it — the G105 objection, applied to reading rather than writing. **The good news is the port is largely built.** G75's handshake is already delivered through MCP `initialize`'s `instructions` field — the same pre-first-token slot — and already carries a now-view from `_state.md` under a token budget. So the question this row exists to answer is **not** "how do we inject memory at session start" (solved) but **"what belongs in that slot besides the now-view?"** Concretely: auto-memory's `feedback` type — *how to work with this person on this project* — is the one category the handshake carries nothing of, and it is precisely WikiSkill's third layer, i.e. **G112**. A `skill`-type entity is the natural home; the research is whether Sleep can distil those reliably from episodes, or whether they must stay user-asserted. **Open questions the row exists to answer, cheapest first:** (a) Can a `SessionStart` hook write the handshake into context for harnesses that drop MCP `instructions`? `handshake.HOOK_POINTER` already anticipates this and the hook itself is G49/G76 — measure whether that path alone closes the gap. (b) What is the honest token budget for a lessons block on top of the ≤ 1,800-token primer, and what gets trimmed first (the `_state.md` trim order is the precedent: people → preferences → conversations → projects). (c) Does a `feedback` belief decay like any other, or is it `durable`? A working agreement ("PRs go to dev") should not fade; an observation about a paused queue should. (d) Migration: is a one-time import of the existing auto-memory directory worth it, or is it cleaner to let the two coexist until Cicada's version is demonstrably better? **Rails.** **Cicada must never read the harness's memory directory uninvited** — it sits beside `~/.claude/`'s transcripts and falls under the same rule that keeps those unread (G105/G93); an import is a user-initiated action with a visible preview, exactly like every other export path, never a background sweep. Nothing here creates a second store: a migrated fact becomes an ordinary entity or claim in the bank, with provenance saying it came from a harness memory file. And the win is only real if it is **portable** — a lessons block that works in Claude Code and nowhere else has reimplemented the thing it was meant to replace. → extends **G75** (the handshake is the delivery slot), **G112** (compiling experience into loadable skills is the same layer), **G53** (`_state.md` is the existing always-loaded cursor); consumes **G66** (decay is the differentiator), **G118** (provenance is the other one); relates **G49**/**G76** (the SessionStart hook), **G117** (onboarding is where a person would opt into this). RESEARCH, M — measure (a) and (b) before writing any code. (a)'s SessionStart primer is built by **G149** (2026-09-24). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | CONDITIONAL · Research |
| G167 | <a id="g167"></a>**A local browser engine for JavaScript-only pages** (surfaced by the 2026-09-29 reading-the-web research; the owner asked for the two ends — a fast reader, or the agent's freedom — and this is the middle) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** No local JavaScript browser-engine rung is implemented. Add only after public-reader needs_js failures justify it, under the fetch/ToS rails; it is not a first-consolidation dependency. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g167). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Some pages return `needs_js` (G164's R3) — the bytes hold a shell, not the page. A small local renderer would read them without an agent. **Candidates (spec §14 D-RW2):** Obscura (Apache-2.0, Rust, ~39 MB, created April 2026, v0.2.3, so pin a source) or Lightpanda (AGPL-3.0, ~84–88 MB, more mature, beta Web API coverage; Cicada is MIT, and running it as a separate downloaded process over CDP, never bundled or modified, is the conventional reading — a legal call the spec cannot make). Either needs a rail amendment (a fetch budget above 4 s; a ruling that a user-installed renderer is the same rail). **Recommendation: later** — ship the Reader first, measure how many saved and chat pages are `needs_js`, decide on that number. Until a local renderer exists, a page the reader cannot read because it needs JavaScript is the same kind of failure as a wall: once G164 stamps `needs_js` it joins the surfaced set as wall kind `js` (`reading_walls.WALL_KINDS` reserves the name), offered to an agent only with the same per-site switch; G167 then shrinks that list instead of replacing the permissions page. → depends **G164**, **G165**. Triage: DECIDE (slice S9). **Previous execution label (historical):** ❓ later, on S1's measured `needs_js` share. | CONDITIONAL · Research |
| G168 | <a id="g168"></a>**FUTURE — a small classifier model to navigate sites faster and cheaper (the owner's "jev"), as a Settings option that "needs OpenRouter"** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29: "in backlog document for future, that i think we can use a classifier model to navigate quicker and cheaper, through websites using a model like jev. thing is that its not in the plans people normally have. but we can build it still and have it in settings as an option and a 'needs openrouter' or smth like that, if the user wants to turn it on.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** The proposed small navigation classifier is future research, not an available engine. Needs concrete navigation failures and evaluated model/permission/cost choices. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g168). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What Jev is (research 2026-09-29; the vendor's figures are claims, marked so).** TypeSafe AI's first 'System One' decision model, launched 2026-09-15; not a chat LLM and never prose: send unstructured state plus typed questions and get typed answers with probabilities in one parallel forward pass. Three primitives: **Choice** (one of N, a probability each), **Noul** (yes/no as the probability of yes), **Score** (a position on an ordered scale). Model id `typesafe/jev-1.13` (alias `~typesafe/jev-latest`; `typesafe/jev-router` is a router built on it). Served **only through OpenRouter**, billed to the OpenRouter key — no separate account: `POST /api/alpha/decisions` (or the SDK against `/api/v1/systemone`), responses carry `usage.cost`; 32K context. **Reported, not re-run:** about $0.042 per million input tokens, output free, 70–500 ms end to end. Weights closed, no disclosed size, so it cannot be self-hosted — which is why it cannot be the only option (portability is the point). **Claims versus verified:** TypeSafe claims 40–200x faster and up to ~400x cheaper than LLM workflows, and an independent critique says that holds only against the slowest, priciest baselines in TypeSafe's own eval; 'cannot hallucinate' means the output matches the schema, a wrong answer still type-checks; no calibration curves, rate limits unpublished, the training method (RLCD) unreviewed; one workflow was clearly weaker (61.8% against 74.7% for a comparison model on invoice processing); and splitting a decision into typed questions improved every model tested, so that gain is the decomposition, not Jev. **Same-name projects, not the model meant:** `AgentJev-0.6B` (a third-party Apache-2.0 imitation on Qwen3-0.6B, ~1.2 GB, PyTorch, ~50 ms a decision, ~300 ms over 64 candidates) and Featherless's 'Simple Jev' (next-token logits from any Hugging Face model as a typed classifier) — proof the pattern reproduces with open weights. **How it would work here (spec §10):** score candidate links from an already-fetched page (anchor text, path, heading) against a goal, and answer stop conditions ('does this page already hold the answer?', 'is this a wall or junk?'); precedent is Mind2Web's MindAct (a fine-tuned DeBERTa ranking elements, ~85–89% recall in the top 50, then a larger model choosing). Uses: G61's 'find the page that holds this fact' from a source's home page in one or two hops, and G102's recon across a site's `about` and `docs`. **Never a crawler; it sees only text fetched under the rail; a Noul 'is this a wall?' may retire a page and can never trigger a retry.** **Constraints:** one interface, `Navigator` (`rank(goal, candidates)`, `stop(goal, page)`), with four implementations — `heuristic` (default, zero cost, no key), `embedder` (the on-device EmbeddingGemma cosine, already shipped), `jev` (OpenRouter), `local-classifier` (a fine-tuned MiniLM or DeBERTa; open, CPU, nothing leaves); a Settings row 'Smarter navigation' under Reading the web, drawn Off · **Needs OpenRouter** and, until built, a switch-less 'Not available yet' (a deliberate exception to 'absent, not disabled' because the owner asked for the row); `LeavesMacNote` gains the sentence about where page text and the goal go; off by default, no gate treats it as implicit, a person's name is never sent (G159's rule). **Do first, and it is the whole gate (S8):** a small navigation set of real 'find the team page from the home page' tasks over public sites, and three baselines measured against each other — the heuristic, the on-device embedder and a MindAct-style MiniLM. Build Jev only if it beats the embedder by a margin worth an OpenRouter key. Cheapest baseline first, because that is the one most people can use without a key. → depends **G164**, **G122** (OpenRouter is already a card, PKCE sign-in), **G61**/**G102**; the spec's slice is S8. Triage: RESEARCH 💸. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🅿️ future — RESEARCH, benchmark first. | CONDITIONAL · Research |
| G173 | <a id="g173"></a>**Later findings from the first-run review — known, deliberately not fixed** (2026-09-29; one register, each item with its own trigger, so none is rediscovered) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Preserve the individual later triggers: large batches, orphan cache growth and demo/version edges. Recheck audit fixes before reopening; none is a blanket first-run blocker. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g173). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** (1) **Over-promotion on a big first batch.** A first drain over a large export promotes more entities than a steady state would, because 'two separate conversations' is easy to meet in bulk. *Expected, not a defect:* decay and the person's *keep* answers prune it (G147, G171's capped questions). Proposed trigger to revisit: a measured first drain that leaves the graph unreadable after a couple of decay weeks. (2) **Duplicates across Claude and ChatGPT variant names** (the same tool or person spelled two ways by two vendors). The dedup sweep exists but **still has no button in the app** (R-O17: the endpoint blocks the event loop and does not commit what it merges); Settings → Memory shows no 'Look for duplicates' until that is fixed. Trigger: the first real drain's duplicate count. (3) **A logo guess for pages without a URL.** A company page with no domain can show a wrong guessed logo; It is already among the parked live-pass findings in TODO; the fix belongs to **G159**'s RESEARCH (multi-word domain guessing), not here. (4) **Pending-name lines never expire.** `<bank>/pending_entities.jsonl` (G141 PJ-0b) holds claims for a name that has no page yet, at most 50 per name; a name that never earns a page keeps its line indefinitely. Needs an age-out rule (proposed: measured in Sleep weeks, like decay), which would be a new ruling. (5) **The 50-session trailer cap** (`MAX_SESSION_TRAILERS`, applied by the call site, not the builder): a drain batch that consolidates more than 50 conversations lists only the first 50 in `Cicada-Session:` trailers, so the commit's trailers under-report a big batch. Sleep v5's default batch of 25 stays under it; raise or paginate the trailer before offering larger batches (**G163**). → relates **G159**, **G141**, **G147**, **G163**, R-O17. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🅿️ parked — each with its trigger. | CONDITIONAL · Research |

## Closed choices, permanent redirects and principles

| ID | Goal | Reasoning and remaining implementation | Current status |
|---|---|---|---|
| G79 | <a id="g79"></a>**North star — Cicada is worth having only if it learns from lived experience AND curation, and gives it back at the right moment** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "the best version of cicada is one that smartly gives and learns from all my experience as a human + the things that i've curated and might be relevant to me") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** The north star is a scope filter, not an implementation task. Evaluate it through G10/G93 and curated-context trials; keep its reasoning, remove it from the build sequence. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g79). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Not a feature — the sentence every other row is scored against, recorded so it can arbitrate scope. **Read literally it names two intake streams and one output discipline.** *Experience* = what actually happened to the user: conversations (MCP capture, imports), decisions and their reversals, work done in repos, places, people, the passage of time itself (the decay layer is the only part of Cicada that models experience as something that *fades*). *Curation* = what the user deliberately chose to keep: saves with reasons (G71), bookmarks, papers, design references (G70), prompts (G73), skills (G72) — signal the user paid attention to select, which is categorically different from conversational exhaust and should probably carry different trust and different decay (already true structurally: media/bookmarks are `evergreen`, G66). *Smartly gives* = the output discipline, and the part Cicada is furthest from: **relevance at the right moment, unprompted, without noise.** Today Cicada answers when asked (`/ask`, `cicada_recall`) and interrupts on a schedule (inbox); it does not yet notice "you are about to do X and three months ago you curated the exact thing that helps." **The measurable claim hiding in the sentence** (worth stating as a thesis-grade goal): the system should surface a *curated* item inside an *experiential* context the user never explicitly linked — i.e. cross-stream retrieval, not within-stream search. That is a testable capability (does a Pinterest save from March ever legitimately reach a September conversation about a project?) and today the honest answer is "only if the user searches for it." **Use as a filter:** a row that only adds intake (more capture channels) advances half the sentence; a row that only adds surfaces advances none of it; rows that connect curated artifacts into live context (G70's design identity, G60's timed questions, G75's handshake priming, G53's state dictionary, G52's Ask) advance the whole of it — score accordingly. **Tension to keep honest:** "gives smartly" and "never intrusive" (UX principle 4) pull against each other; the resolution so far has been pull-not-push, and if that stays fixed, the ceiling on "smartly gives" is set by whatever the agent loads at conversation start — which makes **G75** (handshake) and **G53** (state dictionary) load-bearing for the north star, not niceties. → the scoring lens over the whole table; most directly served by **G53**, **G75**, **G52**, **G70**, **G66**, **G71**; the reason **G74** is the top blocker (nothing learns while Sleep is dead). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | DECISION · Principle |
| G99 | <a id="g99"></a>**A relational tier — DECLINED at this scale; the derived tier just has to become actually derived** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, following G96: "in what way can the addition of sqlite as relational db benefit cicada… and if we should add it to the task queue"; answered by a 4-lens measured sweep + an adversarial rebuttal, every headline number re-run against the live bank) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** A relational authoritative tier was declined by measurement. Derived vectors/FTS exist and audit fixes landed; revisit only the row's scale/concurrency triggers, not to implement a second fact database. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g99). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Verdict: do NOT build SQL over memory. Cicada does not need a relational tier at today's scale — it needs the SQLite file it already ships to stop being versioned, stale, and undetectably wrong.** **Three of the four flagship "SQL wins" collapsed under measurement:** (a) `GET /conversations/{id}` at 1,625 ms is not a lookup problem — `bank_index.IndexedFile.body()` re-reads and re-parses every page while the same object already caches frontmatter by `(mtime_ns, size)`; raw reads of all 1,731 entities cost 24 ms, `body()` 626 ms, `parse_claims()` 876 ms. It is PyYAML, not the filesystem. Memoizing parsed claims under the key `bank_index` already uses gives **1,528 ms → ~6 ms (~250×) in ~10 lines**, and cannot be stale by construction. (b) The "perspectival retrieval silently returns nothing" bug lives in a helper with **zero production callers that pass filters**. (c) `graph_edges.yaml` (696 KB, 6,741 edges) parses in 545 ms with `yaml.safe_load`, **88.7 ms with `CSafeLoader`, 1.8 ms as JSON** — a 6,741-row list needs no query engine; only the *atomicity* half survives, and the fix is already written in-repo (`logo_service.py:483-498`, tmp + `os.replace`). **Only two things genuinely survive.** (i) **Filtered KNN is impossible, not slow** — `vector_index.py:182` stores metadata as a JSON blob, so the `timestamp` the episode index already computes and the `type`/`status`/`confidence` the entity index already computes are unqueryable; no router exposes a date parameter and `cicada_recall` takes `{query}` alone, so *"what was I reading in March"* cannot be asked at any price. sqlite-vec (already imported) supports `vec0` metadata columns — ~30 lines, inside a file whose own docstring calls itself disposable. (ii) **Life-data aggregation is inexpressible** — 20,000 one-file-per-sample markdown records = 2.7 MB of content occupying **78.1 MB on disk** (block amplification) and 2.67 s to scan; the same rows in SQLite with an index = 1.6 MB and **1.58 ms**. But G94's own rule (aggregates, never samples) means the *graph* never holds a series — what has nowhere to live is the raw store you aggregate *from*. **URGENT sub-item, and it is a bug not architecture — G99a, do it BEFORE G74 restores Sleep:** `vector_index.db` (**35 MB) is tracked in the live bank's git** — verified: `git ls-files` lists it, the bank has **no `.gitignore`** (`scaffold_bank` writes none), and `commit_changes` runs `git add -A` (`git_service.py:841`). The bank's `.git` is **69 MB against 16 MB of markdown**; `duplicate_bank` copies it verbatim so 30 MB becomes commit #1 of every new bank; and because `_rebuild_table` drops and rebuilds, **a nightly Sleep would commit a fresh ~30 MB blob every night (~11 GB/yr) the moment the engine lands.** It is also **silently stale**: db mtime 2026-07-14 while **636 entity pages are newer**, `index_meta` stores only `{model, dim}`, and `/status`'s `_leann_present` is a bare existence check that reports it healthy. G99a = bank `.gitignore` for `vector_index.db`/`-wal`/`-shm`, `git rm --cached` on existing banks, exclude derived artifacts from `duplicate_bank`, add a `source_version` (bank HEAD + content hash) checked on open and surfaced by `/status`; plus `journal_mode=WAL` + `synchronous=NORMAL` (`_connect` sets **no PRAGMA at all**) and stop the three `except sqlite3.OperationalError: return []` handlers from turning a lock into "the agent has no memory" with no log line. **Ordered tail:** G99b memoize parsed claims (~10 lines; test: warm `/conversations/{id}` p50 < 20 ms); G99c `CSafeLoader` + port the atomic-write pattern to the five hand-rolled stores (`save_url_index`, `notes_sync`, `calendar_registry`, `sync_state`, `inbox_generator`); **G99d shipped (feat/saved-at, 2026-09-01)** — `RawItem.added` is now plumbed end to end: `api/services/saved_at.py` normalizes each of the five parsers' source-specific formats (Netscape epoch seconds, Chrome's WebKit microseconds, Takeout's ISO-8601, TikTok's `Date`, LinkedIn's drifting column) to an ISO date, persisted as a distinct top-level `saved_at:` on episode/entity frontmatter and as `content_saved_at` in `url_index.json`/`MediaSourceItem`/Swift's `MediaFeedItem`, with `GET /sources` and the Feed's Recent sort preferring it over the ingest timestamp (parsed to a real instant, not a raw-string compare, so same-day ties are deterministic). **0% of the 789 existing items had a recoverable save date** — checked `url_index.json`, episode bodies, `~/Library/Application Support/Cicada/upload_history.json`, and common export-file locations; the original `add_date`/`date_added` values were discarded at ingest time by the pre-fix code and never persisted anywhere to recover from. Historical items therefore carry no `saved_at` by design (never a guessed one) rather than a wrong one, and the fix is forward-looking only — it takes effect on the next fresh import, not retroactively on the live bank; G99e promote `vec0` metadata to real columns and expose `since`/`until`/`type` on `/search`, `/ask` and `cicada_recall` (~1 day, the one genuine SQLite win available now). **Explicitly NOT queued:** relational tables in `vector_index.db`, or migrating `url_index.json`/`sync_state.json`/`feeds.yaml`/`calendars.yaml`/`banks.yaml`/`graph_edges.yaml`/the in-page claims blocks — 3-to-789-row human-scale files whose last commits read "+9 insertions", where SQLite would trade `git diff`, `grep` and `git blame` (the substrate `/entities/{id}/history` and `/contributors` are *built on*) for query power nobody needs. **Concurrency strengthens the decline:** three processes touch a bank but there is exactly **one writer** today (Sleep); a full rebuild is a 0.71 s transaction and 40 concurrent read probes saw zero errors. The proposal would *create* the footgun it claims to solve — capture-time tables written from both MCP and API turn a single-writer batch file into three-process multi-writer under `journal_mode=delete`, and a Sleep-rebuilt claim table would be **75 days stale right now**, replacing a correct-by-construction 6 ms read with a 0.2 ms wrong one. **The invariant, applicable without re-reading any of this: markdown+git is the only source of truth; a SQLite table may exist only if deleting every `.db` costs CPU time and never a fact, and no derived artifact is ever tracked in a bank's git or copied by a bank operation.** **Triggers to revisit — named metrics, not vibes:** T1 after G99b, warm p50 still > 250 ms ⇒ revisit a derived claims table; T2 claims > ~25,000 (10× today) or entities > 10,000; **T3 (the only real forcing function) one merged G94 adapter retaining raw samples above ~50,000 rows/yr ⇒ build `~/.cicada/streams/<bank>.db` — outside every bank and outside git, on the logos/telemetry rail — whose only output into memory is an aggregated episode plus claims, which costs the thesis claim nothing**; T4 the day a second process writes the index, WAL stops being optional and the write path needs `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. **Previous execution label (historical):** **(Absorbs G96, merged 2026-09-01)** — Cicada already implements the "vector as entryway, not full retrieval" pattern Ed Honour's argument called for: `cicada_recall` runs a sqlite-vec search and returns entry points (`suggested_entities`, a `relevant_hub` with `hub_members_preview`, `next_tool: cicada_recall_detail`, `mcp/server.py:823-855`), after which the agent traverses wikilinks and hub pages (`_index.md` → `hubs/` → `entities/`); this row's four-lens measurement is what settled the one place Cicada deliberately disagrees with him (no relational store). **Decided 2026-09-01** — **G99a shipped** (`api/services/bank_registry.py` — bank `.gitignore` for `vector_index.db`/`-wal`/`-shm`, `git rm --cached` on existing banks, derived artifacts excluded from `duplicate_bank`); **G99d shipped**; G99b, G99c, G99e S/XS; streams DB gated on T3; general relational tier **declined**. | CONDITIONAL · Ruling |
| G101 | <a id="g101"></a>**Raw conversations as an evidence layer — decide what to keep, and prove Sleep is still needed** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "is sleep really needed anymore? is it because we process and save all the raw and real conversations? and could we even do this as an extra layer of context backup or provenance… is there a practical way to store all conversations smartly?") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Raw episodes and beliefs both exist. Removing Sleep was declined because text has no temporal belief object; no raw-store replacement implementation is queued. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g101). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The answer to the question, recorded because it is a founding-decision-level judgment: raw storage does NOT replace Sleep, it makes Sleep auditable.** Sleep is *not* needed for retrieval — semantic search over raw text is RAG and it works. It is needed for four things raw text structurally cannot do: (1) **identity resolution** — "Mongo" / "MongoDB" / "the database we switched to" is a judgment, not a lookup; (2) **contradiction and decay** — *text cannot decay*; only a belief can go stale, be superseded, or be contradicted, so "time as a signal" requires a belief object to attach time to, and a transcript is a permanent fact about what was said, never about what is still true; (3) **a stable address** for confidence, sources and history to accumulate across dozens of conversations; (4) **compression** — measured in the G76 audit: 2.4 MB of curated memory versus 238 MB of transcripts for a single project (~100-300×, mostly tool-output noise). **What to build.** Store the *human-readable conversation*, never the raw harness transcript — that ratio is the whole argument, and the G48 spec invariant already forbids transcript content in a bank. Whole conversations are already reconstructable by grouping episodes on `session_id`, so the open questions are narrower than they look: **what to retain** (turns yes; tool calls, tool output and file dumps no), **where it lives** (episodes are already in the bank; anything bulkier belongs outside it on the logos/telemetry rail), **dedup** (content-hash, same as `url_index`), and **whether an unconsolidated conversation is browsable** before Sleep touches it. **The payoff is G100:** once the raw side is stored deliberately rather than incidentally, a citation can point into it, and every belief becomes traceable to the sentence that produced it — including for a future agent auditing a contributor's work, which is the user's stated motivation. → answers the "is Sleep needed" question for the thesis; depends on **G48**, feeds **G100**, bounded by **G76**'s privacy measurement and **G94/G95**'s aggregate-not-raw rail. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | DECISION · Ruling |

| G100 | <a id="g100"></a>**Span citation — record WHICH SENTENCE convinced the contributor, and render it** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "maybe this can be the contributor who decides the part that influenced it to make the addition/substraction/modification… have a view that shows the raw conversation text and only the relevant portion highlighted… reference the conversation as notebooklm references the files") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Sentence/span citations moved to G118; provenance extensions remain there. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g100). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap, stated precisely: Cicada's provenance stops at DOCUMENT granularity.** `source_episodes` says *which* episode, `git blame` + the commit trailers say *which model, which session, which trigger* — but nothing records **which sentence**. So "why does Cicada believe this?" is answerable only as "somewhere in this wall of text". Verified: `grep -ci 'span citation\|char offset'` over this backlog → **0**; `cicada_write_claim` takes `sources: [str]` (a *where to look it up* ref, G61) and has **no quote or span parameter**. **Design.** (i) **The contributor cites at write time** — the agent knows what convinced it; asking later is reconstruction. Add an optional `quote` (and/or `[start,end]` offsets into the named episode) to `cicada_write_claim` and the `agentic_write` path. (ii) **Store both offset and quote**: offsets are compact and render in O(1); the quote is self-verifying, so if the offset no longer matches, fall back to searching for the quote — a repair path, not a failure. (iii) **Derived spans are a SEPARATE CLASS from asserted ones.** For the ~2,300 claims that predate this, candidate spans can be *found* by fuzzy/regex matching an entity's name and aliases against its `source_episodes` — useful, and it covers the user's "not an exact mention but like a quote" case — but it must render visibly differently. One is evidence the writer stood behind; the other is a guess the reader must check. Never merge the two. (iv) **The viewer is the existing DiffView pattern pointed at conversation text**: raw episode body, the cited span highlighted, and **prev/next across every conversation that contributed to this belief** — the NotebookLM behaviour the user described. G48 already makes "which conversations" reconstructable (`session_id` on episodes + `entity_sources.py`), so this layer only adds *where inside each one*. **The dependency to verify before building: episodes must be immutable.** Capture is append-only and no rewrite path was found in a first pass, but `mark_processed` flips frontmatter on the same file — confirm it rewrites frontmatter only and never reflows the body, or offsets silently rot. If immutability cannot be guaranteed, store the quote as primary and treat offsets as a cache. **Why this is worth more than it looks:** it is the difference between a memory system that *claims* provenance and one that can be audited a sentence at a time — and it is the natural home for the "which part of the conversation influenced this" question that no other personal-memory system answers. → extends **G48** (session identity), **G61** (`sources:` — distinct: G61 is *where to re-check a fact*, this is *what originally said it*), **G67** (DiffView, the renderer to reuse); prerequisite-ish for **G101**; strengthens the thesis's provenance claim. **Absorbed into G118 (2026-09-03):** design point (i) — the contributor cites at write time — shipped as G118 slice 1 (`evidence: [{episode, quote}]` on `cicada_write_claim`, `evidence_quote` in Stage 1); (ii) is settled the other way (offsets + hash only, never the quote — the bank holds the text, `stale` replaces the repair path); the immutability dependency is verified for the body (`markdown_parser.parse` strips it identically on every rewrite; `mark_processed` never reflows it). (iii) derived spans and (iv) the viewer stay open under G118 slices 2+. **Previous execution label (historical):** ⤴ absorbed into G118 — (i)/(ii) shipped in slice 1 (PR #44); (iii)/(iv) open. | REDIRECT · Closed |
| G157 | <a id="g157"></a>**A Cicada CLI** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "Working on a CLI might be good to so also write this in the backlog.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Moved to G180; no second CLI task. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g157). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Same CLI request as G180. Keep this ID as a permanent redirect; all new implementation/measurement details go to G180. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The gap.** Every surface today is the app, MCP, or `curl` against `localhost:8000` with a bearer token read from `~/.cicada/api_token`; `install.sh` and `scripts/` are maintainer tools, not a user interface. A CLI gives a person on a server or over SSH (**G132**) and an agent without MCP a way in, and makes the round-4 copy-paste agent setup (D5, `GET /agents/setup`) something a terminal can run. **Shape to decide:** a thin client over the existing HTTP API — `cicada recall`, `ask`, `inbox` (answer an item), `save <url>`, `sleep` (start a cycle, which stays a user trigger under ruling 4), `status`, `banks`, `export`, `setup <harness>` — reading the same token, never a second code path to the bank. **Constraints:** it never touches bank files directly (the filesystem is the source of truth *through* the one git writer per bank, F2-back R-B1); its writes carry `Cicada-Author: user` or the harness label exactly as the API assigns; a query argument is never logged (G136 R22) and shell history is the person's concern, stated in `--help`; portable — no author-machine path, installable with the backend. Contrast with the Agents page's "no CLI tab" (round-4 decision 4): that is about which agent harnesses onboarding lists, not this. → relates **G76** (install), **G132** (cross-device), **G135** (remote connector). **Previous execution label (historical):** REDIRECT → G180. | REDIRECT · Closed |
